

===== do-americans-need-a-visa-for-bali [visas-and-documents / entry-stack] 1111w =====
Do Americans Need a Visa for Bali?
Yes. A US passport holder needs a visa to enter Bali, and for a vacation it is an easy one to get: you buy it online before you fly or at the airport when you land. What catches Americans out is rarely the visa itself. The trouble comes from the passport rules and from two other things you have to do that are not the visa at all.


## The short answer for US passport holders

Americans visiting Bali as tourists need a visa, and the one that fits a normal vacation is Indonesia's B1 tourist visa, widely called the visa on arrival. The State Department puts it plainly: a visa is required. A US traveler can buy it online in advance or at the airport on arrival, and both routes produce the same visa.

Bali is a province of Indonesia, so everything here is Indonesian immigration law. There is no separate "Bali visa": the visa you need is the national Indonesian one.



## What the visa lets you do

The B1 visa covers tourism, visiting friends or family, business meetings and medical treatment. It does not cover work: selling goods or services, or being paid by anyone in Indonesia, is prohibited. Paid work is not allowed on this visa, and the US Consular Agency reports a current enforcement campaign in Bali that includes visa violations.


## What the Bali visa costs and how long it lasts

The Indonesian tourist visa for Americans costs IDR 500,000, set in rupiah, so the dollar amount on your card statement moves with the exchange rate on the day you pay. The visa gives a stay of 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days.


## Thirty days is counted, not rounded

Thirty days is not "a month", and the penalty for getting it wrong is steep: overstaying can bring a fine of IDR 1,000,000 per day, along with possible detention, deportation or a ban on coming back. If your trip runs close to the limit, count the days on a calendar and build in a margin.


## Staying longer than 30 days

If you want more time, you extend once, for another 30 days. The official fee for that extension is IDR 500,000 per application. Start the process well before your first 30 days run out. An expired visa is an overstay, whatever your intentions were.


## Buying the visa online or at the airport

American travelers can buy the Bali visa on Indonesia's official e-visa website before departure, or pay for it at the visa counter in the airport before reaching immigration. The State Department notes that the airport route can mean long lines. Buying online moves that step to your kitchen table, which matters most after twenty-plus hours of travel.


## The online route

The official site is run by Indonesia's Directorate General of Immigration. You pay by Visa, Mastercard or JCB card, and one application can cover up to five travelers, which helps families. Once issued, the visa is good for entry for 90 days, so there is no reason to leave it to the last week. You will be asked for your departure date and a booked return flight.

Use the official address only. Search results for this visa also show agencies that charge a service fee for filling in the same form.


## Passport rules that turn Americans away

A US passport must be valid for at least 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages to enter Bali. The US Consular Agency in Bali is blunt about it: immigration will not let you in otherwise. A visa does not fix a short passport.


## The blank-page detail people miss

The endorsement pages at the back of a US passport do not count as blank pages. If your passport is nearly full, count the visa pages only. Check this before you book anything, while there is still time to renew.


## Emergency passports

The visa on arrival is not available to a traveler on a limited-validity emergency passport. In that case you need a visa obtained before arrival from an Indonesian embassy or consulate. This mostly affects people who lose a passport shortly before the trip and get a quick replacement.


## What immigration asks to see

Beyond the passport, the officer can ask for an outbound ticket from Indonesia. Have the booking on your phone and, ideally, on paper. You must also leave Indonesia on the same passport you entered with, which matters to dual nationals who carry two.


## Two things that are not the visa

Getting into Bali takes three separate steps, and the visa is only the first. Indonesia also requires an arrival card from every traveler, and Bali charges its own tourist levy. Each is handled through its own official system, and paying for one does not cover the others.


## The arrival card

Every traveler completes an arrival card through Indonesia's All Indonesia system. It is free, it produces a QR code that you show to immigration and customs, and the form only opens shortly before you travel: fill it in during the last two days before you land.


## The Bali tourist levy

Bali charges foreign visitors a levy of IDR 150,000 per person, separate from the visa and paid cashless. It has its own rules and its own website, covered in our guide to the Bali tourist levy.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The visa question looked like a single form, and the answer turned out to be a stack: a visa, a passport test, an arrival card and a levy, run by different offices with different deadlines. Each one is easy. What goes wrong is the sequence: some steps cannot be done early, and one has to be settled months ahead, when you renew a passport.

The same stack also fixes the outer edge of your trip: how many days you can stay, and what proof of leaving you must already hold when you arrive. That ordering, from passport check to landing, is laid out step by step in the complete Bali trip guide, next to the other connected decisions a first trip turns on.



## FAQ

Can Americans get a visa on arrival in Bali?Yes. US passport holders can buy the B1 tourist visa at the airport on arrival or online in advance. Both give the same 30-day stay.

Is Bali visa-free for US citizens?No. The State Department says a visa is required for US citizens visiting Indonesia, which includes Bali.

How long can a US citizen stay in Bali?Thirty days on the tourist visa, extendable once for another 30 days.

Do I need a return ticket to enter Bali?Immigration can ask for an outbound ticket from Indonesia, and the online visa application asks for a booked return flight.



===== bali-tourist-levy-explained [visas-and-documents / entry-stack] 1082w =====
The Bali Tourist Levy, Explained
Bali charges foreign tourists a one-time levy, separate from the visa, and you can pay it online before you leave home. The amount is small. What matters is knowing that it exists, paying it on the real website, and keeping the proof, because a tourist who has not paid can be refused service at the island's attractions.


## What the Bali tourist levy is

The Bali tourist levy is a fee the provincial government charges international visitors to fund the protection of Balinese culture and the natural environment. The Bali tourist levy costs IDR 150,000 per person and is set in rupiah, so its dollar value shifts with the exchange rate on the day you pay.

The levy is a Bali rule, not a national one. It rests on provincial regulations and applies to international tourists who travel to Bali, apart from a short list of exemptions, whichever airport or port that tourist used to enter Indonesia. Flying into Jakarta first does not change it.



## How it differs from the visa

The visa is issued by Indonesia's national immigration service and lets you into the country. The levy is collected by Bali's provincial government and has nothing to do with your right to enter. They are paid on different websites, to different authorities, and paying one does not cover the other. Many first-time visitors pay for the visa and assume they are done.


## Who has to pay the Bali tourist levy

International tourists who travel to Bali for tourism pay the levy, with a short list of exemptions, and the official guidance says plainly that cruise passengers who come ashore are included. The levy is paid once per trip, not once per day or per hotel, so a three-week stay costs the same as a long weekend.


## Who is exempt

The exemptions are narrow and none of them describes an ordinary vacationer. The official list covers holders of diplomatic and official visas, transport crew, holders of Indonesian stay permits, and holders of family unification, student and golden visas. If you are flying in on a tourist visa, you pay.


## Leaving and coming back

The official wording is that the levy is paid one time while traveling in Bali, before the person leaves Indonesia. Read plainly, a side trip from Bali to Lombok or the Gili Islands and back sits inside the same trip, because you have not left the country. A separate vacation next year is a new trip and a new payment.


## How to pay the Bali tourist levy

The Bali tourist levy is paid cashless through the province's Love Bali system, and the simplest time to do it is online before you fly. Travelers can also pay at counters at Bali's airport and seaport on arrival, or through hotels, travel agents and attractions that are registered in the system. Cash is not accepted anywhere.


## Paying online before you go

On the Love Bali website you enter your passport details and arrival date, then pay. Accepted methods include Visa, Mastercard, American Express and JCB cards. For an American, a credit card is the practical choice; the site also lists bank transfer and Indonesia's QR payment standard, and the United States is not among the countries connected to that QR scheme.

Use a card that does not charge a foreign transaction fee if you have one. The charge is processed in rupiah, and a card with a three percent foreign fee adds a little to every such payment on the trip.


## Your proof of payment

After paying, you receive a levy voucher with a QR code by email. Save it somewhere you can reach without signal: a screenshot in your photos works. The levy is charged per person, so check that you have a voucher for each traveler in your group before you close the browser.


## Paying when you land

If you forget, there are payment counters at the airport. This works, but it adds one more line at the end of a very long travel day, right after immigration and baggage claim. Paying from home removes the step.


## What happens if you do not pay

A foreign tourist who has not paid the Bali levy can be refused service at tourist attractions under the Bali governor's 2025 circular on visitor conduct. In practice that means the voucher is something you may be asked to show at a temple or a ticket booth, not only at the airport.


## Checks happen away from the airport

Because the stated consequence is being refused service at tourist attractions, the realistic place to be asked is at the entrance to somewhere you have driven an hour to see. Having the QR code on your phone turns that into a ten-second check.

Nothing official says how often the check happens, and that is a poor reason to skip the payment. The levy is small, the rule is clear, and the same circular that sets the penalty also tells residents how to report tourists who break the rules.


## Avoiding fake levy websites

The only official place to pay the Bali tourist levy online is the provincial government's Love Bali site, at lovebali.baliprov.go.id. Search results for the levy also show third-party sites that take the payment on your behalf and add their own fee. The official address ends in baliprov.go.id, the domain of the Bali provincial government.


## How to tell the real one

Check the address bar before you type a passport number. The official site charges the official amount. If a site quotes a higher total than the figure above, you are either on a third party's page or paying a third party's fee.

The same caution applies to the visa and the arrival card, which have their own official sites and their own imitators. Three small government payments, each with a separate website, is the kind of chore that is easy to get slightly wrong, and it is set out in order in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How much is the Bali tourist tax?The Bali tourist levy is IDR 150,000 per person, paid once per trip. It is charged in rupiah, so the dollar amount depends on the exchange rate when you pay.

Do children pay the Bali tourist levy?The official exemption list is organized by visa type, and we found no exemption for children on it.

Can I pay the Bali levy in cash at the airport?No. Payment is cashless only, online in advance or by card at the airport and seaport counters.

Is the Bali levy the same as the visa?No. The visa is a national immigration document. The levy is a separate provincial charge paid on a different website.



===== bringing-medication-into-bali [visas-and-documents / health-chain] 1106w =====
Bringing Medication into Bali and Indonesia
Some medication can come to Bali with you and some cannot, and which is which depends on the class of drug, not on whether you hold a valid US prescription. Indonesia bans one whole class of medicine outright, limits another, and allows ordinary prescriptions with paperwork. Check which class yours falls in before you pack anything.


## The rule in one paragraph

Medication for personal use can enter Indonesia in a traveler's own baggage without advance approval from the health authorities, subject to conditions: a doctor's prescription, a declaration form, and limits that depend on the class of drug. Indonesia's food and drug agency allows a supply of up to 90 days' use for prescribed medicine, with a shorter limit for one class and an outright ban on another.

The agency responsible is BPOM, Indonesia's equivalent of the FDA. Its guidance is written by class of drug, not by brand name, and that is the detail that matters most for Americans, as you will see below.



## What "personal use" means here

The allowance covers medicine you carry with you, in your own bags, for your own treatment. The rule described here is for medicine carried in a passenger's baggage, not for anything mailed or couriered. Whatever you need for the trip should travel with you from the first day.


## What counts as acceptable paperwork

Prescription medicine brought into Indonesia from abroad must be supported by a doctor's prescription or a hospital recommendation, according to the food and drug agency; over-the-counter products have a small separate allowance, covered below. The agency also describes a special form with supporting documents for personal-use medicine, so the prescription is the document to have ready.


## What to carry

The documented requirements are the prescription and the agency's form. Beyond that, our advice is practical, not official: keep each medicine in its original pharmacy packaging with your name on the label, carry a copy of the prescription for each item, and ask your doctor for a short letter listing each drug by its generic name, the dose and the condition treated. US brand names are often different abroad.

Keep everything in your carry-on, together, where you can hand it over in one motion. Loose pills in a weekly organizer are the worst case: nothing ties them to you or to a prescription.


## Over-the-counter medicine

Ordinary non-prescription medicine has its own small allowance. BPOM permits over-the-counter products without a prescription for at most three days' need. For a longer trip, that means a large stock of non-prescription products is outside the allowance.


## The three classes and their limits

Indonesia sorts medicine into classes with different limits. Ordinary prescription medicine is allowed for up to 90 days of use. Psychotropic medicine is allowed for up to 60 days of need. Narcotic-class medicine and precursors may not be brought in from abroad at all, with or without a prescription.


## Why this is the part to check first

The ban on the narcotic class has no exception for a valid American prescription. A drug that your doctor prescribes and your pharmacy fills without comment can still be one Indonesia will not admit. The State Department says the same thing from the other side: some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia.


## No official list exists to look your drug up

Here is the uncomfortable part. Indonesian customs does not publish a list of prohibited medicines, and the food and drug agency's guidance names classes, not products. We are not going to tell you that a particular US prescription is allowed or banned, because no official source we could open says so drug by drug.

What you can do is find out which class your own medication falls into. BPOM publishes a contact for individual questions about bringing medicine in for personal use, and your prescribing doctor can tell you whether a drug is a controlled substance and what the non-controlled alternatives are. Do this well before you travel.


## What happens at customs

Medicines are one of the items that send an arriving passenger to the red channel at Indonesian customs, where baggage can be physically inspected. Declaring your medication is the expected behavior, not an admission of anything. Medicine that does not meet the rules can be seized and destroyed.


## Declare it

The arrival card you complete before landing includes the customs declaration. Answer the medicine question honestly, then go where the officers direct you with your prescriptions in hand. Be ready to say what each medicine is and to show the prescription for it.


## If your medicine is refused

The agency's wording is that non-compliant medicine can be seized and destroyed. That is why the realistic backup plan has to exist before you fly: know the generic name of what you take, ask your doctor what you would switch to if it were unavailable, and do not count on buying a replacement for a banned drug once you are there.


## Why the stakes are higher than a lost bottle

Indonesia's drug laws are strict, and the State Department's warning covers prescriptions too: some medications that are legal with a US prescription are illegal in Indonesia. The State Department warns that convictions can bring heavy fines and long jail sentences. Marijuana, cannabis and CBD products are illegal in Indonesia, including products that are legal in your home state.


## CBD and medical marijuana

This catches Americans more than any pill does. A CBD gummy for sleep or a cannabis vape cartridge is illegal there, and a prescription or medical card from a US state does not change what the State Department says about Indonesian law. Leave all of it at home, and check jacket pockets and toiletry bags before you pack.

Medication is one strand of a larger question about staying well on the island, which also runs through what your insurance covers and where you would be treated. How those pieces fit together is mapped in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Can I bring prescription medication to Bali?Yes, for personal use, in your own baggage, with a doctor's prescription, for up to 90 days of use. Narcotic-class medicines are the exception and may not be brought in at all.

Is Adderall legal in Bali?The State Department says some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia. Speak with your doctor about alternatives before you travel.

Do I need a doctor's letter for medication in Bali?Indonesia's food and drug agency requires a doctor's prescription or hospital recommendation for medicine brought from abroad. A letter listing generic names and doses makes the customs check faster.

Can I bring CBD to Bali?No. Marijuana, cannabis and CBD products are illegal in Indonesia.



===== bali-customs-alcohol-and-vape-rules [visas-and-documents / None] 1144w =====
What You Can Bring into Bali: Customs, Alcohol and Vapes
Indonesian customs sets fixed allowances for alcohol, tobacco and vapes, and the rule for going over them surprises people: the excess is destroyed, not taxed and returned to you. There are also rules for the value of what you carry, for cash and for medicines. Knowing the numbers before you shop at duty-free saves both money and an awkward moment at the airport.


## The allowances at a glance

Indonesian customs sets a per-person allowance for goods, alcohol, tobacco and e-cigarette products. An adult arriving in Bali may bring 1 liter of alcohol, a fixed quantity of tobacco or vape products, and personal goods up to a set value free of duty. The allowances are set per traveler, so each adult should carry their own.


- Personal goods: USD 500 per person per arrival, duty-free

- Alcohol: 1 liter per adult aged 21 or over

- Tobacco: 200 cigarettes, or 25 cigars, or 100 grams of tobacco

- Vapes: 30 ml of open-system liquid, or 12 ml of closed-system liquid, for travelers 18 or over




## Alcohol: one liter, and a different age line

An adult traveler may bring one liter of alcoholic drink into Indonesia free of excise, and the allowance applies only to passengers aged 21 and over. That is a single standard bottle of spirits or a little more than one bottle of wine per adult. A second duty-free bottle is over the limit.


## What happens to the extra bottle

Customs does not charge you duty on the excess and let you keep it. Alcohol, tobacco or vape products above the allowance are destroyed on the spot by the customs officer. The two-for-one offer at the departure airport is a bad deal for a solo traveler flying to Bali.


## Couples and groups

The allowance is per adult, so two adults traveling together can bring two liters between them. It is sensible for each person to carry their own bottle through customs. Travelers under 21 have no alcohol allowance, so a 19-year-old cannot carry a bottle for the family.


## Cigarettes and tobacco

The tobacco allowance for an adult arriving in Indonesia is 200 cigarettes, or 25 cigars, or 100 grams of sliced or other tobacco. Those are alternatives, not a combined total. A traveler who brings more than one type gets a proportional share of each, so a full carton plus a box of cigars is over the limit.


## The one-carton rule of thumb

Two hundred cigarettes is one standard carton. If you smoke, one carton per adult is the number to remember, and as with alcohol, the excess is destroyed, not taxed.


## Vapes and e-cigarettes

E-cigarette products have their own customs allowance in Indonesia, available to passengers aged 18 and over. A traveler may bring 30 ml of liquid for open, refillable systems, or 12 ml for closed pod or cartridge systems, or 140 sticks or 40 capsules of solid heated-tobacco product.


## Thirty milliliters is less than it sounds

A single common bottle of e-liquid in the United States holds 30 ml or more, and many hold 60 ml or 100 ml. One large bottle can put you over the allowance by itself. Closed pods are measured the same way: add up the milliliters printed on the packs before you fly.


## THC cartridges are a different matter entirely

Nothing in the vape allowance covers cannabis. Marijuana, cannabis and CBD products are illegal in Indonesia, and the State Department warns that drug convictions there can bring heavy fines and long jail sentences. A THC cartridge left in a jacket pocket is a criminal problem, not a customs one. Check every bag and pocket before you leave home.


## The USD 500 goods allowance

Personal goods worth up to USD 500 per person, per arrival, enter Indonesia free of import duty and tax. Above that value, customs charges 10% import duty plus value-added tax on the excess. The figure is set in US dollars in the regulation itself, so this is one Indonesian number that does not move with the exchange rate.


## Your own laptop and camera

The allowance is aimed at goods you are importing, such as new purchases and gifts, and the customs rules treat goods for personal use differently from goods that are not. The red-channel list includes goods that are not for personal use, so several identical new items in sealed boxes are the kind of thing that invites questions in a way your own used laptop and camera do not.


## Gifts for people in Bali

If you are carrying gifts, keep receipts handy and keep the total under the allowance, which is counted per person and per arrival.


## Cash, and the declaration threshold

Cash is not capped at Indonesian customs, but it must be declared above a threshold. Money or other payment instruments worth IDR 100,000,000 or more, in rupiah or the foreign-currency equivalent, must be declared on entry and exit. Failing to declare carries a penalty of 10% of the amount, up to a maximum of IDR 300,000,000.

That threshold is far more cash than a vacation calls for, so for most travelers the rule is simply something to know exists. It is a declaration duty, not a limit: declared money is allowed in.


## The customs form and the two channels

Arriving passengers declare their goods electronically before landing, as part of Indonesia's arrival card, and then walk through either a green channel or a red one. The red channel means physical inspection. Customs lists the things that send a passenger there, and several are easy to be carrying without thinking about it.


## What sends you to the red channel

The official list includes goods above the duty-free value, animal and plant products, medicines, cash at or above the declaration threshold, goods not for personal use, and a few categories no tourist should be near, such as weapons. Being sent to the red channel is not an accusation. It is where declared items are looked at.


## Fill the form in honestly

The declaration is part of the arrival card that every traveler completes in the last two days before landing. Answer the questions as they apply to you. Declaring what you carry is the expected behavior, and it keeps the inspection short.



## FAQ

How much alcohol can I bring into Bali?One liter per adult aged 21 or over. Anything above the allowance is destroyed by customs, not taxed.

Can I bring a vape to Bali?Yes, within the allowance for travelers 18 or over: 30 ml of open-system liquid or 12 ml of closed-system liquid. Cannabis and CBD products are illegal.

How many cigarettes can I take to Bali?Two hundred cigarettes, or 25 cigars, or 100 grams of tobacco per adult.

Do I have to declare cash when entering Bali?Only at or above IDR 100,000,000 or the foreign-currency equivalent. Below that, no declaration is needed.



===== renting-a-scooter-in-bali [scooters / scooter-liability] 1093w =====
Renting a Scooter in Bali: License, Insurance and Police Stops
Renting a scooter in Bali is easy, and that ease hides the real question. Being handed the keys says nothing about whether you are legally allowed to ride, and the gap between "they rented it to me" and "I was licensed to ride it" decides what a police stop or a crash will cost you.


## What an American legally needs to ride a scooter in Bali

An American riding a scooter in Bali needs an International Driving Permit carried together with a US license, and Indonesian law requires that the license match the type of vehicle. The State Department says a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia, and a rental agreement does not change that.


## The permit

The International Driving Permit is a booklet that translates your license; it is issued in the United States before you leave, and AAA, which issues it, lists Indonesia among the countries that honor it. Arrange it before you leave home. The permit has no force on its own, so the plastic license from your state travels with it.


## The part most visitors miss: the license class

Indonesia's traffic law requires every driver to hold a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and it treats motorcycles as their own license class, separate from cars. A scooter is a motorcycle under that law. As we read the statute, a US license that covers cars only does not match a scooter, and a permit translates your license; it does not add a class.

No official page we could find says a car-only license is enough for a scooter in Bali, and the statute points the other way. Our advice, which is an interpretation and not an official ruling: if your US license has no motorcycle endorsement, treat yourself as unlicensed on a scooter.



## What the rental shop will and will not check

A rental agreement is not a license check. Do not read being handed a scooter as evidence that you may legally ride it. Your license status, your insurance and your riding experience are yours to sort out before you reach the counter.


## What to do before you ride away

Photograph the scooter from every side, including existing scratches. Ask for the vehicle registration, since drivers must carry it. Test both brakes and the lights, and get a helmet that fits and fastens for you and any passenger.


## Never leave your passport as a deposit

If a shop asks to hold your passport, decline and offer a cash deposit or a photocopy instead. Your passport is the one document you cannot replace quickly, and handing it over weakens your position in any dispute about damage.


## Police stops and what the law says about fines

Police checks on foreign riders in Bali are part of a current enforcement campaign that the US Consular Agency describes as focused on traffic and vehicle violations, among other offenses. Indonesia's traffic law sets a maximum of 4 months' detention or a fine of IDR 1,000,000 for riding without the required license, with lower maximums for not carrying one and for riding without a helmet.


## What officers look for

The rules themselves are clear: a helmet for rider and passenger, a license and permit, the registration, and sober riding. Bali's 2025 rules for foreign visitors repeat that list and add that riders must not carry more passengers than the vehicle allows.


## If you are fined

The State Department's advice is to ask for an official receipt and follow the local procedure; it notes that travelers occasionally report requests for unofficial payments. The amounts above are legal maximums, not a roadside price list.


## If you crash

A crash is the risk that matters most on a scooter in Bali, and the legal position of a foreign rider is weaker than most expect. The State Department's guidance is that drivers involved in a traffic accident should stay at the scene and notify the police.


## Who gets blamed

The State Department notes that in collisions between cars and motorcycles, the driver of the larger vehicle is often presumed responsible. That presumption is no shield for a rider who was not licensed to be on the scooter in the first place.


## The question to put to your insurer before you go

Ask your travel insurer, in writing, one specific question: "Am I covered for injuries while riding a rented scooter in Indonesia if my US license has no motorcycle endorsement?" Policies differ, and only your own insurer can tell you where you stand. Get the reply by email and keep it.


## Whether you should rent one at all

A scooter makes sense in Bali for a rider who already holds a motorcycle license at home, has ridden in traffic, and is staying somewhere the distances are short. For a first-time rider, Bali's roads are a poor place to learn; ride-hailing apps and cars with drivers are the alternatives.


## If you ride

Wear a helmet with the strap fastened and closed shoes. Do not ride after drinking; Bali's own rules for visitors prohibit it. Avoid riding at night on roads you do not know.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The question started as "can I rent a scooter" and the answer was never about the scooter. It was a chain: the license you hold at home decides whether you are legal, being legal decides what a police stop costs, and both bear on what your insurer will say after a crash. Each link was fixed before you left the United States.

The same chain reaches into where you stay, since a base that only works with a scooter is a different base for someone who should not be on one. How transport, licensing, insurance and the choice of base lock together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Can I ride a scooter in Bali with a US driver's license?Not on a US license alone. The State Department says foreign drivers must carry an International Driving Permit, and Indonesian law requires a license matching the vehicle type, with motorcycles as their own class.

Do I need a motorcycle license to rent a scooter in Bali?Shops will rent without one, but Indonesia's traffic law treats motorcycles as a separate license class. Without a motorcycle endorsement, assume you are not licensed for a scooter.

Is a helmet required on a scooter in Bali?Yes. Helmets are required by Indonesian law for riders and passengers.

Where do I get an International Driving Permit?In the United States, before you travel. AAA issues it and lists Indonesia as a country that honors it.



===== is-bali-safe [health-and-safety / health-chain] 1100w =====
Is Bali Safe for American Travelers?
The US government's advisory for Indonesia, which includes Bali, asks travelers to exercise increased caution; it does not advise against going. The risks that matter there are specific, and several of them turn on choices you make: the road, what you drink, the island's animals and mosquitoes, and Indonesian law. This page goes through each using official sources.


## What the US government says about Bali

The US State Department's travel advisory for Indonesia, which covers Bali, is Level 2, Exercise increased caution, on a four-level scale. The advisory cites natural disasters, unrest and terrorism as the reasons. Its strongest warning, Level 4, applies to two provinces in Papua, far from Bali.


## How to read a Level 2

Level 2 is not a warning against going. It asks travelers to stay aware and to read the country guidance. The advisory is issued for Indonesia as a whole, a country that stretches across three time zones, so parts of its reasoning concern places a Bali visitor will never be near. Read the current text before you travel, since advisories are revised.



## Natural hazards

The advisory notes that Indonesia regularly experiences earthquakes, tsunamis and volcanic eruptions, and that these can disrupt transport and services. For a visitor, the practical step is small: know where the high ground is if you are staying on the coast, and keep your airline's app on your phone so you learn of disruptions directly.


## The road is the risk to plan around

Traffic is the hazard a visitor to Bali meets every day, and scooters are where it concentrates. The State Department describes roads shared by cars, trucks and large numbers of motorcycles that weave between vehicles and pass on either side. Riding one yourself adds a legal problem, because a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia.


## If you do not ride

Riding is a choice, not a requirement. Ride-hailing apps operate in Indonesia and licensed taxis are available, and a car with a driver can be hired by the day. Each of those puts someone else behind the wheel.


## If you do ride

Indonesian law requires a helmet and a license matching the vehicle, and Bali's own rules for visitors prohibit riding under the influence of alcohol. The licensing question, and what it means for insurance, has its own page on renting a scooter in Bali.


## Drinks, and what is in them

Drink safety is the Bali risk the State Department describes most bluntly. Its guidance warns of drink poisoning, including alcohol mixed with methanol, and reports drink spiking and sexual assault in Bali, Lombok and the Gili Islands. The official advice is to watch drinks being prepared, never accept drinks from strangers, and never leave a drink unattended.


## What that means on a night out

Our advice, which goes beyond the official wording: order drinks whose seal you can see broken, such as bottled beer, be cautious with unusually cheap spirits and mixed drinks, and go out with people you trust to notice if something is wrong. If someone becomes ill in a way that seems out of proportion to what they drank, treat it as a medical emergency.


## Animals and mosquitoes

Health risks in Bali come more from animals and insects than from anything exotic. The CDC says dogs infected with rabies are commonly found in Indonesia, and it currently lists a Level 2 travel health notice for Zika in Bali. On the reassuring side, the CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands.


## Dogs and monkeys

Do not pet street dogs, and keep your distance from monkeys at temples and forests, however tame they look. Any bite or scratch that breaks the skin needs washing and prompt medical attention; the CDC's advice after a bite is to seek care immediately.


## Mosquitoes

The CDC notes that dengue is a risk throughout Indonesia, and its Zika notice advises all travelers to Bali to prevent mosquito bites. Use repellent in the daytime as well as the evening. Anyone who is pregnant or planning a pregnancy should read the Zika notice and speak with a doctor before booking.


## Indonesian law is strict where Americans least expect it

Indonesian drug law applies to visitors in full, and it reaches products that are legal at home. Marijuana, cannabis and CBD products are illegal in Indonesia, and the State Department warns that some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia. Convictions can bring heavy fines and long jail sentences.


## An enforcement campaign is under way

The US Consular Agency in Bali reports a current enforcement campaign by police, immigration, transport and tourism officials, with stepped-up attention to traffic violations, visa violations and drug possession by foreigners. Bali also has a formal list of rules for visitors, covered on its own page.


## Who to call in Bali

Emergency services in Bali are reached on short national numbers: 112 for the unified emergency line, 110 for police, 119 for an ambulance and 113 for fire. The 112 line is free and works without phone credit. Save the numbers before you land, since you will not want to search for them when you need them.


## The US Consular Agency

The United States has a Consular Agency in Bali, in Jimbaran, reachable at +62-361-958-6161. For a medical or safety emergency, call the local emergency numbers first; the agency is the contact for a lost passport, an arrest or other consular help.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. "Is Bali safe" turned out not to be one question. The answers pointed at things you decide before you leave: whether you ride, what medication is in your bag, which vaccines you have, what your insurance covers and where the nearest hospital is from your hotel. Each choice is small, and each one changes what a bad day would cost.

How those choices connect, from your medicine cabinet to your insurance policy to your choice of base, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is Bali safe for American tourists?The State Department rates Indonesia, including Bali, at Level 2, Exercise increased caution. The main practical risks are traffic, drink safety, animal bites, mosquito-borne illness and strict drug laws.

Is there malaria in Bali?The CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands.

What is the emergency number in Bali?Dial 112 for the unified emergency line, 110 for police, 119 for an ambulance or 113 for fire.

Is there a US embassy in Bali?There is a US Consular Agency in Jimbaran, Bali. The embassy is in Jakarta.



===== bali-rules-for-tourists [culture-and-etiquette / None] 1076w =====
Bali's Rules for Tourists: What Gets People Fined or Deported
Bali has an official rulebook for foreign visitors, issued by the governor in 2025, and it is more specific than most travelers expect. It covers how to dress and behave at temples, how to pay for things, how to ride a scooter and where you may stay. The rules are enforced under Indonesian law, so read them before you go.


## The rulebook and where it comes from

Bali's current rules for foreign tourists are set out in Governor's Circular No. 7 of 2025, titled "New Order for Foreign Tourists While in Bali". The provincial government published it in March 2025 with a list of obligations, a list of prohibitions, and a statement that violations will be dealt with firmly under Indonesian law.


## Why older lists you find online are out of date

Search results for Bali's tourist rules are full of 2023 news stories about an earlier list of "dos and don'ts". That list was real, but the 2025 circular is the current text and it adds obligations the older one did not have, including rules on payment, licensed accommodation and transport.



## Rules at temples and sacred places

The temple rules in Bali's 2025 circular limit where a foreign visitor may go, and not only how to dress. Foreign tourists may not enter the inner and middle courtyards of a temple or other sanctified place unless they are there to pray, wearing Balinese traditional dress. The restriction concerns those inner courtyards, not temple grounds as a whole.


## What the circular prohibits at sacred sites

The text prohibits climbing sacred trees, climbing on sacred structures, and behavior that defiles a holy place, with posing for photos in indecent clothing or unclothed given as the example. The circular also states that a person entering the sacred courtyards must not be menstruating. We report that as what the document says.


## Dress and conduct

The obligations side asks visitors to honor the sanctity of temples and religious symbols, to respect local customs, and to wear modest, appropriate clothing at sacred places, tourist attractions and public areas. Our practical advice: carry a sarong and a light cover for your shoulders, so you are never caught unprepared at a temple gate.


## Rules about money

Bali's circular tells foreign tourists how to exchange and spend money. Visitors are to change currency only at authorized money changers, bank or non-bank, which are identified by a license number and a Bank Indonesia QR-code logo. Payments are to be made in rupiah, and the circular tells visitors to pay using Indonesia's standard QR code.


## What this means for Americans

Two points follow. First, use only licensed changers and ignore street-corner booths; the license number and logo are what to look for. Second, the instruction to pay by QR meets a practical limit: Indonesia's cross-border QR scheme covers a handful of Asian countries and the United States is not among them, so plan on cards and rupiah cash.


## The tourist levy is on the list

Paying Bali's tourist levy is one of the listed obligations, and the circular attaches a specific consequence: a tourist who has not paid can be refused service at tourist attractions. The levy can be paid online before you fly.


## Rules on the road

The road rules in Bali's circular apply to any foreign visitor who drives or rides. The circular requires a valid international or national driving license, obeying traffic law, wearing a helmet, not carrying more passengers than the vehicle allows, and not riding under the influence of alcohol or drugs.


## The licensing point

For Americans, the license requirement is the one that bites. The State Department says a US license alone is not recognized in Indonesia and that an International Driving Permit is required. If you plan to ride a scooter, read the licensing rules before you rent.


## Hired cars

The circular also requires visitors to use roadworthy four-wheeled transport that officially belongs to a rental business or rental association. In practice, book cars and drivers through an established company or your accommodation.


## Rules about where you stay and how you behave

Bali's circular requires foreign tourists to stay in accommodation that holds a license under the regulations, and it sets out a list of prohibited behavior. Visitors must not litter or pollute lakes, springs, rivers, the sea or public places, and must not use single-use plastics such as plastic bags, styrofoam, plastic straws and plastic-packaged drinks.


## Conduct

The prohibitions also cover abusive language, disorderly or aggressive behavior toward officials, local people or other tourists, and spreading hate speech or false information on social media. Working or doing business without the proper official documents is prohibited, which matters to anyone tempted to take paid work on a tourist visa.


## The plastics rule in practice

Our advice is simple: bring a reusable water bottle and a tote bag, and you will rarely need the items the circular bans.


## How the rules are enforced

Enforcement of Bali's visitor rules runs through ordinary Indonesian law, backed by public reporting. The circular says violators will be dealt with firmly and sanctioned or prosecuted under the applicable regulations, and it asks residents to report violations by foreign tourists to a provincial WhatsApp hotline.


## What the US government adds

The US Consular Agency in Bali reports a current enforcement campaign involving police, immigration, transport and tourism officials, with increased enforcement of traffic and vehicle violations, illegal work and other visa violations, and drug possession. The practical reading is simple: the rules above are being applied, so follow them.



## FAQ

What are the rules for tourists in Bali?Governor's Circular No. 7 of 2025 lists them: respect temples and customs, dress modestly, pay the tourist levy, use licensed money changers and pay in rupiah, hold a valid license and wear a helmet if you ride, stay in licensed accommodation, and do not litter or use single-use plastics.

Can tourists go inside temples in Bali?Visitors may not enter the inner and middle courtyards unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress. The restriction applies to those inner areas.

Are plastic bags banned in Bali?The 2025 circular prohibits foreign tourists from using single-use plastics, including plastic bags, styrofoam, plastic straws and plastic-packaged drinks.

What happens if a tourist breaks the rules in Bali?The circular says violations are dealt with under Indonesian law, and residents are asked to report them. A tourist who has not paid the levy can be refused service at attractions.



===== nyepi-bali-day-of-silence [when-to-go / holiday-collision] 1069w =====
Nyepi in Bali: What the Day of Silence Shuts Down
Nyepi is Bali's day of silence, the Balinese New Year. For 24 hours regular flights stop, mobile data is switched off, and residents and visitors are asked to stay indoors. For a traveler it is either a quiet day spent at the hotel or a day that collides with a flight. Which one depends on knowing the date.


## What Nyepi is and when it falls

Nyepi is the Balinese Hindu New Year under the Saka calendar, observed as a full day of silence across Bali, and it is an Indonesian national holiday. The date moves every year. The next Nyepi falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, according to Indonesia's official holiday decree.


## Why the date moves

Nyepi follows a lunar-based calendar, so it lands on a different day of the Western calendar each year, usually in March. In 2026 it fell on March 19. Do not rely on a date from an old blog post: check the current year's Indonesian holiday list before you book flights anywhere near March.



## What shuts down on Nyepi

Nyepi brings a full day of official closures in Bali, and they include things a traveler depends on. Mobile data and television and radio broadcasts are switched off for 24 hours, from 6 a.m. to 6 a.m., under a circular from Indonesia's communications ministry, with hospitals and other vital services exempt.


## The airport

Bali's airport stops regular commercial flights for the same 24 hours. For Nyepi 2026, the airport's general manager announced that all regular commercial flights would halt from 6 a.m. on the day until 6 a.m. the next morning, according to Indonesian press reports of the statement. Hours for 2027 have not been announced, so confirm your own flight with your airline.


## Staying indoors

The annual joint appeal issued by Bali's religious and provincial authorities asks residents and tourists alike not to travel or leave their accommodation during Nyepi, and prohibits fireworks, loudspeakers and other noise, as reported by Indonesian media. Visitors observe the day inside the place they are staying.


## Phones and internet

The ministry's circular covers mobile data and broadcast television and radio; operators text customers in the days beforehand to warn them. It does not cover fixed-line connections. Our advice: download maps, tickets, books and anything else you need the day before, and tell people at home that you will be hard to reach.


## What the 6 a.m. to 6 a.m. window does to your flights

The Nyepi closure runs from early morning to early morning, so it reaches into the nights on both sides. A flight due to land late the evening before, or to leave early the morning after, sits close to the edge of the window. An arrival or departure on Nyepi itself is not possible on a regular commercial flight.


## If you are flying from the United States

American itineraries are the ones most exposed to this. The journey crosses the date line and takes more than a day, so the calendar date you leave home is not the date you land. Work out your arrival date in Bali time, then check it against Nyepi. A connection that slips by a few hours can turn a safe arrival into one on the wrong side of the closure.


## Do not plan to arrive the night before

Our advice is to land at least a full day before Nyepi, not the evening before. That leaves room for a delayed connection, and it gives you time to reach your hotel and buy what you need before the island closes.


## Where to be when the island goes quiet

Nyepi is spent wherever you are staying, so the choice of accommodation for that night matters more than usual. You will be on the property for a full day and night with no option of going out to eat, to a beach or to another town. Choose a place where you would be content to do exactly that.


## What to arrange

Ask your accommodation before you book how it handles Nyepi: what meals are served, which facilities stay open, and what is expected of guests after dark. Buy snacks and water the day before, since you will not be able to go out for them.


## Is it worth being there?

Some travelers plan a trip to include Nyepi on purpose: a day with no traffic and no aircraft is rare anywhere. Others would rather not give up a day of a short trip. Both are reasonable, as long as it is a choice.


## Nyepi 2027 sits next to another holiday

Nyepi 2027 falls two days before Eid al-Fitr, a major national holiday in Indonesia. The official holiday decree puts Nyepi on Monday, March 8, with Eid al-Fitr on March 10 and 11 and collective leave days on March 9, 12 and 15, which makes most of that week a holiday across the country.


## What that means for booking

Our advice, if your dates fall in that week: book flights and rooms early, and treat any side trip to another island with more care than usual, since a national holiday week sits on both sides of the closure.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Nyepi looked like a single date to avoid or enjoy. In practice it fixed several other things at once: which day you can land, where you sleep that night, whether a side trip fits, and how much slack your return flight needs. A trip is a chain of dates, and one immovable day changes the links on either side of it.

Nyepi is the strictest of Bali's calendar days, and there are others that close roads or fill hotels. How to lay a trip over the Balinese calendar is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

When is Nyepi in 2027?Monday, March 8, 2027, according to Indonesia's official holiday decree.

Is the Bali airport closed on Nyepi?Yes. Regular commercial flights stop for 24 hours. In 2026 the closure ran from 6 a.m. on Nyepi to 6 a.m. the next day; confirm the current year's hours with your airline.

Can tourists go outside on Nyepi?Bali's annual joint appeal asks residents and tourists alike not to travel or leave their accommodation for the day, according to Indonesian press reports of the appeal.

Does the internet work in Bali on Nyepi?Mobile data is switched off for 24 hours under a ministerial circular, with hospitals and vital services exempt. The circular does not cover fixed-line connections.



===== vaccines-and-animal-bites-in-bali [health-and-safety / health-chain] 1109w =====
Vaccines for Bali, and What to Do After a Dog or Monkey Bite
No vaccine is required to enter Bali, and that fact leads many travelers to skip the subject. The CDC still recommends several for Indonesia, and one health risk on the island needs a decision before you fly, not after: rabies, which the CDC says is commonly found in dogs in Indonesia and is the reason a bite or scratch is always treated as urgent.


## Vaccines for Bali: required versus recommended

Indonesia requires no vaccinations for entry, according to the US State Department. The CDC separately recommends that travelers to Indonesia be up to date on routine vaccines and recommends hepatitis A, hepatitis B and typhoid for most travelers. Recommended is not the same as required: nobody checks at the airport.


## The routine ones first

The CDC's starting point is that routine vaccines should be current before any international trip. Its Indonesia page also carries a global measles notice, a reminder that the basics matter more than anything exotic. A pre-travel appointment is the simplest way to check your record.


## Hepatitis A and typhoid

Both are spread through contaminated food and water. The CDC recommends hepatitis A vaccination for unvaccinated travelers aged one and over going to Indonesia, and typhoid vaccination for most travelers. The CDC adds that the typhoid vaccine is not fully protective, so careful food and water habits still matter.



## Hepatitis B and Japanese encephalitis

The CDC recommends hepatitis B vaccination for unvaccinated travelers of all ages. Japanese encephalitis vaccine is a narrower recommendation, aimed at travelers who are moving to an affected area, spending long periods such as a month or more there, or visiting frequently. For a one- or two-week vacation, that one is a conversation with a doctor, not a default.


## Malaria, dengue and Zika

Mosquito-borne illness in Bali is mostly about dengue and Zika, not malaria. The CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands. Malaria is present in other parts of Indonesia, which matters if your trip continues beyond Bali.


## Dengue

The CDC lists dengue as a risk throughout Indonesia. There is no pill to take against it; protection means avoiding bites. Use an effective repellent during the day as well as in the evening, and choose accommodation with screens or air conditioning where you can.


## Zika and pregnancy

The CDC currently has a Level 2 travel health notice for Zika in Bali, issued after cases were reported in travelers returning from the island. The notice advises all travelers to Bali to prevent mosquito bites and sexual transmission during and after travel. Anyone who is pregnant or planning a pregnancy should read the notice and talk to a doctor before booking.


## Rabies is the decision to make before you fly

Rabies is a risk the CDC flags for the whole of Indonesia: it says dogs infected with rabies are commonly found in the country. The CDC also says rabies vaccines are typically available throughout most of the country after an exposure. Whether to be vaccinated before travel depends, in the CDC's words, on your planned activities and on how quickly you could reach treatment.


## Who should consider the pre-travel vaccine

The CDC frames it as two questions: will your activities increase the chance of contact with potentially rabid animals, and might you have difficulty getting prompt post-exposure treatment? Travelers heading to remote parts of the island, families with small children who are drawn to animals, and anyone planning long stays have more reason to ask their doctor about it.


## The cost question

Pre-travel rabies vaccination is a series of shots, and whether your US health plan pays for it is a question for your insurer. It does not remove the need for treatment after a bite; it simplifies that treatment. Ask your doctor and your insurer what each route would cost you.


## What to do after a dog or monkey bite

A bite or scratch from a dog, monkey or other mammal in Bali should be treated as a possible rabies exposure, whether or not the animal looked healthy. The CDC's instruction is to seek medical attention immediately. Rabies is fatal once symptoms appear, and treatment given promptly after exposure prevents it.


## The first minutes

Wash the wound thoroughly with soap and running water, then go to a clinic or hospital the same day. Do not wait to see whether the wound looks serious, and do not let a small scratch persuade you that it does not count. Tell the staff exactly what happened and what animal it was.


## Monkeys are the common case

Monkeys at Bali's temples and forests live close to visitors. Do not feed them, do not carry food in your hands, and do not try to take anything back that a monkey has grabbed. Keep small children close. The CDC's general advice for Indonesia is to avoid wild animals and unfamiliar dogs and cats.


## Call your insurer the same day

If you have travel insurance, phone the assistance number as soon as you have washed the wound and are on your way to care. Treatment can involve several visits, and it is easier to have the insurer involved from the start than to claim afterward.


## Food and water habits

Food and water precautions matter in Bali because the two most commonly recommended vaccines, hepatitis A and typhoid, protect against illnesses spread that way, and neither covers ordinary traveler's diarrhea. The CDC advises travelers to Indonesia to follow safe food and water practices and to wash hands at key times.


## Practical habits

Our advice, beyond the official wording: drink sealed or filtered water, eat food that is cooked and served hot, and be more careful in the first days while your stomach adjusts. The CDC also warns against swimming or wading in water that may be contaminated, particularly after flooding.



## FAQ

Do I need any vaccines for Bali?None are required for entry. The CDC recommends being current on routine vaccines and recommends hepatitis A, hepatitis B and typhoid for most travelers to Indonesia.

Do I need malaria pills for Bali?The CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands. Other parts of Indonesia do have malaria.

What should I do if a monkey bites me in Bali?Wash the wound thoroughly with soap and water and seek medical attention immediately. Treat any bite or scratch as a possible rabies exposure.

Is there rabies in Bali?The CDC says dogs infected with rabies are commonly found in Indonesia, and its advice applies to travelers in Bali: avoid unfamiliar animals and seek care immediately after a bite.



===== international-driving-permit-for-bali [scooters / scooter-liability] 981w =====
Do Americans Need an International Driving Permit in Bali?
Yes. An American who wants to drive in Bali needs an International Driving Permit, carried together with a US license. The permit is necessary but not always sufficient: it only translates the license you already hold, so it does not make a car license valid for a scooter. A US permit is issued at home, so arrange it before you leave.


## What the rule is

The US State Department states the rule for Indonesia in one line: a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia, and foreign drivers must carry an International Driving Permit. Bali's own 2025 rules for foreign visitors say the same from the other side, requiring a valid international or national driving license.


## What the permit actually is

An International Driving Permit is a booklet that translates your existing license into several languages in a standard format. It is not a license. It has no force without the US license it was issued against, so carry both documents together.


## Where Americans get one

In the United States the permit is issued by AAA, and AAA's own country list includes Indonesia among the countries that honor it. You apply before you travel, with your valid US license. Plan this as a pre-departure errand, alongside checking your passport's expiry date.



## What the permit covers, and what it does not

An International Driving Permit carries over the vehicle classes on your US license and nothing more. Indonesia's traffic law requires every driver to hold a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and the law treats motorcycles as their own license class, separate from cars.


## Cars

If your US license covers passenger cars, the permit translates exactly that. With both documents, an American is carrying what the State Department says a foreign driver must carry. Whether you want to drive in Bali is a separate matter from whether you may: traffic keeps left, and the State Department describes roads shared with large numbers of motorcycles that pass on either side.


## Scooters and motorcycles

Scooters are where the permit is most often misunderstood. As we read the statute, a license that covers cars only does not match a motorcycle, and a permit that translates a car license cannot add a motorcycle class. If your US license has no motorcycle endorsement, our advice is to treat yourself as unlicensed on a scooter. That is an interpretation, not an official ruling, and we found no official page that says a car license is enough.


## What happens without one

Driving or riding in Indonesia without the required license is an offense under the national traffic law. The statute sets a maximum of 4 months' detention or a fine of IDR 1,000,000 for riding without the required license, with a lower maximum for a driver who holds a license and cannot produce it. Those are legal ceilings, not a roadside price list.


## Checks are part of a wider campaign

The US Consular Agency in Bali reports a current enforcement campaign involving police, immigration, transport and tourism officials, with increased enforcement of traffic and vehicle violations by foreigners. The State Department's advice, if you are fined, is to ask for an official receipt and follow the local procedure.


## The larger cost is not the fine

A fine is the small consequence. The larger one is what an unlicensed rider's position looks like after a crash, both with the police and with a travel insurer. Ask your insurer in writing, before you go, whether you are covered while riding a vehicle your home license does not cover, and keep the reply.


## Other documents to carry on the road

A driver in Indonesia is expected to carry more than a license. The State Department says drivers must also carry identification and the vehicle's registration documents and present them on request. For a rental, that means asking for the registration papers when you collect the vehicle and keeping them with you.


## A simple kit

Our advice: keep the permit, your US license, a copy of your passport's photo page and the registration together in one waterproof sleeve. Leave your passport itself somewhere safe, and do not hand it to a rental shop as a deposit. If a shop asks to hold your passport, offer a photocopy or a cash deposit instead; your passport is the document you can least afford to be without.


## Helmets

Helmets are required for motorcycle riders and passengers under Indonesian law, and Bali's rules for visitors repeat the requirement.


## Do you need a permit if you never drive?

A visitor who does not drive or ride in Bali has no need for an International Driving Permit. Ride-hailing apps operate in Indonesia and licensed taxis are available, and cars can be hired with a driver. For many first trips that is the simpler arrangement, since it removes the licensing question entirely.


## Getting one anyway

If there is any chance you will rent a car or scooter, get the permit before you leave. It is issued in the United States, and a rental is the kind of decision that gets made on the spot. Whether riding fits your trip at all depends on where you stay and how you plan to move between places, a connection worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Do Americans need an International Driving Permit in Bali?Yes, to drive or ride. The State Department says a US license alone is not recognized in Indonesia and that foreign drivers must carry an International Driving Permit.

Where do I get an International Driving Permit in the US?From AAA, before you travel. AAA lists Indonesia among the countries that honor the permit.

Does an International Driving Permit cover scooters in Bali?The permit translates the classes on your US license. Indonesian law treats motorcycles as a separate license class, so a car-only license does not match a scooter.

Can I get an International Driving Permit in Bali?No. A US permit is issued in the United States against your US license, so arrange it before you leave.



===== money-in-bali-cash-cards-atms [money-and-costs / money-leak] 1134w =====
Money in Bali: Cash, Cards and ATMs
Bali runs on rupiah, and a visitor needs both a card and cash to get through a day comfortably. Plan on a card for the larger purchases and rupiah notes for the small ones. The rules around money are few, but each one costs a little if you ignore it, and this page sets them out in order.


## The currency, and the rule about using it

The currency in Bali is the Indonesian rupiah, and prices are quoted in thousands and millions of it. Bali's 2025 rules for foreign visitors require transactions to be made in rupiah, so a price offered in US dollars is not how you should expect to pay. Carry rupiah for small purchases and a card for larger ones.


## Getting used to the zeros

Rupiah amounts are large numbers: Bali's tourist levy, for example, is IDR 150,000 per person. Prices are sometimes written with the thousands dropped, as "150" or "150K". Count the zeros before you hand over notes; a 10,000 and a 100,000 note differ by one digit and a factor of ten.


## Do not freeze an exchange rate in your head

The rupiah moves against the dollar, so check the rate when you travel instead of relying on a figure from an old article. Check the current rate on the day you travel and work out one easy rule of thumb for yourself, such as what 100,000 rupiah is worth in dollars this week.

100 USD ≈ … IDR (enable JavaScript to see today's rate)


## Changing money without losing any

Bali's rules for visitors say to change money only at a licensed changer, and they describe how to recognize one. The 2025 circular tells foreign tourists to exchange currency only at authorized money changers, bank or non-bank, which are identified by a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo carrying a QR code.


## What to look for

Look for the license number and the Bank Indonesia QR logo displayed at the counter before you hand anything over. A booth that cannot show them is not one the rules allow you to use, whatever rate it advertises on the board outside.


## Our advice at the counter

This part is advice, not regulation. Agree the amount before the notes are counted, count the rupiah yourself in front of the cashier, and be the last person to touch the stack before it goes into your wallet. Do not let anyone recount it for you after you have checked it.


## Cards and ATMs

Cards are the practical way to pay for the large items of a Bali trip, and a US card works for the official payments too. Indonesia's e-visa site takes Visa, Mastercard and JCB, and Bali's tourist levy site takes Visa, Mastercard, American Express and JCB. Bring at least two cards on different networks, kept in different bags.


## Foreign transaction fees

Prices in Bali are charged in rupiah, so check before you leave whether your card adds a foreign transaction fee, and how much. Our advice is to carry a card that does not. When a terminal offers to charge you in dollars instead of rupiah, our advice is to choose rupiah and let your own bank do the conversion.


## ATMs

We have not found an official source for withdrawal limits or operator fees at Bali's ATMs, and the figures in travel blogs disagree with each other, so we are not going to quote one. The part you can check before you go is your own bank's fee schedule for withdrawals abroad, and our advice is to read it.


## Paying by QR code

QR payment is Indonesia's national standard, and Bali's rules for visitors tell tourists to use it, but the cross-border version is not open to American banking apps. Bank Indonesia's cross-border QR scheme connects Thailand, Malaysia, Singapore, Japan, South Korea and China. The United States is not on that list.

For an American visitor that leaves cards and cash as the two working methods. The QR question has a page of its own, including what the central bank says and what it does not.


## What gets added to the bill

Restaurant and hotel bills in Bali carry a local tax, and may also carry a separate service charge. In Badung regency, which covers Kuta, Seminyak, Canggu, Uluwatu and Nusa Dua, the local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%. Gianyar regency, which includes Ubud, sets the same rate.


## Tax versus service charge

The two lines on a bill are different things. The tax goes to the local government. A service charge, under an Indonesian labor regulation covering hotels and hotel restaurants, is an amount added on top of the listed price for service, and it must be distributed to the staff. The regulation sets no percentage, so the rate is whatever the business has chosen.


## Read the menu's small print

A menu or room rate may be quoted before tax and service. Our advice is to look for a line at the bottom of the menu saying whether prices include them, so the total does not surprise you.


## Carrying cash across the border

Cash is not capped when you enter or leave Indonesia, but large amounts must be declared. Customs requires a declaration for money or other payment instruments worth IDR 100,000,000 or more, in rupiah or the foreign-currency equivalent. That is far more than a vacation calls for, so for most travelers the rule is one to know exists.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. No single item was large: a foreign transaction fee, a conversion offered at the terminal, a tax line, a service line, a changer's rate. Each one is a small percentage, and they stack on the same dollar. The trip's real cost is the listed prices plus that stack, and the stack is decided by choices made at home: which cards you carry and what your bank charges.

How those small percentages add up across a whole trip, and which ones you can remove before you leave, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Can I use US dollars in Bali?Bali's rules for foreign visitors require transactions to be made in rupiah. Change dollars at a licensed money changer or withdraw rupiah from an ATM.

Can I use my credit card in Bali?US cards work on the official visa and tourist levy sites, and a card is the practical way to pay for larger purchases. Carry rupiah cash for small ones.

How do I know a money changer in Bali is legitimate?Authorized changers display a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo with a QR code. Bali's rules tell visitors to use only those.

Is tax included in restaurant prices in Bali?Often not. In Badung and Gianyar regencies the local tax on restaurant food and drink is 10%, and many businesses add a separate service charge.



===== tipping-in-bali [money-and-costs / None] 1076w =====
Tipping in Bali: Service Charge, Tax and When to Add More
Tipping in Bali is optional, and the bill has often done part of the job already. Many hotel and restaurant bills include a service charge that, by regulation, goes to the staff, plus a local tax that does not. Once you can read those two lines, deciding what to leave on top becomes simple.


## What is already on the bill

A restaurant or hotel bill in Bali can carry two additions to the menu price: a local tax and a service charge. In Badung regency, which covers Kuta, Seminyak, Canggu, Jimbaran, Uluwatu and Nusa Dua, the local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%.


## The tax line

The tax is a regional government charge on food, drink and accommodation. Each regency sets its own rate; Gianyar regency, which includes Ubud, also sets it at 10% for restaurants and hotels. This money goes to the local government and has nothing to do with the people who served you.


## The service line

A service charge is different. Under an Indonesian labor regulation, a service charge at a hotel or a hotel restaurant is an amount added on top of the listed price for service, and it must be distributed to the workers, half shared equally and the rest by seniority and performance, after allowances for breakage or loss and for staff development. The regulation sets no percentage, so the rate you see is the business's own.



## Why this matters for tipping

If your bill shows a service charge at a hotel or hotel restaurant, part of what you paid is already going to the staff as a group. An extra tip is then a personal thank-you to the person who looked after you, not a correction to an unfair bill. Where no service charge appears, nothing has gone to the staff beyond their wages.


## How to read "plus plus"

A price in Bali may be quoted before tax and service, with the two additions applied when the bill is totaled. A menu may mark this with a line of small print or with plus signs after the price. The total you pay is the listed price with both additions on top.


## Check before you order

Our advice is to look at the foot of the menu for a line saying whether prices include tax and service. If it is not stated, ask. Where both are added, the total is higher than the menu price, and it is better to know that before you order.


## The nightlife tax is much higher

One category is taxed differently. In Badung and Gianyar, entertainment at bars, nightclubs, discos, karaoke venues and spas was set at a 40% local tax by the regencies' 2023 tax regulations, in force from January 2024. That rate was publicly contested after it took effect and may have been amended since, so look at how a venue states its prices instead of assuming a figure.


## When to leave more, and how

Tipping beyond the bill is a personal choice in Bali, with no official rule or fixed percentage behind it. We could not find an authoritative source for customary amounts, and the figures quoted on travel sites vary widely. The suggestions here are our own view of what is reasonable, not a local standard.


## Restaurants and cafes

Where a service charge is on the bill, leaving nothing further is fine. Where it is not, rounding the bill up or leaving a modest amount in cash is a kind gesture that will be appreciated. Give it directly to your server if you want it to reach that person.


## Drivers and guides

A driver who has spent the day with you, or a guide on a trek or tour, has given you a personal service that no bill line covers. A cash tip at the end of the day is appropriate if you were happy. Decide the amount by how long they worked and how much they added to your day.


## Hotel staff and spa therapists

For housekeeping, porters and spa therapists, a small cash tip handed over personally is the clearest way to say thank you. If the hotel adds a service charge, the staff already share in it, and an individual tip is an extra.


## Practical points about paying tips

Tips in Bali are best given in rupiah cash, handed to the person you mean to thank. Bali's rules for foreign visitors require transactions to be in rupiah, and small rupiah notes are useful all day for exactly this. Keep a separate fold of small notes so you are not searching for change.


## Cash versus card

If you add a tip to a card payment, you have no way of knowing how it is distributed. Cash handed to an individual removes the question.


## Do not tip in coins or foreign change

Our advice is to tip in rupiah. Foreign coins are the least useful thing to leave, because the person receiving them has to find a way to change them; if all you have is dollars, a banknote is better than coins.


## Bargaining is separate from tipping

Bargaining and tipping are two different customs, and mixing them causes awkwardness. Our view is that a price you have agreed with a market stall or an independent driver is the whole price, and adding to it is your choice.


## Agree the price first

For anything without a posted price, such as a day with a driver, agree the total and what it covers before you set off. Then a tip at the end is clearly a gift, and neither side is left guessing.



## FAQ

Do you tip in Bali?Tipping is optional. A hotel or restaurant bill may already include a service charge, which at hotels must by regulation be distributed to staff, and an extra cash tip is a personal thank-you.

Is service charge included in Bali restaurants?Many businesses add one, on top of a local tax. The service charge rate is set by the business; the local tax on restaurant food and drink is 10% in Badung and Gianyar regencies.

Should I tip in rupiah or dollars?Rupiah. Bali's rules for visitors require transactions in rupiah, and foreign coins cannot be exchanged.

How much is tax on a restaurant bill in Bali?The local tax on restaurant food and drink is 10% in Badung and Gianyar regencies. Bars, nightclubs and spas are subject to a much higher entertainment tax.



===== paying-by-qr-code-in-bali [money-and-costs / money-leak] 1034w =====
Paying by QR Code in Bali: Can Foreign Cards and Apps Use It?
QR codes are Indonesia's standard way to pay, and Bali's own rules tell visitors to use them. For Americans there is a catch: the cross-border system that lets foreigners scan those codes with their home banking app does not include the United States. Here is what the central bank says, and what to carry instead.


## What the QR codes are

The QR codes at shops and restaurants in Bali belong to QRIS, Indonesia's national standard for QR payments, which is run under Bank Indonesia, the central bank. A customer scans the merchant's code with a payment app, enters the amount in rupiah and confirms. It is a single national standard, not one company's product.


## Why visitors keep hearing about it

Bali's 2025 rules for foreign visitors tell tourists to make payments using Indonesia's standard QR code and to transact in rupiah. The province's tourist levy site also lists QRIS among its payment methods. So the system is part of the official picture a visitor is given, even if not every visitor can use it.



## Which foreign visitors can scan and pay
