Foreign visitors can pay by QRIS only if their home country is connected to Indonesia's cross-border scheme. Bank Indonesia lists the connected countries as Thailand, Malaysia, Singapore, Japan, South Korea and China. A visitor from one of those opens a home payment app, scans, enters the rupiah amount and confirms in their own currency.


## How the cross-border version works

Bank Indonesia describes the steps for a foreign user: open the payment application, scan the QRIS code, enter the payment amount in rupiah, confirm the destination and the amount in your own currency, and enter a PIN. The merchant uses the same printed code as for domestic customers. The conversion happens inside the visitor's own app.


## Where the United States stands

The United States is not among the connected countries, and it does not appear anywhere on Bank Indonesia's page about the scheme. That means a US bank's app or a US digital wallet has no cross-border route to pay a QRIS code. Scanning the code with your phone's camera will not produce a payment screen you can use.


## What an American should carry instead

An American visitor to Bali pays with cards and rupiah cash. Cards cover the official payments: Indonesia's e-visa site accepts Visa, Mastercard and JCB, and Bali's tourist levy site accepts Visa, Mastercard, American Express and JCB. Cash covers the small purchases where a QR code would have been the local customer's choice.


## Cards

Bring two cards on different networks and keep them apart. If your card charges a foreign transaction fee, it applies to every purchase in Bali, so a card without one is worth carrying. When a terminal offers to bill you in dollars, our advice is to choose rupiah.


## Cash

Keep a supply of small rupiah notes. Change money only at authorized changers, which Bali's rules identify by a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo with a QR code, or withdraw rupiah from an ATM.


## Official fees

The payments a visitor owes the authorities do not depend on QR. The visa fee and the tourist levy can both be paid by card on their official sites before you leave home, which is the simplest way to deal with them. The levy site lists QRIS as one option among several, and a card works in its place.


## If a place takes only QR

Ask whether cash is accepted. Bali's rules require transactions in rupiah, and rupiah notes are legal tender. If a counter really cannot take cash or a card, you will need to buy elsewhere.


## What about tourist wallets and local apps?

Whether a short-stay American can set up a local wallet to pay by QR is a question we could not settle from an official source. Bank Indonesia's page on the cross-border scheme describes payments from the connected countries and says nothing about a route for other visitors. Press reports describe products aimed at tourists, and we have not verified how they work for a US traveler.


## Why we are not recommending a workaround

Without a source we trust on how a visitor would open and fund a local wallet, the honest advice is not to build a trip around QR payment. Plan on cards and cash.


## If the list changes

Bank Indonesia has added countries to the scheme over time, and its page is the place to check. If the United States is added, American banking apps that take part would be able to pay a QRIS code the way visitors from the connected countries do now.


## Reading a QR code is not paying it

Paying with a QRIS code needs a payment app that is connected to the scheme; a phone's camera can read the code, but that alone does not move any money. Pointing a phone camera at it, or using a generic QR reader, shows a string of characters or an error. If a seller asks you to scan a code and you are an American visitor, the practical answer is to ask for the card terminal or pay in cash.


## Be careful with payment links

Our advice applies anywhere: do not enter card details into a web page that a QR code opened unless you can see it is the business's own or a payment provider you recognize. The official sites for Indonesia's visa and Bali's tourist levy are reached by typing their addresses, not by scanning a code on a poster.



## FAQ

Can Americans use QRIS in Bali?Not through US banking apps. Bank Indonesia's cross-border QR scheme connects Thailand, Malaysia, Singapore, Japan, South Korea and China; the United States is not included.

Does Apple Pay work with QR codes in Bali?QRIS codes are paid through apps that belong to Indonesia's QR scheme. Bank Indonesia's list of connected countries does not include the United States.

How should Americans pay in Bali?With cards for larger purchases and rupiah cash for small ones. The official visa and tourist levy sites accept major US card networks.

What is QRIS?QRIS is Indonesia's national QR payment standard, run under Bank Indonesia. It is a single standard used across the country.



===== indonesia-phone-registration-for-tourists [phones-and-internet / None] 1068w =====
Indonesia's Phone Registration Rule: Do Tourists Need It?
No. A tourist visiting Bali for a normal vacation does not need to register a phone with Indonesian customs. Indonesia does have a phone registration system that ties lasting use of its mobile networks to a registered device, but customs itself says short-stay visitors are outside it in the two ways that matter. The confusion comes from rules written for residents.


## What the registration system is

Indonesia runs an IMEI registration system for phones brought in from abroad, and its customs service publishes the rules. The IMEI is the identifying number of a phone. Customs sets out two cases in which a short-stay visitor does not need to register at all.


## Why it exists

The system is about imported devices and the duty owed on them. Indonesian customs lists IMEI registration among the matters a passenger declares on arrival, alongside goods above the duty-free value. A resident bringing a new phone home from abroad is the case it was built for.



## What customs says about tourists

Indonesian customs answers the tourist question directly in its own guidance. For a person staying only temporarily, such as a tourist or business visitor, IMEI registration is not required in two situations: when the phone keeps using its home SIM on roaming, and when the visitor uses an Indonesian SIM registered at a mobile operator's outlet.


## If you roam on your US plan

A phone that keeps its American SIM and connects through your US carrier's roaming is not affected. The registration rule concerns devices using an Indonesian signal provider, and a roaming phone is still a customer of its home network. Check your carrier's international rates before you rely on this for a whole trip.


## If you buy a local SIM

A visitor who wants a local number registers the SIM at an operator's outlet, and customs says this gives network access for up to 90 days. That covers any trip on a 30-day tourist visa, including one that is extended. Our advice is to bring your passport to the outlet.


## Where a travel eSIM fits

A travel eSIM bought from an international provider before you leave works like roaming from the phone's point of view: the plan belongs to a foreign provider and connects through partner networks in Indonesia. Customs' guidance names roaming on a home SIM and a local SIM registered at an outlet; it does not mention travel eSIMs by name.


## Our reading

We read a foreign travel eSIM as falling on the roaming side of that line, since it is not an Indonesian subscription. That is an inference from the wording, not a statement by customs. If you want certainty, the two cases customs does name are the safe ones: your own carrier's roaming, or a local SIM set up at an operator's shop.


## Check that your phone is unlocked

A local SIM or a travel eSIM only works in a phone that is not locked to your US carrier. This is a matter between you and your carrier, separate from Indonesian rules. Ask your carrier before you travel.


## What to do at the airport

An American tourist on a short stay does not need to visit the customs registration desk for a personal phone. If you plan to roam or to use a local SIM from an operator's outlet, customs' own guidance says registration is not required, so you can walk past the counter with a clear conscience.


## When the answer is different

The picture changes for someone staying long term. The 90-day access that comes with an outlet-registered tourist SIM is a limit as well as an allowance. Anyone planning to stay beyond that, or to leave a phone behind in Indonesia, is in the situation the registration system was designed for and should read the customs rules for residents.


## Bringing a new, boxed phone

A brand-new phone in a sealed box is a different matter from the one in your pocket. Customs allows personal goods up to USD 500 per person free of duty and treats goods that are not for personal use as a declaration matter. A device you are bringing as a gift for someone who lives in Indonesia is outside the two visitor cases above, so ask customs at arrival how it should be handled.


## Choosing how to stay connected

The registration rule leaves an American tourist three workable options for mobile service in Bali: roaming on a US plan, a travel eSIM bought in advance, or a local SIM bought at an operator's outlet. None of them requires dealing with customs. The choice comes down to cost, convenience and whether you want a local number.


## Roaming

Roaming is the least effort, and the cost depends entirely on your US plan, so check its international rates. It keeps your US number working for calls and verification texts, which matters for banking apps that send codes by text.


## Travel eSIM

A travel eSIM is set up before you fly and connects when you land, with no shop visit. Many such plans provide data only, in which case your US number remains on your physical SIM for texts if you leave that line switched on.


## Local SIM

A local SIM gives you an Indonesian number, which is useful for apps and services that expect one. Buy it at an operator's own outlet, since that is the route customs names, and expect to show your passport.



## FAQ

Do tourists need to register their phone in Bali?No. Indonesian customs says IMEI registration is not required for short-stay visitors who roam on their home SIM or who use an Indonesian SIM registered at a mobile operator's outlet.

Will my phone be blocked in Indonesia?Not on a normal vacation. A tourist SIM registered at an operator's outlet gives network access for up to 90 days, and a phone roaming on its home SIM is not affected.

Do I have to pay tax on my phone when entering Bali?A personal phone you are carrying for your own use on a short visit does not need registration. A new, boxed device brought as a gift is an import and is treated differently.

Does a travel eSIM need IMEI registration in Indonesia?Customs' guidance does not mention travel eSIMs by name. It exempts short-stay visitors roaming on a home SIM; we read a foreign travel eSIM as falling on that side, which is our inference.



===== bali-visa-on-arrival-vs-e-voa [visas-and-documents / entry-stack] 1015w =====
Visa on Arrival vs. e-VOA for Bali: Which to Get
They are the same visa. The visa on arrival and the e-VOA both give an American the same B1 tourist visa, at the same official price, for the same 30 days. The only real difference is where you do the paperwork: at your kitchen table before you fly, or at a counter in the airport after a very long journey.


## What you get either way

The visa on arrival and the e-VOA are two ways of buying Indonesia's B1 tourist visa, and the result is identical. Either route costs IDR 500,000 and gives a stay of 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days. The same rules about what you may do in Indonesia apply to both.


## What the letters mean

VOA stands for visa on arrival. The "e" in e-VOA means electronic: the same visa, applied and paid for online in advance. Indonesia's official e-visa site lists it for US passport holders as "B1 - Tourist (Visa On Arrival)". If you see the two terms treated as different products with different prices, you are probably looking at an agency's page.



## The case for buying online first

Buying the e-VOA before you fly moves the payment and form-filling out of the airport, and the State Department notes that travelers who wait to buy at the airport may face long lines. There is no nonstop flight from the United States to Bali, so the visa counter comes at the end of a long journey with at least one connection.


## How the online route works

You apply on Indonesia's official e-visa website, run by the Directorate General of Immigration, and pay by Visa, Mastercard or JCB card. One application can cover up to five people, which suits a family. The application asks for your departure date and a booked return flight. Once approved, the visa is sent as a download link by email.


## How early to do it

The visa is valid for entry for 90 days, so it can be bought well ahead. Our advice is to do it once your flights are booked and your passport details are final, leaving time to sort out any problem with the application before you travel.


## What can go wrong online

The main risk online is the wrong website. Third-party sites may charge a fee on top of the official price for the same application. Use the address printed on the State Department's Indonesia page, and check that the price you are asked for matches the official one.


## The case for buying at the airport

Buying the visa on arrival at the airport needs no preparation, which makes it the fallback for a trip booked at short notice or an application left too late. The State Department confirms that a traveler can purchase a visa upon arrival at the airport, at a booth located before the immigration counters.


## What it costs you

The price is the same. The cost is time and attention at the end of a long travel day: a payment counter first, then the immigration line.


## When it is the right choice

The airport route makes sense if you could not complete the online application, if your plans changed at the last moment, or if you are uneasy about entering card details on a website you are not sure is the official one. It is a perfectly valid way to enter Bali.


## What neither route changes

Choosing the e-VOA or the airport counter does not change the entry requirements that actually turn people away. A US passport must be valid for at least 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages, and immigration can ask to see an outbound ticket from Indonesia.


## The arrival card is separate

Every traveler also completes Indonesia's arrival card, which is free and separate from the visa. It only opens shortly before travel: fill it in during the last two days before you land, and save the QR code it produces.


## So is the tourist levy

Bali's tourist levy is a third item, charged by the province, at IDR 150,000 per person. Paying for an e-VOA does not pay the levy.


## Extending either one

Both versions of the visa can be extended once, for another 30 days, and the official fee for a 30-day stay permit is IDR 500,000 per application. Indonesia's immigration directorate says the extension is applied for online at the official e-visa site whichever way the visa was bought.


## An office visit is part of it

Since 29 May 2025 an extension also requires a photograph and an interview at an immigration office, after the online application. That applies to the e-VOA and to the airport visa alike, so the way you bought the visa does not change how much effort the extension takes.


## Start the extension early

Whichever route you used, begin the extension well before the first 30 days end. An expired visa is an overstay, and overstaying carries a fine of IDR 1,000,000 per day.


## Our recommendation

For most Americans the e-VOA is the better choice, because it removes a line from the worst moment of the journey and costs the same. Buy it on the official site after your flights are booked. If that does not work out, the airport counter is there. Where the visa sits among the other pre-flight steps is laid out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is the e-VOA the same as the visa on arrival?Yes. Both are Indonesia's B1 tourist visa, with the same official fee and the same 30-day stay. The e-VOA is bought online in advance; the visa on arrival is bought at the airport.

Is the e-VOA cheaper than the visa on arrival?No. The official fee is the same either way. A higher price online usually means a third-party agency.

How long before my trip can I get an e-VOA?The visa is valid for entry for 90 days, so it can be bought well in advance.

Can I still get a visa at Bali airport?Yes. The State Department confirms a visa can be purchased on arrival at the airport, at a booth before the immigration counters.



===== indonesia-arrival-declaration-form [visas-and-documents / entry-stack] 1152w =====
Indonesia's Arrival Declaration Form: What to Fill In Before You Land
Every traveler flying into Bali has to submit Indonesia's arrival card, called All Indonesia, before reaching immigration. The card is free, it is filled in online or in the official app, and it can only be completed within 72 hours of arrival. It is a separate step from the visa and from Bali's tourist levy, and finishing one of those does not finish the others.


## What the arrival card is

Indonesia's arrival card is a digital declaration that every international passenger submits through the government's All Indonesia system. The State Department tells American travelers to complete an arrival card online or through the All Indonesia mobile app, and Indonesia's own e-visa site states that all travelers are required to submit one before arriving. The result is a QR code that you show on arrival.


## One form in place of several

Guidance issued to international conference delegates in 2025 describes the card as a single form that combines the immigration, customs and health declarations, replacing the separate customs and health forms that arriving passengers used before. That is why older trip reports mention a customs form with its own QR code. For a trip now, the arrival card is the form to look for.


## It is not a visa

The arrival card does not give anyone permission to enter Indonesia. An American still needs a tourist visa, and can buy it online in advance or at the airport on arrival. The visa has a fee; the arrival card has none. If a website asks for payment to file an arrival card, it is not the official one.


## When to fill in the arrival card

The arrival card can only be completed in the last 72 hours before you arrive in Indonesia. The State Department notes that the website will not let you finish the card if you try earlier than that. Our advice is to do it during the last two days before you land, when your flight number and first hotel are settled and the window is open.


## Count from arrival, not departure

The window is measured from your arrival in Indonesia. There is no nonstop flight from the United States to Bali, and a trip with a connection crosses many time zones and the date line. Work out the local date you land in Bali, then count back. Our advice is to do it the evening before you leave home, or at the layover airport if the window has not opened yet.


## Why not earlier

The system refuses early submissions, so there is nothing to gain by trying to file a week ahead. Put a reminder in your calendar for two days before arrival instead, next to the reminder to check in for your flight.



## What the form asks for

The arrival card asks for the details an immigration officer and a customs officer would otherwise collect on paper. The delegate guidance lists passport details, an email address and phone number, arrival and departure dates, the flight, the place you will stay, and a set of health and customs questions. Have your passport and your booking confirmations beside you before you start.


## Passport and contact details

Enter your name and passport number as they are printed in the passport. The same guidance says the QR code is sent to the email address you give, so use one you can open on your phone while traveling. Each person needs a card of their own, including children and infants, so a family of four submits four.


## Travel and accommodation

You will be asked for your arrival date, your flight, and where you are staying. Use the address of your first night's accommodation. Indonesia's visa rules also expect a visitor to hold an outbound ticket from Indonesia, so have your departure date and flight ready as well.


## Customs questions

The customs part asks whether you are carrying things that must be declared. The rules behind those questions are fixed by Indonesian customs. Personal goods are free of duty up to USD 500 per person per arrival, an adult may bring 1 liter of alcohol, and cash worth IDR 100,000,000 or more must be declared. Medicines are on the list of items that send a passenger to the inspection channel, so answer the medicine question accurately and carry your prescriptions.


## What to do with the QR code

Submitting the arrival card produces a QR code, and the State Department's instruction is to save it and show it to immigration and customs when you arrive. Our advice is to keep a screenshot in your phone's photos, where it opens without a connection, and to save one copy per traveler. A printed copy in your passport wallet costs nothing and survives a flat battery.


## Answer truthfully

The card is a declaration to two government agencies. Our advice, if your answers change after you submit it, for example because you buy duty-free alcohol on the way, is to tell the customs officer when you arrive. Alcohol and tobacco above the allowance are destroyed on the spot by the customs officer, so a purchase at a layover airport counts toward the same limit.


## Where the arrival card fits with the visa and the levy

An American flying to Bali deals with three separate systems before or at arrival: the tourist visa, at IDR 500,000; Bali's tourist levy, at IDR 150,000 per person; and the arrival card, which is free. Each is handled separately. Only the arrival card has a time window that stops you from doing it early.


## A workable order

Our advice is to handle the visa and the levy once your flights are booked, since neither has a short window, and to leave the arrival card for the last two days. Keep all three confirmations in one folder on your phone. How these steps line up with the passport rules and the rest of the pre-flight list is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.


## Use the official addresses

The State Department's Indonesia page links to the official arrival card site and names the All Indonesia app, and that page is the safest place to start from. The card is free of charge. A site that charges a fee is a third party, and you have no need to give it your passport details.



## FAQ

Do I need an arrival card for Bali if I already have an e-VOA?Yes. The visa and the arrival card are separate. Every traveler submits an arrival card, whatever visa they hold.

How early can I fill in the Indonesia arrival card?Only within 72 hours before you arrive. The State Department notes that the website will not let you complete it earlier.

Does the Indonesia arrival card cost anything?No. The State Department describes the service as completely free. A site that asks for payment is not the official one.

Do children need their own arrival card?Guidance issued to international delegates in 2025 says each person needs a card, including children and infants.



===== extending-a-bali-visa-on-arrival [visas-and-documents / None] 1145w =====
Extending a Bali Visa on Arrival: How It Works
An American on a visa on arrival can stay in Bali for 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days. The extension is applied for and paid online, and since late May 2025 it also requires a photograph and an interview at an immigration office. The official fee is IDR 500,000 per application. Start before the first 30 days run out.


## Who can extend a visa on arrival

A visa on arrival can be extended whether it was bought online as an e-VOA or as a sticker at the airport counter. Indonesia's immigration directorate said in November 2024 that the online extension is open to holders of both versions. The extension is granted once, for another 30 days, which makes 60 days the longest stay on this visa.


## What an extension does not do

An extension lengthens the stay; it does not change what the visa allows. The same conditions apply for the second 30 days as for the first, including the ban on selling goods or services, or being paid by anyone in Indonesia. A visitor who wants to stay beyond 60 days needs a different visa, and that is a separate application with its own rules.


## Decide before you fly if you can

If you already know the trip will run past 30 days, plan the extension into your itinerary before you leave home. It requires a visit to an immigration office, so part of a day has to be set aside for it. Knowing that in advance lets you choose where you will be staying during the third and fourth weeks of the trip.


## How the extension works

The extension of a visa on arrival has two parts: an online application with payment, followed by a visit in person. Indonesia's immigration directorate announced that from 29 May 2025 foreigners extending a stay permit must have a photograph taken and attend an interview at an immigration office, after registering and uploading their documents on the official e-visa website. The announcement says the procedure covers visa on arrival holders.


## Step one: apply and pay online

The application is made at the same official site that sells the e-VOA. According to the immigration directorate, a traveler who bought an e-VOA uses the "Extend My Visa" button on the home page and enters a passport number, nationality and date of birth. Payment is made on the site by Visa or Mastercard debit or credit card.


## If you bought the visa at the airport

A traveler whose visa was issued as a sticker at the airport counter uses a different entry point on the same site. The directorate's instructions are to open the "Services" menu and choose "Find Existing Stay Permit", then enter the passport number, nationality and date of birth. The visa record appears and can be used to apply for the extension.


## Step two: the immigration office

After the online step, the applicant goes to an immigration office for the photograph and interview. The directorate describes verification as being done by the office for the place where the foreigner is staying. Its announcement asks applicants to give truthful information in the interview. Bring your passport, and bring your onward ticket, since the visa rules expect a visitor to hold an outbound ticket from Indonesia.



## When to apply for the extension

Apply for the extension before the first 30 days end, and leave room for the office visit. Indonesia's immigration directorate says a foreigner is not charged for overstaying if the extension was applied for and paid before the visa expired. We have not found an official figure for how long processing takes, so we are not going to quote one.


## Our advice on timing

Our advice is to file the online application about two weeks before the 30 days end. That is our own margin and not an official deadline. It leaves time to get an appointment or a walk-in visit, to return if a document is missing, and to change your plans if the answer is no.


## Count the days correctly

Read the expiry date on your visa or in the e-visa record instead of counting from memory. Thirty days is shorter than most calendar months, so a trip planned as "a month" can run past the visa by a day or two.


## What the extension costs

The official fee for a 30-day stay permit, which is what the extension of a visa on arrival is, is IDR 500,000 per application. That is the same amount as the visa itself, at IDR 500,000. The fee is set in rupiah, so the dollar amount on your card statement depends on the exchange rate on the day.


## Agents charge more

A visa agent who handles the paperwork charges a fee on top of the official price. An agent cannot remove the in-person step, because the photograph and interview are yours to attend. Whether the convenience is worth the price is your decision; what the government charges is the figure above.


## What happens if you overstay

Staying past the date on a visa on arrival without an extension in place is an overstay, and the State Department warns that it can bring a fine of IDR 1,000,000 per day, along with possible detention, deportation or a ban on returning. The fine for a single day is twice the cost of the extension itself.


## The alternative to extending

A traveler who does not want to spend a day on the extension has one clean alternative, which is to leave Indonesia before day 30. Whether a new visa on re-entry suits your trip depends on your route and your flights, and each entry means a new visa fee, a new arrival card and the same passport rules.


## Keep the same passport

You must leave Indonesia on the passport you entered with. If your passport is replaced during the trip, the State Department says you need a special pass from immigration before you depart. On a long stay, check that the passport will still meet the entry rules for any country you visit next.



## FAQ

Can I extend a Bali visa on arrival online?The application and payment are made online at the official e-visa site. Since 29 May 2025 the applicant must also go to an immigration office for a photograph and an interview.

How many times can a visa on arrival be extended?Once, for another 30 days. The longest stay on this visa is 60 days.

How much does it cost to extend a visa on arrival in Bali?The official fee for a 30-day stay permit is IDR 500,000 per application. Agents add their own fee on top.

Can I extend a visa bought at the airport counter?Yes. Indonesia's immigration directorate says both the e-VOA and the airport sticker visa can be extended through the official site.



===== passport-validity-rules-for-indonesia [visas-and-documents / None] 1204w =====
Passport Validity and Blank-Page Rules for Indonesia
To enter Bali, a US passport must be valid for at least 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages. Both conditions are checked, and a traveler who does not meet them is refused entry. Check the expiry date and count the empty pages before you book flights, because renewing a passport takes longer than buying a ticket.


## The six-month passport rule

Indonesia requires a visitor's passport to be valid for at least six months beyond the date of arrival. The State Department lists the requirement on its Indonesia page, and the US Consular Agency in Bali repeats it for all visitors to the island. A passport that is still valid on the day you land, but expires four months later, does not meet the rule.


## Count from the day you land

The six months run from your arrival in Indonesia, so the date that matters is the one you land in Bali. A flight from the United States crosses the date line and lands on a later calendar date than it departs. Take your arrival date, add six months, and compare the result with the expiry date printed on your passport's photo page.


## Why a close call is a bad idea

The US Consular Agency in Bali states the consequence plainly: Balinese immigration authorities will not allow you to enter the country if you do not meet the requirement. There is no fee that fixes a short passport at the border. If your passport is within a year of expiring, our advice is to renew it before you plan the trip.


## The two blank pages rule

Indonesia also requires two blank pages in the passport, and the US Consular Agency in Bali adds a detail many travelers miss: the endorsement page at the back of a US passport does not count as a blank page. The two pages must be visa pages with nothing stamped or stuck on them.


## How to count your pages

Open the passport and look for pages headed "Visas" that are completely empty. Pages headed "Endorsements" do not count toward the two. A page with a single small stamp in one corner is no longer blank. If you have traveled a great deal on this passport, do the count now instead of at the check-in desk.


## If you are short of pages

A passport that has run out of visa pages has to be replaced, and that is done through the State Department's passport renewal process. Processing times change through the year, so check the State Department's passport pages for the current estimate and the expedited options before you set your travel dates.



## What immigration asks to see on arrival

Indonesia's official visa finder lists the documents a US tourist presents to the immigration officer on arrival: a passport valid for at least six months and an outbound ticket from Indonesia. The passport rule and the ticket rule sit side by side in the same list, so prepare both.


## The onward ticket

The outbound ticket is a ticket that leaves Indonesia, to any country. It should fall inside the stay your visa allows, which for the tourist visa is 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days. Keep the booking confirmation where you can show it without a data connection, such as a screenshot or a printout.


## The arrival card uses the same passport

The arrival card that every traveler submits in the last 72 hours before landing asks for your passport details. Enter the number of the passport you will travel on. If you renew your passport after buying an e-VOA, the visa was issued against the old passport number, so renew first and buy the visa second.


## Emergency and limited-validity passports

A US emergency passport does not work with the visa on arrival. The US Consular Agency in Bali says a traveler on a limited-validity passport needs a visa obtained before arrival, which means applying at an Indonesian embassy or consulate before the trip. Anyone issued an emergency passport shortly before departure should deal with this before flying.


## If your passport is lost in Bali

A passport lost or stolen during the trip is replaced through the US Consular Agency in Bali, which can be reached at +62-361-958-6161. A replacement creates a second problem, which is that your entry was recorded against the old passport. That is covered by the next rule.


## Leave on the passport you arrived with

A traveler must leave Indonesia with the same passport used to enter. The State Department says that if your passport is replaced during the trip, you must obtain a special pass from Indonesian immigration authorities before leaving. A new passport alone is not enough to board your flight home, so allow time for that step.


## Dual nationals

The same-passport rule matters most to travelers who hold two passports. Enter and leave Indonesia on one of them, and buy the visa and fill in the arrival card with that passport's details. Mixing passports between the visa, the arrival card and the border is how records stop matching.


## Keep a copy

Our advice is to keep a photo of your passport's photo page and of your visa on your phone, and a paper copy in a different bag from the passport. A copy is not a travel document, but it makes a report and a replacement application quicker to fill in.


## A passport check to do before booking

A passport check for Bali takes four steps, and it belongs before the flight purchase. Find the expiry date; work out your arrival date and add six months; count the empty visa pages, leaving out the endorsement pages; and confirm the passport is a full-validity one. If any step fails, renew first.


## Check every traveler

Each person in the group needs a passport that passes the same check, including children. A child's US passport is issued for a shorter period than an adult's, so a passport that felt new can be close to the six-month line. Look at every expiry date, not only your own.


## Your connection country has rules too

There is no nonstop flight from the United States to Bali, so the trip passes through at least one other country. If you plan to leave the airport during a layover, that country's entry rules apply to your passport as well. Check them on the State Department's page for that country.



## FAQ

How long does my passport need to be valid for Bali?At least six months beyond your arrival date, according to the State Department and the US Consular Agency in Bali.

How many blank passport pages do I need for Indonesia?Two. The US Consular Agency in Bali notes that the endorsement page at the back of the passport does not count as a blank page.

Can I enter Bali on an emergency passport?Not with a visa on arrival. A traveler on a limited-validity passport needs a visa obtained before arriving in Indonesia.

What if my passport is replaced while I am in Bali?The State Department says you must obtain a special pass from Indonesian immigration before leaving, because you must exit on the passport you entered with.



===== indonesia-drug-laws-for-travelers [health-and-safety / None] 1091w =====
Indonesia's Drug Laws: What Americans Must Know Before Bali
Indonesia's drug laws are among the strictest an American traveler will meet, and they apply in Bali exactly as they do in the rest of the country. Marijuana, cannabis and CBD products are illegal, and the State Department adds that some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia. It warns that convictions can bring heavy fines, long jail sentences and, in extreme cases, the death penalty.


## What is illegal in Indonesia

Indonesia bans the drugs an American would expect and several products that are legal at home. The State Department lists marijuana, cannabis and CBD products as illegal in Indonesia, with no exception for medical use or for a product bought legally in a US state. Indonesia's Narcotics Law places the cannabis plant, all of its parts and its products in Class I, the most strictly controlled group.


## Cannabis in every form

The Narcotics Law's Class I list names the cannabis plant, every plant of the cannabis genus, and all parts of it, including seeds, resin and hashish. THC is listed separately in the same class. A vape cartridge, an edible or a pre-rolled joint bought legally in the United States falls inside that definition once it is in Indonesia.


## CBD is not an exception

CBD oil, gummies and creams are sold openly in much of the United States, which is why they end up in luggage by accident. The State Department names CBD products among the things that are illegal in Indonesia. Our advice is to go through your toiletry bag and carry-on before you pack and leave every CBD product at home.


## What the Narcotics Law provides

Indonesia's Narcotics Law sets prison terms that start high. For unlawful possession of a Class I narcotic the law provides 4 to 12 years in prison and a fine of between 800 million and 8 billion rupiah. Those are the ranges written in the statute; what a court does in a given case is for the court.


## Bringing drugs into the country

Importing a Class I narcotic is a more serious offense than possessing one. The law provides 5 to 15 years in prison for importing, and where the amount is more than 1 kilogram of plant material, or more than 5 grams in other forms, the penalty can be death or life imprisonment. Carrying something through an airport is importing it.


## Personal use

The law treats personal use separately, and it is still a crime. A person who uses a Class I narcotic can be sentenced to up to 4 years in prison. The same article allows a court to order medical and social rehabilitation for someone shown to be a victim of drug abuse, but that is a decision made inside a criminal case.



## Prescription medication is a separate risk

Prescription medication is where a careful traveler can still go wrong in Indonesia. The State Department says some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal there, and that the Indonesian customs office does not share a list of prohibited drugs. A US prescription does not make a banned substance legal in another country.


## What the medicine rules allow

Indonesia's food and drug agency sets limits by class of medicine. A traveler may bring 90 days' use for prescribed medicine, backed by a doctor's prescription, and a smaller supply of psychotropic medicines. Narcotic-class medicines may not be brought in at all. Medicine that does not comply can be seized and destroyed.


## Check your own prescription

Because there is no published list, the work falls to you. Find out which class your medication belongs to, carry it in its original packaging with the prescription, and ask your doctor about an alternative if it is a stimulant or a strong painkiller. The full rules by class are in our guide to bringing medication into Bali.


## How enforcement reaches tourists

Drug enforcement in Bali is active, and visitors are not outside it. The US Consular Agency in Bali reports a current enforcement campaign by Indonesian police, immigration, transport and tourism officials, with increased enforcement against illegal drug possession among other offenses. An American arrested in Indonesia is subject to Indonesian law and Indonesian courts.


## What the consulate can and cannot do

The US Consular Agency in Bali can be reached at +62-361-958-6161. A consular agency is not a court and has no power over an Indonesian criminal case. Our advice, if you are arrested, is to ask at once for the US Consular Agency to be notified.


## Do not carry anything for anyone

Under the Narcotics Law, importing is the offense with the heaviest penalties, and the person carrying the bag is the person who imported it. Pack your own luggage, keep it with you, and do not carry a package, a gift or a bag for someone else through an airport.


## Offers on the street

A visitor to Bali's nightlife areas may be offered drugs. The possession article of the Narcotics Law is written for anyone, visitors included, and the minimum sentence it provides for possessing a Class I narcotic is four years. Our advice is the obvious one: say no and keep walking.


## Drinks are a different danger

The State Department also warns of drink spiking in Bali, Lombok and the Gili Islands. Its advice is to make sure drinks are prepared in your sight, to accept none from strangers, and to leave none unattended. A spiked drink is a safety problem first, and it is a reason to stay with people you trust on a night out.


## Alcohol is legal

Alcohol is legal in Bali for adults, and none of the above applies to a beer at dinner. The legal line an American needs to remember is simple: alcohol is permitted; cannabis, CBD and every recreational drug are not; and prescription medicine needs checking before the trip.



## FAQ

Is weed legal in Bali?No. Marijuana and cannabis are illegal throughout Indonesia, including Bali, and the Narcotics Law puts cannabis in its most strictly controlled class.

Can I bring CBD oil to Bali?No. The State Department lists CBD products among the things that are illegal in Indonesia.

Can I bring ADHD medication to Bali?The State Department says some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia. Check your specific medication before you travel and ask your doctor about alternatives.

Does Indonesia have the death penalty for drugs?Yes. The State Department says the death penalty may apply in extreme cases, and the Narcotics Law provides for it in serious import cases.



===== arak-and-methanol-in-bali [health-and-safety / health-chain] 1132w =====
Arak and Methanol in Bali: How to Drink Safely
The danger with arak in Bali is methanol, a toxic alcohol that can end up in badly made or adulterated spirits. The State Department warns of drink poisoning in Indonesia, including alcohol mixed with methanol. You cannot see, smell or taste the difference in a mixed drink, so the protection is in what you choose to drink and where: sealed, branded drinks opened in front of you.


## What arak is, and where methanol comes in

Arak is a spirit distilled in Bali, and the risk attached to it comes from how a batch is made and what is added to it. The CDC explains that methanol is a toxic byproduct of alcohol production: commercial producers remove it, but with improper distillation it can remain in the final product or become more concentrated.


## Adulteration is the second route

The CDC also notes that methanol is sometimes used to adulterate beverages in place of ethanol, the alcohol people intend to drink. That means the problem is not limited to one traditional spirit. Our view is that any unbranded spirit, and any mixed drink made from one, deserves the same caution.


## Why you cannot tell

Methanol is a colorless liquid, and according to the CDC its odor does not give sufficient warning. In a cocktail with juice, syrup and ice there is nothing for a drinker to notice. We have found no official source that offers a reliable way to judge a poured drink by its look, taste or price, and we are not going to suggest one.


## What the official warnings say about Bali

The State Department's warning on drinks in Indonesia covers two separate dangers. One is drink poisoning, such as alcohol mixed with methanol or other deadly chemicals. The other is drink spiking, which it says has been reported in Bali, Lombok and the Gili Islands along with sexual assault. Both are reasons to control what goes into your glass.


## The State Department's three rules

The State Department's advice is short. Make sure drinks are prepared in your sight. Do not accept drinks from strangers. Do not leave a drink unattended. These rules cost nothing and they cover both dangers at once.


## The CDC's advice

The CDC's guidance for travelers on alcohol abroad is to select only reputable brands and to drink in moderation. A sealed bottle or can of a brand you recognize, opened in front of you, is the practical form of that advice in a bar.



## How to drink more safely in Bali

Drinking more safely in Bali comes down to choosing drinks whose contents you can verify. Bottled beer, canned drinks and wine poured from a bottle you watched being opened leave little room for substitution. Spirits are where the risk sits, and the risk grows when the spirit comes from an unlabeled container or arrives already mixed.


## Our practical rules

These are our own suggestions, built on the official advice above.


- Order sealed beer, cider or canned drinks when you are unsure of a venue.

- If you want a spirit, ask to see the bottle and watch the drink being poured.

- Skip free shots, drinks handed to you, and spirits served from unmarked bottles or jugs.

- Buy bottles to take home only with an intact seal and a proper label.

- Keep your drink in your hand, and get a new one if you put it down.



## If you want to try arak

Arak is part of Balinese culture, and wanting to taste it is reasonable. Our advice is to try it from a sealed, labeled bottle, not from a jug or a refilled water bottle. If you cannot see the bottle it came from, choose something else.


## Bring your own allowance

A traveler aged 21 or over may bring 1 liter of alcohol into Indonesia. A bottle of your usual spirit from duty free gives you a known supply for drinks at a villa. Stay inside the limit, because any excess is destroyed on the spot by the customs officer.


## Symptoms of methanol poisoning

Methanol poisoning is hard to recognize early because it starts like being drunk or hungover. The CDC lists dizziness, headache and nausea among the effects, followed in serious cases by permanent loss of vision, organ failure and death. The CDC also says the effects may not become apparent until 1 to 72 hours after exposure.


## The warning signs

According to the CDC, early effects include drowsiness, confusion, headache and dizziness, and problems with sight are a hallmark: blurred vision, sensitivity to light, and partial or total loss of vision. Our advice is to treat a hangover far worse than the amount you drank, or any change in vision after drinking, as an emergency.


## The delay is the trap

Because symptoms can take a day or more to appear, a person may feel fine at the end of the night and become ill the following afternoon. If several people who shared the same drinks all feel unusually sick, that is a further reason to act quickly.


## What to do if you suspect methanol poisoning

Suspected methanol poisoning needs a hospital, and it needs one quickly. The CDC states that effective antidotes exist and should be given as soon as possible once the patient is in a medical facility. Do not wait to see whether the symptoms pass, and do not try to treat it yourself with more alcohol.


## Who to call

In an emergency in Indonesia, call 112 for the unified emergency line, 110 for police, 119 for an ambulance and 113 for fire. Tell the hospital staff that you suspect methanol and what you drank, where and when. The US Consular Agency in Bali can be reached at +62-361-958-6161 and can help an American patient contact family.


## Tell the people you were with

Anyone who drank from the same bottle or the same round is at the same risk, whether or not they feel ill yet. Contact them, and tell the venue or your accommodation. Keep the bottle if you have it.



## FAQ

Is it safe to drink arak in Bali?The risk is methanol in badly made or adulterated spirits. Our advice is to drink arak only from a sealed, labeled bottle, and to choose something else when you cannot see the bottle.

Can you taste methanol in a drink?No reliable way exists. Methanol is a colorless liquid, and the CDC says its odor does not give sufficient warning.

How soon do methanol poisoning symptoms start?The CDC says effects may not become apparent until 1 to 72 hours after exposure.

What should I do if I think I have methanol poisoning in Bali?Go to a hospital immediately and say you suspect methanol. The CDC states that effective antidotes exist and should be given as soon as possible.



===== bali-temple-dress-code-and-etiquette [culture-and-etiquette / None] 1072w =====
What to Wear in Bali: Temple Dress Code and Etiquette
Bali asks visitors to dress modestly and behave respectfully, and at temples the rule is written down. The island's rulebook for foreign tourists, Governor's Circular No. 7 of 2025, requires modest dress in sacred areas and limits who may enter the inner parts of a temple. Pack one outfit that covers shoulders and knees, and you are ready for most of it.


## What the rules say about dress

Bali's circular for foreign tourists requires modest dress and polite conduct, and it does not limit that to temples. The circular names sacred areas, tourist sites, restaurants, shops and the road as places where visitors are expected to dress and behave appropriately. Our reading is that swimwear belongs at the beach and the pool.


## What modest means in practice

The circular does not publish a list of garments, so what follows is our own reading. Shoulders covered, knees covered and no bare midriff is a standard that will not be questioned at a temple or a village ceremony. A light long-sleeved shirt and loose trousers or a long skirt also happen to be comfortable in the heat.


## On the street and in shops

The same circular asks for appropriate dress in restaurants, shops and on the road. Our advice is simple: put a shirt on when you leave the beach, and wear one on a scooter. Walking through a town in a bikini top or bare-chested is the behavior the rule was written about.


## Who may enter a temple, and how far

Bali's circular restricts how far a foreign tourist may go inside a temple. Tourists may not enter the inner and middle courtyards of a temple or other sanctified place unless they are there to pray and are wearing Balinese traditional dress.


## Balinese traditional dress

The circular's wording is "Balinese traditional dress", and it does not define the items. Our understanding is that for a visitor this means a sarong wrapped around the waist with a sash tied over it, for men and women alike. Follow whatever the temple's own staff ask of you at the entrance.


## The menstruation rule

The circular also states that a person entering those inner areas to pray must not be menstruating. We report the rule as it is written in the circular.



## What not to do at a sacred site

Bali's circular lists specific acts that are prohibited at sacred sites. Climbing sacred trees is banned. So is climbing on sacred structures, and so is posing indecently or unclothed at a sacred place. The circular says violations are to be dealt with firmly under Indonesian law.


## Photos

The circular's ban is on indecent and unclothed posing and on climbing structures for a picture. Our advice goes a little further: do not stand on walls, shrines or statues for a better angle, do not step over offerings to get a shot, and ask before photographing people who are praying.


## Offerings on the ground

Small woven trays of flowers and rice are placed on sidewalks, doorsteps and steps every day. They are offerings, and they are sacred while they are fresh. We have no rule to cite for this one; it is simple courtesy to walk around them instead of over them.


## Conduct the rules ask for everywhere

Bali's circular goes beyond clothing. Foreign tourists are prohibited from using abusive language, behaving rudely, causing disturbances or acting aggressively toward officials, local people or other tourists. The same text prohibits littering and the use of single-use plastics such as plastic bags, styrofoam, plastic straws and plastic-packaged drinks.


## What to carry

The plastics rule has a practical side. Our advice is to carry a refillable bottle and a cloth bag, which keeps you inside the rule without thinking about it. A light scarf or sarong in your day bag covers shoulders or knees whenever a visit turns out to include a temple.


## Ceremonies you come across

You will meet ceremonies on the road, on the beach and in villages. If a procession is crossing, wait for it. If you are invited to watch, stay at the back and dress as you would for a temple. These are our suggestions; the circular's requirement is respectful conduct.


## What happens if you ignore the rules

Bali's circular says that violations by foreign tourists will be dealt with firmly in line with Indonesian law, and it asks residents to report them to a provincial hotline. The US Consular Agency in Bali separately reports a current enforcement campaign by Indonesian authorities. A disrespectful photo is no longer treated as a private matter.


## The levy is part of the same rulebook

The same set of rules includes Bali's tourist levy, which is IDR 150,000 per person.


## A packing list for temples and ceremonies

A packing list for Bali's temples is short, and every item on it is useful elsewhere on the trip. These are our own suggestions, built on the circular's requirement of modest dress in sacred places and on its ban on single-use plastics.


- A shirt or top that covers the shoulders.

- Trousers, a long skirt or a dress that covers the knees.

- A light scarf that can cover shoulders at short notice.

- Sandals that slip on and off easily.

- A cloth bag and a refillable water bottle.



## Buying a sarong

A sarong bought on your first day will be used at every temple afterward and as a beach wrap or a blanket on a cold flight home. If you would prefer to own one, markets across the island sell them, and you can ask the seller to show you how the sash is tied.



## FAQ

What should I wear to a temple in Bali?Modest clothing that covers shoulders and knees, with a sarong and sash where the temple asks for them. Bali's rules for foreign tourists require modest dress in sacred areas.

Can tourists go inside Balinese temples?Tourists may not enter the inner and middle courtyards unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress. The circular does not restrict the outer areas in the same way.

Can I wear shorts in Bali?Yes, at the beach and around your hotel. Bali's rules ask for appropriate dress at sacred sites, tourist sites, restaurants and shops, so cover up for temples and towns.

Is there a rule about menstruation at Balinese temples?Yes. The governor's circular says a person entering the inner temple areas to pray must not be menstruating.



===== galungan-and-kuningan-explained [when-to-go / holiday-collision] 1036w =====
Galungan and Kuningan Explained for Visitors
Galungan is the Balinese festival marking the victory of good over evil, and Kuningan closes it ten days later. The pair comes around every 210 days, so it falls twice in most calendar years and its dates move. In 2027 Galungan is on January 13 and again on August 11. Neither day is a national holiday, and neither carries the restrictions that Nyepi does.


## What Galungan celebrates

Galungan marks the victory of dharma over adharma, which is usually put in English as good over evil. Balinese families pray at home and at their temples, and the belief is that ancestral spirits visit during the festival period. It is a family occasion first, held in household compounds and village temples all over the island.


## The penjor

The sign of Galungan that every visitor notices is the penjor, a tall curved bamboo pole decorated with produce and set up outside homes. A penjor stands as a symbol of gratitude. Set up outside each home for the festival, they are its most visible sign.


## Kuningan

Kuningan falls ten days after Galungan and closes the festival. In 2027 it is on Saturday, January 23, and on Saturday, August 21. Families make offerings again on Kuningan, and the ten days between the two dates are part of one festive period.


## When Galungan and Kuningan fall

Galungan follows the Balinese Pawukon calendar, which has a cycle of 210 days, so the festival does not sit on a fixed date in the Western calendar. It comes about every seven months. That means two Galungans in most years, and a festival that lands in a different month from one year to the next.


## The 2027 dates

In 2027 Galungan falls on Wednesday, January 13, with Kuningan on Saturday, January 23. The second pair of the year is Wednesday, August 11, and Saturday, August 21. We took these dates from published Balinese calendar listings for 2027, which agree with each other; check a current calendar for any later year.


## The day before

The day before Galungan is called Penampahan, and it is the day of preparation, when families cook and finish the offerings and decorations. For a visitor, the day before and the day itself are the two days when the festival is most visible.



## Is Bali open during Galungan?

Galungan and Kuningan are not on Indonesia's list of national holidays, and we have found no official restriction on visitors attached to either day. The measures documented for Nyepi, the day of silence, belong to Nyepi alone. What changes during Galungan is that Balinese families are at home or at the temple, so individual businesses may keep different hours.


## Do not confuse it with Nyepi

Nyepi is the one day of the Balinese year that stops the island, and it is a separate festival on a different calendar. In 2027 Nyepi is on Monday, March 8, 2027. The restrictions documented for Nyepi do not apply to Galungan.


## What we cannot tell you

We have not found an official source on which businesses close for Galungan or how traffic changes, so we will not describe either as fact. If a particular restaurant, driver or tour matters to your plans on January 13 or August 11, 2027, ask them directly whether they are working that day.


## How to be a good guest during Galungan

A visitor in Bali during Galungan is welcome to watch, and the island's rules for tourists set the limits. Bali's circular for foreign visitors requires modest dress and polite conduct in sacred areas, and it bars tourists from the inner and middle courtyards of a temple unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress.


## Dress for it

Our advice is to dress on Galungan as you would for a temple visit, with shoulders and knees covered, even if you are only walking through a village. You may pass family ceremonies along the way. A sarong and a sash in your bag let you accept an invitation if one comes.


## On the road

If you meet a procession on the road, our advice is to treat it as having the right of way. Wait for it to pass, keep the engine noise down, and do not push through on a scooter. This is our advice, not a published rule, and it is the difference between watching the festival and being in its way.


## Photographs

The penjor and the decorated streets are yours to photograph. People at prayer are a different matter. Ask first, keep your distance, and never climb on a shrine or a wall for a better view. Bali's rules prohibit climbing on sacred structures and indecent posing at sacred sites.


## Planning a trip around Galungan

Galungan is a good reason to choose one week over another, since the penjor are up along the roads and no official restriction on visitors applies. The planning question is a small one: which of your days need a specific person, such as a driver or a guide, and whether that person is working.


## Our advice on booking

Book drivers and guided activities for the Galungan and Kuningan dates ahead of time and confirm them a few days before. Keep the day of Galungan itself light: a morning in a village or in Ubud's back streets looking at the penjor is a better use of it than a long itinerary.


## The wider calendar

Galungan is one of several dates that move through the Balinese and Indonesian calendars every year, and some of the others do close things. How those dates fall against a trip is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is Galungan in Bali?Galungan is a Balinese Hindu festival marking the victory of dharma over adharma, or good over evil. It comes every 210 days, and Kuningan follows ten days later.

When is Galungan in 2027?Wednesday, January 13, and Wednesday, August 11. Kuningan follows on January 23 and August 21.

Is everything closed in Bali during Galungan?Galungan is not a national holiday, and we have found no official restriction on visitors for it. Ask individual businesses whether they are working that day.

What are the bamboo poles in Bali?They are penjor, curved bamboo poles decorated with produce and set up outside homes for Galungan as a symbol of gratitude.



===== bali-public-holidays-what-closes [when-to-go / holiday-collision] 1140w =====
Public Holidays in Bali: What Closes and What Gets Crowded
Nyepi, the Balinese day of silence, is the one public holiday that comes with island-wide restrictions on visitors, and in 2027 it falls on Monday, March 8, 2027. We have found no such restrictions for Indonesia's other national holidays, though several of them create long weekends. The dates change every year and are fixed by a government decree.


## How Indonesia's public holidays are set

Indonesia's public holidays are fixed each year by a joint decree of three ministers, published the year before. The decree lists the national holidays and a second group of days called collective leave, which bridge a holiday to the nearest weekend. The list for 2027 was signed in September 2026, so the dates for any trip can be checked well ahead.


## National holidays and collective leave

A national holiday is a day off for the country. A collective leave day is an extra day attached to a holiday. For a visitor, the two together show when Indonesians have a long stretch of days off, and those are the periods to look at when you choose travel dates.


## Many faiths, one calendar

Indonesia's holiday list covers several religions, which is why it contains Islamic, Christian, Hindu, Buddhist and Chinese festivals alongside the civic ones. Several of them follow lunar calendars, so they land on different Western dates each year. A list from last year's trip report is no guide to this year's.


## The 2027 public holidays

Indonesia's national holidays for 2027, as fixed by the joint decree, run through the whole year. The dates most likely to touch a Bali trip are listed here, with the collective leave days that extend them. Check the current decree for any other year.


- January 1: New Year's Day.

- February 6: Chinese New Year, with collective leave on February 5.

- March 8: Nyepi.

- March 10 and 11: Eid al-Fitr, with collective leave on March 9, 12 and 15.

- March 26: Good Friday, with collective leave on March 25.

- May 1: Labor Day. May 6: Ascension Day.

- May 17: Eid al-Adha, with collective leave on May 18.

- May 20: Vesak, with collective leave on May 19.

- June 1: Pancasila Day. June 6: Islamic New Year.

- August 17: Independence Day.

- December 25: Christmas, with collective leave on December 24.



## The March 2027 cluster

March 2027 is unusual. Nyepi on March 8 is followed directly by Eid al-Fitr on March 10 and 11, with collective leave on March 9, 12 and 15. Counting the weekends, the holidays and leave days run without a working day from Saturday, March 6, to Monday, March 15, and Bali's day of silence falls inside that stretch.



## Nyepi is the day with restrictions

Nyepi is different in kind from every other holiday on the list. On Nyepi, mobile data and television and radio broadcasts in Bali are switched off for 24 hours, from 6 a.m. to 6 a.m., under a ministerial circular issued each year. Press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe all regular commercial flights halting for the same 24 hours.


## What it means for a trip

According to press reports of the joint appeal issued by Bali's authorities each year, residents and tourists alike stay inside their accommodation for the day. On that evidence, plan on being unable to fly in, fly out or go out on Nyepi, and confirm your own flight with your airline if your dates fall near it, and read our Nyepi guide before you book.


## The date moves

Nyepi follows the Balinese Saka calendar. It fell on March 19 in 2026 and falls on March 8 in 2027. Any trip in March needs its dates checked against that year's Nyepi before the flights are bought.


## What the other holidays change

Indonesia's other national holidays are fixed by the same decree, and the decree does nothing more than set days off. Nyepi's restrictions come from separate measures specific to that day, and we have found none for the other holidays. What a long weekend changes is how many people are traveling at the same time as you, because Bali is a holiday destination for Indonesians too.


## What we cannot tell you

We have not found an official source that says which shops, attractions or offices in Bali close on which holiday, or by how much prices and traffic rise, so we are not going to state any of it as fact. A government office is the safest thing to assume closed on a national holiday. For anything else that matters to your plans, ask the business.


## Immigration and other offices

One practical consequence follows from the calendar itself. A visa extension now requires a photograph and an interview at an immigration office, and an office visit cannot happen on a day the office is shut. If your first 30 days end near a cluster of holidays, start the extension earlier.


## Balinese festivals that are not national holidays

Bali's own religious calendar adds festival days that do not appear in the national decree. The best known are Galungan and Kuningan, which come every 210 days. In 2027 Galungan falls on January 13 and August 11, with Kuningan ten days after each. Those days decorate the island; they do not close it.


## Ceremonies happen every week

Temple anniversaries, cremations and village ceremonies take place throughout the year on dates set locally. No national list covers them. A road closed for a procession is part of travel in Bali, and the practical answer is a schedule with room in it.


## How to check your own dates

Checking a Bali trip against the holiday calendar takes a few minutes and is worth doing before you buy flights. Look up that year's Nyepi date first, because it is the only one that can stop an arrival or a departure. Then look at the national list for long weekends, and at the Galungan dates if you would like to see the festival.


## Our advice on choosing dates

Our advice is to avoid arriving or leaving within a day of Nyepi unless you are coming for it on purpose, and to book accommodation early for any stay that overlaps a long Indonesian weekend. How these dates sit against the rest of a trip is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is Bali closed on public holidays?Nyepi, the day of silence, is the one holiday with island-wide restrictions. We have found none for the other national holidays, though government offices should be assumed closed.

When is Nyepi in 2027?Monday, March 8, 2027. The date follows the Balinese calendar and changes every year.

Who decides Indonesia's public holidays?A joint decree of three ministers fixes the national holidays and collective leave days for each year. The 2027 list was signed in September 2026.

Is Galungan a public holiday in Bali?Galungan is not on Indonesia's list of national holidays. It is a Balinese festival that comes every 210 days.



===== can-you-drink-the-water-in-bali [health-and-safety / None] 1076w =====
Can You Drink the Tap Water in Bali?
No. Do not drink the tap water in Bali. The State Department says that in many areas of Indonesia tap water is not safe to drink, and it advises bottled water from a major brand with the seal intact. The same caution applies to ice and to anything rinsed in tap water. Sealed bottled water is sold everywhere on the island, so the rule is easy to follow.


## What the official advice says

The State Department's guidance on water in Indonesia is direct: in many areas, tap water is not safe to drink. It adds that bottled water and beverages are generally safe, and that travelers should use major brands and make sure the seal is intact. Impure water, it notes, can cause diseases such as typhoid, cholera, hepatitis and dysentery.


## Restaurants and hotels

The State Department also warns that many restaurants and hotels in Indonesia serve tap water unless bottled water is specifically requested. The practical step is to ask for bottled water by name and to check that the bottle arrives sealed. A carafe or a glass already poured tells you nothing about where the water came from.


## The CDC's version

The CDC's advice for destinations where tap water may be contaminated is the same in substance: do not drink the tap water, and choose drinks from unopened, factory-sealed bottles or cans. Hot coffee or tea should be safe if it is served steaming hot.


## Ice, smoothies and brushing your teeth

Tap water in Bali reaches a traveler in more ways than a glass. The State Department suggests asking for no ice in drinks, since ice may be made using tap water, and the CDC advises against ice in destinations where drinking water is a concern. The same thinking applies to anything blended, rinsed or diluted with water you did not see.


## Ice

We have no official source describing how ice is made at any particular bar or restaurant in Bali, so we will not tell you that a certain kind of ice is safe. The official position is the cautious one. If you want to follow it, order drinks without ice, or drink from a sealed, chilled bottle or can.


## Brushing your teeth

The CDC's advice is to brush your teeth with bottled or disinfected water. Keep a bottle by the sink so that it becomes a habit, and keep your mouth closed in the shower. This is the step people forget in the first two days, when jet lag has them on autopilot.


## Fountain drinks and juices

The CDC advises against fountain drinks, since the water most likely comes from the tap. A fresh juice or smoothie may be made with water or ice as well as fruit. Our advice is to ask what goes into it, or to choose a sealed drink when you are unsure.



## Food washed in tap water

Tap water also matters on the plate. The CDC's guidance for travelers is to avoid raw foods and fresh salads, which may have been washed in contaminated water, and to choose food that is cooked and served hot. Fruit you peel yourself is a different case from fruit that was cut and rinsed in a kitchen.


## Street food

The CDC's advice is to avoid food from street vendors, and it adds that if you choose to eat street food, the same food safety rules apply. In practice that means food cooked in front of you and served hot. Whether to eat at a street stall is your decision; the rule to apply is the same either way.


## How strict to be

These are cautious rules, and plenty of travelers relax them. We are not going to tell you which ones are safe to drop, because we have no source that says so. Our advice is to hold to them most firmly in the first days of the trip and before any day you cannot afford to lose, such as a flight or a boat crossing.


## Making water safe yourself

Tap water can be made safe when bottled water is not at hand. The State Department lists filtering, boiling, disinfecting and distilling as the methods, and the CDC likewise says tap water can be disinfected by boiling, filtering or chemically treating it. A kettle in a hotel room is the simplest of these.


## Refill stations and filtered water

Bali's rules for visitors prohibit single-use plastics, including plastic-packaged drinks, which sits awkwardly beside advice to drink bottled water. Some accommodation offers filtered water for refilling a bottle. We cannot vouch for any individual filter, so ask your accommodation how its drinking water is supplied and decide from there.


## A bottle with a filter

A refillable bottle with a built-in filter or purifier is one way to follow both the health advice and the plastics rule. Check the maker's claims about what the filter removes before you rely on it, since filters differ, and bring it from home.


## If you get sick anyway

A stomach illness is the usual worry after drinking contaminated water. Our advice is to drink plenty of safe fluids and rest. The State Department notes that medical care is not free in Indonesia and that hospitals and doctors often require payment or a sizable deposit before treatment, so know where your insurance details are.


## When to see a doctor

Our advice is to see a doctor if there is blood, a high fever, signs of dehydration, or no improvement after a couple of days, and sooner for a child. The CDC recommends hepatitis A, hepatitis B and typhoid vaccination for travel to Indonesia; hepatitis A and typhoid are both spread through contaminated food and water, so that conversation belongs before the trip.



## FAQ

Can you drink tap water in Bali?No. The State Department says tap water is not safe to drink in many areas of Indonesia and advises sealed bottled water from a major brand.

Is ice safe in Bali?The State Department suggests requesting no ice, since ice may be made using tap water. We have no official source on how any particular venue makes its ice.

Can I brush my teeth with tap water in Bali?The CDC's advice for destinations where tap water may be contaminated is to brush your teeth with bottled or disinfected water.

Is bottled water safe in Bali?The State Department says bottled water and beverages are generally safe, and advises using major brands and checking that the seal is intact.



===== dengue-and-mosquitoes-in-bali [health-and-safety / None] 1100w =====
Dengue and Mosquitoes in Bali
Dengue is the mosquito-borne illness a visitor to Bali should plan for, and there is no pill or routine vaccine for an American traveler to take against it. Protection is repellent, clothing and where you sleep. The CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands, so on a normal Bali trip the mosquito question is about dengue and Zika.


## What dengue is

Dengue is a viral illness spread to people mainly through the bite of an infected Aedes mosquito. The CDC lists dengue among the diseases a traveler to Indonesia can get from a mosquito bite, and its advice against it is the same two words it gives for every such disease: avoid bug bites.


## These mosquitoes bite in the daytime too

The CDC states that the mosquitoes that spread dengue bite during the day and at night. That is the detail that changes behavior. Repellent put on only at dusk leaves the whole day uncovered, including breakfast on a terrace and an afternoon in a garden cafe.


## No vaccine for most travelers

The CDC says no dengue vaccine is currently available in the United States. An American preparing for Bali cannot add dengue to the list of travel shots. The CDC also notes that many of the diseases spread by bugs in Indonesia cannot be prevented with a vaccine or medicine, which is why bite prevention carries the whole load.


## How to prevent mosquito bites in Bali

Preventing mosquito bites in Bali comes down to the steps the CDC lists for Indonesia. Cover exposed skin with long-sleeved shirts and long pants, use an appropriate insect repellent, and stay and sleep in air-conditioned or screened rooms. Where the place you sleep is open to the outdoors, the CDC says to use a bed net.


## Which repellent

The CDC names the active ingredients that work against mosquitoes: DEET, picaridin, oil of lemon eucalyptus or PMD, IR3535 and 2-undecanone. Higher percentages of the active ingredient give longer protection, and the CDC notes that a repellent with 20% or more DEET lasts up to several hours. Always use repellent as directed on the label.


## Clothing

The CDC also recommends clothing and gear treated with permethrin, and it warns never to put permethrin directly on skin. Loose, long, light clothing is comfortable in Bali's heat in the evening and does part of the job on its own. Ankles and feet under a dinner table are worth a second layer of repellent.


## Where you sleep

Many Bali villas and bungalows have open-air living rooms and bathrooms, which is part of their appeal. The CDC's advice points the other way: air-conditioned or screened rooms, or a bed net when the sleeping area is exposed. When you book, our advice is to check that the bedroom itself can be closed and cooled, or that the bed has a net.



## Symptoms of dengue

Dengue's most common symptom is a fever, together with aches and pains. The CDC describes eye pain, typically behind the eyes, along with muscle, joint or bone pain, and lists nausea, vomiting, rash and headache as further symptoms. The illness typically lasts 2 to 7 days, and most people recover in about a week.


## The warning signs

Severe dengue is a medical emergency. The CDC lists the warning signs: belly pain or tenderness, vomiting at least three times in 24 hours, bleeding from the nose or gums, vomiting blood or blood in the stool, and feeling extremely tired or restless. Anyone with one of these signs should go to a clinic or emergency room immediately.


## The danger comes after the fever
