The CDC says the warning signs usually begin in the 24 to 48 hours after the fever has gone away. Feeling better is therefore the wrong moment to stop paying attention. Someone who has had a fever in Bali should be watched for two days after it breaks.


## What to do if you get a fever in Bali

A fever in Bali is a reason to see a doctor, because a blood test is the only way to confirm dengue. The State Department says routine medical care is available in Indonesia's major cities, and that hospitals and doctors often require payment or a sizable deposit before service. Take your passport, your insurance details and a payment card.


## Getting there

The State Department notes that ambulance services in Indonesia are not widely available and can take 45 minutes or longer to arrive, and that travelers may prefer a taxi or private vehicle to the hospital. In an emergency the numbers are 112 for the unified emergency line, 110 for police, 119 for an ambulance and 113 for fire.


## After you fly home

The CDC advises travelers returning from an area with dengue to keep preventing mosquito bites for three weeks, even without symptoms, so the virus is not passed to mosquitoes at home. If you fall ill after the trip, tell your doctor where you traveled.


## Zika in Bali

Zika is spread by mosquito bites as well, and the CDC currently has a Level 2 travel health notice for Zika in Bali, based on cases in travelers returning from the island. The notice tells all travelers to Bali to prevent mosquito bites and sexual transmission of the virus during and after travel.


## Pregnancy

The CDC notes that a pregnant woman infected with Zika can pass it to her unborn baby. Anyone who is pregnant, or who may become pregnant around the time of the trip, should read the current CDC notice and talk to a doctor before booking. Health notices change, so check the date on the one you read.


## What a mosquito kit looks like

Our own packing suggestion follows from the CDC's list: a repellent with one of the named active ingredients, bought at home so you know what is in it; one long-sleeved shirt and one pair of long trousers per person; and a thermometer. Apply repellent after sunscreen, and reapply after swimming.



## FAQ

Is there dengue in Bali?The CDC lists dengue among the mosquito-borne diseases in Indonesia and advises travelers to avoid bug bites.

Is there malaria in Bali?The CDC reports no malaria transmission in the resort areas of Bali, in Ubud, or in the Gili Islands.

Is there a dengue vaccine for travelers?The CDC says no dengue vaccine is currently available in the United States. Prevention means avoiding mosquito bites.

What mosquito repellent works in Bali?The CDC names DEET, picaridin, oil of lemon eucalyptus or PMD, IR3535 and 2-undecanone as effective active ingredients.



===== bali-belly [health-and-safety / health-chain] 1192w =====
Bali Belly: Prevention, Treatment and When to See a Doctor
Bali belly is travelers' diarrhea, and the CDC calls it the most common travel-related illness. In an otherwise healthy adult it is rarely serious, but it can take days out of a trip. Prevention is about what you eat and drink, treatment is mostly fluids, and a doctor is needed when there is blood, fever, dehydration or no improvement.


## What Bali belly is

Bali belly is the local name for travelers' diarrhea, a stomach illness picked up from contaminated food or water. The CDC lists Asia, with the exceptions of Japan and South Korea, among the highest-risk regions for it. The CDC also says that in otherwise healthy adults diarrhea is rarely serious or life-threatening, though it can make a trip very unpleasant.


## Where it comes from

The source is contaminated food or drink. The State Department says that in many areas of Indonesia tap water is not safe to drink, and that impure water can cause diseases including typhoid, hepatitis and dysentery. Water reaches a traveler through ice and washed salads as well as through a tap.


## Why we give no odds

Travel sites often quote a percentage of visitors who get sick in Bali. We have found no official figure for Bali, so we are not going to print one. The honest summary is the CDC's: travelers' diarrhea is common in this part of the world, and the precautions below lower the risk without removing it.


## How to prevent Bali belly

Preventing Bali belly comes down to a short list of habits from the CDC. Eat only food that is cooked and served hot. Avoid food that has been sitting on a buffet. Drink only beverages from factory-sealed containers, avoid ice because it may have been made from unclean water, and wash your hands often with soap and water.


## Water and ice

The State Department's advice for Indonesia is to drink bottled water from a major brand with the seal intact, and it notes that many restaurants and hotels serve tap water unless bottled water is specifically requested. It suggests asking for no ice. The CDC adds that teeth should be brushed with bottled or disinfected water.


## Food

The CDC's guidance is to avoid raw foods and fresh salads, which may have been washed in contaminated water, and to prefer food that arrives hot. It advises against food from street vendors, and says that anyone who chooses to eat street food should apply the same rules. Fruit you peel yourself is the easy exception.


## Hands

The CDC's instruction is to wash your hands often with soap and water, especially after using the bathroom and before eating. This is the least glamorous precaution and one that is easy to keep on a trip. Our advice is to carry hand sanitizer for the moments when there is no sink, such as a market or a boat.



## How to treat Bali belly

Treating Bali belly starts with fluids. The CDC's advice is to drink lots of fluids to stay hydrated, and in serious cases to use oral rehydration solution to replace what is lost. Dehydration is what turns an unpleasant day into a medical problem, particularly in Bali's heat, so drinking comes before everything else.


## Over-the-counter medicine

The CDC notes that several drugs, such as loperamide, can be bought over the counter to treat the symptoms of diarrhea. Our advice is to bring a small supply from home along with oral rehydration salts, since the State Department warns that counterfeit medication is common in Indonesia and may have the wrong strength or dangerous ingredients.


## Antibiotics

According to the CDC, a doctor may prescribe antibiotics for travelers' diarrhea, but they should be considered only for severe cases. That is a conversation to have with your own doctor before the trip, not a decision to make at a pharmacy counter abroad.


## Rest, and safe drinks only

While you are ill, every drink should be a safe one: sealed bottled water, oral rehydration solution, or tea served steaming hot. Our advice is to cancel the day's plans without guilt. Pushing on to a long drive or a boat crossing with a stomach bug makes both the illness and the day worse.


## When to see a doctor

A doctor is the right next step for Bali belly when the illness is more than mild. Our advice, which is general and not a substitute for medical care, is to seek help for blood in the stool, a high fever, repeated vomiting, signs of dehydration, or symptoms that are not improving. Our advice is to have children and older travelers seen sooner.


## What a clinic visit involves

The State Department says routine medical care is available in Indonesia's major cities, and that hospitals and doctors often require payment or a sizable deposit before service or admission. Most care providers accept only cash, and credit card payment is not always available. Take your passport, your insurance details and a way to pay.


## If it continues after you fly home

Our advice to anyone still unwell after the trip is to see a doctor and to say where they traveled. A stomach problem that outlasts the trip needs that visit, because the cause may be something a short illness would not suggest.


## What to pack for your stomach

A stomach kit for Bali is small and worth the space. These are our own suggestions, based on the CDC's treatment advice: oral rehydration salts, an over-the-counter diarrhea medicine such as loperamide, hand sanitizer, and any prescription your own doctor recommends for the trip. Keep it in your day bag, since that is where you will be when it is needed.


## Medicine rules still apply

Whatever you pack has to enter Indonesia legally. Prescription medicine needs its prescription, with a limit of 90 days' use for prescribed medicine, and the State Department says all prescription medication must be declared on the arrival card. Over-the-counter medicine has its own smaller allowance.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. A stomach bug is a small thing, yet the answers kept pointing elsewhere: to the medicine you packed and whether it could cross the border, to a clinic that wants a deposit, to an insurance policy you bought months ago, and to whatever was scheduled for the next day. The illness is minor. What it collides with decides how much of the trip it costs.

How those pieces connect, from the medicine bag to the clinic bill to the day you can least afford to lose, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is Bali belly?Bali belly is travelers' diarrhea, a stomach illness from contaminated food or water. The CDC calls travelers' diarrhea the most common travel-related illness.

How do you avoid Bali belly?The CDC's advice is to eat food that is cooked and served hot, avoid buffets, drink only from factory-sealed containers, avoid ice, and wash your hands often.

What should I take for Bali belly?Fluids first. The CDC advises drinking lots of fluids and using oral rehydration solution in serious cases; over-the-counter drugs such as loperamide treat the symptoms.

Should I take antibiotics for Bali belly?The CDC says antibiotics from a doctor should be considered only for severe cases. Discuss it with your own doctor before the trip.



===== bali-hospitals-and-clinics-billing [health-and-safety / health-chain] 1160w =====
Hospitals and Clinics in Bali: What to Expect and How Billing Works
Medical care in Bali is paid for by the patient, usually before treatment and often in cash. The State Department says hospitals and doctors in Indonesia often require payment or a sizable deposit upfront, that most US health plans do not cover care abroad, and that it strongly recommends insurance covering medical evacuation. With travel insurance, you normally pay first and claim afterward.


## What medical care in Indonesia is like

The State Department's description of medical care in Indonesia is measured. Health facilities exist throughout the country, and routine medical care is available in major cities. Adequate care may be harder to find in rural areas, and most foreigners with the resources leave the country for anything beyond basic procedures. Medical staff may speak little or no English.


## What that means in Bali

The State Department's statement covers Indonesia as a whole and does not rate Bali separately, so we will not rank the island's hospitals or name one as the best. The practical reading is that a clinic visit for a common problem is realistic, and that a serious injury or illness may mean a transfer, possibly out of the country.


## Finding a doctor

The State Department notes that the US embassy maintains a list of doctors and hospitals, and that the embassy does not endorse or recommend any provider on it. The US Consular Agency in Bali, at +62-361-958-6161, is the local point of contact for Americans. Your insurer's assistance line is the other place to ask.


## How billing works

Billing at a hospital or clinic in Indonesia starts before the treatment. The State Department says medical care is not free in Indonesia, that a patient will incur expenses even at a public facility, and that hospitals and doctors often require payment or sizable deposits upfront prior to service or admission. The bill is the patient's to settle.


## Cash first

The State Department's guidance is that most care providers overseas accept only cash payments and that credit card payment is not always available. A traveler should therefore know how they would raise a deposit at short notice: which cards they carry, what their daily withdrawal limit is at home, and who could send money.


## Your US insurance

The State Department is blunt about American coverage. Most US health plans do not cover medical expenses abroad, Medicare and Medicaid do not pay bills abroad, most hospitals and doctors abroad do not accept US health insurance, and the US government does not pay medical bills. Ask your own plan what it covers outside the country before you travel.


## We quote no prices

We have no official source for what a consultation, a night in hospital or a deposit costs in Bali, and figures passed around online vary widely. We are not going to print a number. Ask the clinic for an estimate before treatment when the situation allows it, and ask for itemized receipts afterward.



## How travel insurance fits in

Travel insurance in Indonesia usually works by reimbursement. The State Department highly recommends buying travel insurance before the trip, and notes that if you purchase supplemental insurance you are responsible for seeking reimbursement from the provider. In plain terms, expect to pay the clinic yourself and to claim the money back with paperwork.


## Call the insurer early

If your policy has an assistance line, our advice is to call it as soon as a hospital visit looks likely. Whether an insurer will pay a hospital directly or guarantee a bill is a matter for your policy and for that call. We cannot tell you what your insurer will do, and no article can.


## Keep every document

A claim is built from documents. Ask for the medical report, the itemized bill and proof of payment before you leave the clinic, and photograph each page. If the visit followed an accident or a theft, a police report belongs in the same folder.


## Ambulances and getting to a hospital

Ambulance services in Indonesia are not widely available, according to the State Department, and ambulances can take 45 minutes or longer to arrive. It notes that travelers may prefer to take a taxi or a private vehicle to the hospital instead of waiting. The emergency medical numbers it gives are 118 and 119.


## Know the answer before you need it

Our advice is to ask your accommodation on the first day which clinic or hospital they would send a guest to, and how you would get there at night. Save the address in your phone's map. In an emergency the general numbers are 112 for the unified emergency line, 110 for police, 119 for an ambulance and 113 for fire.


## Transfers cost extra

The State Department says patients must cover all costs of transfer to or between hospitals. A move from a small clinic to a larger hospital is a separate bill, and a move out of the country is a far larger one. That is the reason the evacuation part of an insurance policy matters.


## Medical evacuation

Medical evacuation is the part of the picture the State Department stresses most. It says the US government strongly recommends supplemental insurance to cover medical evacuation, and that travelers who are injured will likely need to travel to a major city for appropriate care. For anything beyond basic procedures, it notes, most foreigners with the means leave Indonesia.


## Check your policy for it

Our advice is to read the evacuation section of any policy before buying: what triggers it, who decides, and where the policy would send you. These terms differ from one policy to the next. A policy bought for trip cancellation alone may contain very little medical cover.


## Medicine and pharmacies

Pharmacies in Indonesia sell a wide range of medicines with few controls, and the State Department warns that counterfeit medication is common and may not work, may have the wrong strength, or may contain dangerous ingredients. Its advice is to buy medication with the help of a medical professional and from trusted places.


## Bring what you rely on

Our advice is to bring the full supply of any medicine you depend on, within Indonesia's import rules, instead of planning to buy it in Bali. How a medical bill, a medicine supply and an insurance policy fit together before the trip is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Do hospitals in Bali take US health insurance?The State Department says most hospitals and doctors abroad do not accept US health insurance, and most US plans do not cover care abroad.

Do you have to pay upfront at a hospital in Bali?Often, yes. The State Department says hospitals and doctors in Indonesia often require payment or sizable deposits before service or admission.

How do you call an ambulance in Bali?The State Department gives 118 or 119 for emergency medical services, and notes that ambulances can take 45 minutes or longer, so a taxi or private vehicle may be faster.

Do I need medical evacuation insurance for Bali?The State Department says the US government strongly recommends supplemental insurance that covers medical evacuation.



===== travel-insurance-for-bali-fine-print [health-and-safety / scooter-liability] 1182w =====
Travel Insurance for Bali: The Scooter and Evacuation Fine Print
Travel insurance for Bali is worth buying, and two clauses decide whether it helps: the one about riding a scooter and the one about medical evacuation. The State Department highly recommends travel insurance for Indonesia and strongly recommends cover for evacuation. Our advice is to read those two sections of a policy before you pay.


## Why insurance matters more in Bali

Travel insurance matters in Bali because medical care there is paid for by the patient. The State Department says medical care is not free in Indonesia, that hospitals and doctors often require payment or a sizable deposit upfront, and that most US health plans do not cover expenses abroad. Medicare and Medicaid do not pay bills abroad at all.


## You pay first

The State Department notes that a traveler who buys supplemental insurance is responsible for seeking reimbursement from the provider. Insurance in Bali is therefore usually a way of getting money back, not a card that makes a bill disappear. The same guidance says most care providers accept only cash.


## What we will not tell you

We cannot say what any insurer will or will not pay, and we name no company and quote no premium. Policies differ, and the wording of your own policy is the only authority on your cover. What follows is a list of the questions that the facts of a Bali trip make worth asking.


## The scooter clause

The scooter clause is the first thing to read, because of how Indonesian law treats licenses. The law requires every driver to hold a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and it has a separate class for motorcycles. The State Department adds that a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia.


## Why the license matters to a policy

A policy may make cover for a motorbike accident depend on the rider being licensed for that vehicle. We are describing a type of condition to look for, not your policy. The question to put to an insurer in writing is direct: am I covered riding a scooter in Indonesia with the license and permit I actually hold?


## The legal position without the right license

Riding without the required license is an offense in itself in Indonesia, carrying up to 4 months' detention or a fine of IDR 1,000,000 for riding without the required license. Bali's rules for visitors also require a valid license and a helmet. Ask your insurer how the policy treats an accident in which the rider was breaking local law.


## Other conditions to look for

A policy that covers motorbikes may attach further conditions. Look for wording about helmets, engine size, riding as a passenger, and alcohol. Indonesian law requires a helmet for rider and passenger alike. Read each condition as a question about how you really plan to get around.



## The evacuation clause

Medical evacuation is the second clause to read, and the State Department gives the reason. It says the US government strongly recommends supplemental insurance to cover medical evacuation, that an injured traveler will likely need to go to a major city for appropriate care, and that most foreigners with the resources leave Indonesia for anything beyond basic procedures.


## What to check

Our advice is to look for three things in the evacuation section: what medical situation triggers it, who makes the decision, and where the policy would take you. Check too whether the policy covers transfers between hospitals, since the State Department says patients must cover all costs of transfer to or between hospitals.


## The assistance line

Our advice is to find the assistance phone number in your policy, save it in your contacts and on paper, and give it to the person you are traveling with. Call it early in any hospital visit, before a decision has to be made in a hurry, and ask how an evacuation would be authorized.


## Medical cover and how claims work

Medical cover on a travel policy is used through a claim, and a claim is made of paperwork. The State Department advises checking with your insurer about evacuation assistance, medical insurance and trip cancellation coverage before you travel. Ask at the same time how a claim is filed and what documents it needs.


## Documents to collect

These are our own suggestions for any medical visit abroad: the doctor's report, an itemized bill, proof of payment, and a police report if an accident or theft was involved. After a road accident, the State Department's guidance is to stay at the scene and notify the police, which also produces the record a claim will ask for.


## Pre-existing conditions and activities

Two more sections deserve a slow read. One is the treatment of medical conditions you already have. The other is the list of activities the policy excludes or charges extra for, so look there for diving, surfing, trekking and motorbikes. Match the list against what you intend to do.


## Cancellation, delays and belongings

Travel insurance also covers the non-medical ways a trip goes wrong, and Bali has its own versions. We know of no nonstop flight from the United States to Bali, so the journey includes a connection that can be missed. The State Department also describes pickpocketing, vehicle theft and snatch-and-grab robberies as common in Indonesia.


## Theft

Ask your insurer whether a theft claim needs a police report, and get one if it does. Phones are the item the State Department singles out, advising travelers to be mindful near roadways where snatch-and-grab crimes occur. Check the limit your policy sets for a single item, which may be lower than the price of a phone.


## Natural events

The State Department's advisory for Indonesia, at Level 2, Exercise increased caution, cites natural disasters among its reasons. Ask how the policy treats volcanic ash, earthquakes and cancelled flights, and whether cover depends on buying the policy before an event is announced.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The policy turned out to be the last link in a chain that starts somewhere else: with the license in your wallet, the permit you did or did not get before leaving, whether you rent a scooter at all, and how far your base is from a hospital. An insurance question became a transport question and a where-to-stay question.

How those links connect, and which of them you can settle before you book, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Do I need travel insurance for Bali?The State Department highly recommends travel insurance for Indonesia and strongly recommends cover for medical evacuation. Most US health plans do not cover care abroad.

Does travel insurance cover scooter accidents in Bali?It depends on your policy's wording. Ask your insurer in writing whether you are covered with the license and permit you hold, since Indonesian law requires a license matching the vehicle.

Does Medicare work in Bali?No. The State Department says US Medicare and Medicaid do not cover expenses or bills abroad.

Will the hospital bill my travel insurance directly?Do not count on it. The State Department says travelers with supplemental insurance are responsible for seeking reimbursement, and that most providers accept only cash.



===== grab-and-gojek-in-bali [drivers-and-ride-apps / traffic-time] 1076w =====
Grab and Gojek in Bali: Where They Work and Where They Don't
Grab and Gojek both operate in Bali, and for a visitor they are the simplest way to book a car or a motorbike ride at a price shown in advance. The State Department lists both as available options in Indonesia and notes that the apps show the driver, the vehicle and the fare. Grab runs airport pickups from its own lounge at the international terminal.


## What Grab and Gojek are

Grab and Gojek are ride-hailing apps, used in Indonesia much as Uber and Lyft are used in the United States. The State Department describes licensed taxis and ride-hailing services as widely available in major Indonesian cities, and says the two apps provide driver identification, vehicle information and fare transparency through the phone.


## Why the fare display matters

The fare shown in the app before you confirm is the practical advantage for a newcomer. There is no meter to watch and no price to negotiate in a language you do not speak. The State Department's advice for Indonesia is to arrange transportation through reputable taxi companies or ride-hailing applications.


## Cars and motorbikes

Both apps offer rides on the back of a motorbike as well as in a car. Grab's own airport page lists car, premium car, electric car and motorbike options, along with advance booking. A motorbike ride is a ride on a motorcycle in Bali traffic, and Indonesian law requires a helmet for the passenger as well as the rider.


## Using the apps as an American

Using Grab or Gojek as an American visitor needs a working data connection and a way to pay. Our advice is to set the app up before you leave home, in case it verifies your account by text message to your US number. Rides are booked and tracked on the phone, so a local SIM, an eSIM or roaming has to be working when you land.


## Paying

Our advice is to check in the app at home which payment methods it offers you, to add a card if it accepts yours, and to carry small rupiah notes as a backup. Indonesia's cross-border QR payment scheme does not include the United States, so do not plan on paying by QR from a US banking app.


## On Nyepi

Ride apps need mobile data, and on Nyepi, Bali's day of silence, mobile data is switched off for 24 hours, from 6 a.m. to 6 a.m. Plan any ride you need around the edges of that window, on the evening before and the morning after.



## Grab and Gojek at Bali airport

Grab operates pickups at Bali's airport from a GrabCar Lounge at the International Arrival Terminal, with rides booked in the app. That gives an arriving American an alternative to negotiating a fare in the arrivals hall after a long journey. We have found no equivalent official page for Gojek at the airport, so we describe only Grab's arrangement.


## What we could not confirm

Grab's page gives no fees or opening hours for the lounge and does not describe a pickup point at the domestic terminal. Check the app on the day. If the lounge is closed or the wait is long, the alternatives are a licensed airport taxi or a transfer arranged with your hotel before you fly.


## Avoiding the touts

The State Department's advice is to avoid unlicensed taxis that solicit passengers directly, particularly in airports, tourist areas and transportation hubs. A driver who approaches you in the arrivals hall is the case it describes. Walk to the lounge or the official taxi counter instead.


## Where the apps work less well

Grab and Gojek are city services, and the State Department's description of them refers to major Indonesian cities. In Bali that points to the built-up south. We have no official source describing coverage area by area, so we are not going to draw a map of where the apps work and where they do not.


## Local restrictions

We have found no official source on whether any area of Bali restricts app pickups, so we state no rule. Our advice is to ask your accommodation what applies on its own street before you rely on an app to get home.


## Our advice for remote areas

Our advice is to assume that an app ride out to a remote temple, waterfall or beach may not be matched by a ride back. For day trips away from the south, a driver booked for the day is the reliable arrangement. Ask your hotel how guests usually get back before you set out.


## Staying safe in a ride

The State Department says most taxi and ride-hail trips in Indonesia occur without issues, and it gives simple checks. Confirm the driver and vehicle information before entering the vehicle, stay aware of your surroundings, and share your trip details through the application when possible. Match the license plate and the driver's photo to the app every time.


## Taxis as the alternative

When an app has no cars, a licensed taxi is the fallback. The State Department describes Blue Bird taxis as generally considered the most reliable, and advises making sure there is a working meter and matching the photo on the dashboard ID with the driver before getting in.


## Phones

The State Department warns of snatch-and-grab crime near roadways, and a phone held out at the curb while you watch for your car is an easy target. Stand back from the road while you wait, and put the phone away once the car has arrived.


## What ride apps cannot fix

Ride apps make short trips in Bali simple to book, and they do nothing about how long those trips take. A car booked in seconds still sits in the same traffic as every other car. How much of a day a drive consumes is the larger planning question, and it is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is there Uber in Bali?The ride-hailing apps in Bali are Grab and Gojek. The State Department lists both as available options in Indonesia.

Can I get a Grab from Bali airport?Yes. Grab operates airport pickups from a GrabCar Lounge at the International Arrival Terminal, booked in the app.

Are Grab and Gojek safe in Bali?The State Department says most taxi and ride-hail trips occur without issues and advises confirming the driver and vehicle before getting in and sharing trip details through the app.

Do Grab and Gojek work everywhere in Bali?We have no official source on coverage by area. For day trips outside the built-up south, our advice is to book a driver for the day.



===== esim-vs-local-sim-in-bali [phones-and-internet / None] 1186w =====
eSIM vs. Local SIM in Bali
For most Americans on a normal vacation, a travel eSIM bought before the flight is the easier choice, and a local SIM is the better one for anyone who wants an Indonesian phone number. Neither requires registering your phone with Indonesian customs: a visitor is exempt when the phone stays on its home SIM with roaming, or uses an Indonesian SIM registered at an operator's outlet.


## The three ways to get data in Bali

An American visitor has three ways to get mobile data in Bali: roaming on a US plan, a travel eSIM bought online, or a local Indonesian SIM. Each one works with Indonesia's phone registration rules, which exempt short-stay visitors. The differences are in price, in convenience on arrival, and in whether you end up with a local number.


## Roaming on your US plan

Roaming is the option with no setup. Your phone keeps its US number and connects to an Indonesian network through your carrier. Indonesian customs names this case directly: a visitor whose phone keeps using its home SIM on roaming does not need to register the device. What it costs is a question for your carrier, and the answer varies a great deal between plans.


## A travel eSIM

A travel eSIM is a data plan downloaded to the phone, bought from an international provider before you leave. It adds a second line for data beside your US number. Most are data-only, which means no Indonesian phone number. Your phone must support eSIM and must not be locked to your US carrier.


## A local SIM

A local SIM is bought from an Indonesian mobile operator and comes with an Indonesian number. Customs says a foreign tourist's phone using an Indonesian SIM registered at an operator's outlet gets network access for up to 90 days, with no customs registration of the device needed.


## The registration rule in one paragraph

Indonesia's phone registration system applies to devices that stay in the country, and customs sets out two cases where a short-stay visitor is outside it. The first is a phone that stays on its home SIM with roaming. The second is a phone using an Indonesian SIM that was registered at a mobile operator's outlet, which is given access for a limited period.


## Where the travel eSIM fits

A travel eSIM from an international provider is a roaming product: the plan belongs to a foreign operator and connects through an Indonesian network. That places it with the first case as we read the rule. Customs does not mention travel eSIMs by name, so this is our reading and not a quoted line.


## Buy a local SIM at an outlet

The customs wording about local SIMs refers to registration at an operator's outlet. Our advice is therefore to buy a local SIM from an operator's own shop or counter, with your passport, and not from an unmarked stall. The 90 days are a limit as well as an allowance, which matters only to a visitor who extends a stay or returns.



## How to choose

Choosing between an eSIM and a local SIM in Bali depends on three questions: whether your phone is unlocked, whether you need a local number, and how much effort you want to spend on arrival. For a one- or two-week vacation where the phone is used for maps, messages and ride apps, data alone is enough.


## Choose an eSIM if

These are our own suggestions.


- Your phone supports eSIM and is unlocked.

- You want data working when the plane lands, with no shop visit.

- You are content to keep your US number for calls and texts over Wi-Fi or apps.



## Choose a local SIM if


- You want an Indonesian number for drivers, restaurants or deliveries to call.

- You are staying for several weeks and expect to use a lot of data.

- Your phone does not support eSIM but is unlocked.



## Choose roaming if

Roaming suits a traveler whose US plan includes Indonesia at a price they accept, or whose phone is locked to the carrier and cannot take another line. Check the daily or monthly charge with your carrier before the trip, and check whether it applies automatically the moment the phone connects abroad.


## Before you leave home

Three checks at home decide whether any of this works, and none of them can be done at the airport in Bali. Confirm that your phone is unlocked, by asking your carrier. Confirm whether it supports eSIM. And install anything that needs a text message to your US number while that number still receives texts normally.


## The unlocked phone

A local SIM or a travel eSIM only works in a phone that is not locked to your US carrier. This is a matter between you and your carrier, separate from Indonesian rules. Our advice is to ask for the unlock well before the trip, since it is a request the carrier processes on its own schedule.


## Apps that verify by text

Banking apps, ride apps and messaging apps often confirm your identity by text message to your US number. Set them up at home. If you remove your US SIM to fit a local one, those texts go to a card sitting in your luggage, which is a reason many travelers prefer to keep the US line active beside an eSIM.


## What does not change with any option

Mobile data in Bali has one scheduled gap that no plan avoids. On Nyepi, the Balinese day of silence, mobile data on the island is switched off for 24 hours, from 6 a.m. to 6 a.m. The measure is addressed to mobile operators, so it applies to a roaming phone and a local SIM alike.


## Coverage

We have no official coverage data for Bali's operators, and we name no network as the best. Signal in the built-up south and signal on a mountain road or a small island are different things on any network. Download offline maps for the areas you will visit before you leave your hotel's Wi-Fi.


## Prices

We quote no prices for SIMs, eSIMs or roaming, because all three change often and vary by seller. Compare the current price of a travel eSIM with your carrier's roaming charge for the same number of days; that comparison usually settles the question.



## FAQ

Is an eSIM or a local SIM better for Bali?A travel eSIM is easier for a short vacation because it works on landing. A local SIM is better if you want an Indonesian number or are staying for several weeks.

Do I need to register my phone to use a SIM in Bali?No. Indonesian customs exempts short-stay visitors who roam on a home SIM or who use an Indonesian SIM registered at an operator's outlet.

How long does a tourist SIM work in Indonesia?Customs says an Indonesian SIM registered at an operator's outlet gives a tourist's phone network access for up to 90 days.

Will my US phone work in Bali?It will roam if your carrier's plan allows it. To use a local SIM or a travel eSIM, the phone must be unlocked.



===== flights-from-the-us-to-bali [flights-and-arrival / long-haul-arrival] 1166w =====
Flights from the US to Bali: Routes, Layovers and Realistic Travel Time
No airline flies nonstop from the United States to Bali, so every trip includes at least one connection. The usual pattern is a long flight across the Pacific to an Asian hub, or east through the Gulf or Istanbul, followed by a shorter flight into Bali. The choice of hub, and whether both flights are on one ticket, shapes the journey more than the choice of airline.


## Is there a direct flight to Bali from the US?

There is no nonstop flight between the United States and Bali. Schedule data for Bali's airport in October 2026 lists dozens of international routes and no US city among them. The longest nonstop flight into Bali on that list is from Istanbul, at about 13 hours. An American traveler changes planes somewhere along the way.


## Why that matters

A trip with a connection has two flights that can run late and one airport where a bag has to change planes. The US Department of Transportation's advice for any connection is to check the time between flights and to ask yourself what happens if the first one is delayed. On a journey this long, that question deserves a real answer before you pay.


## Schedules change

Airlines add and drop routes by season, and the list of hubs below is a snapshot dated October 2026. Search your own dates before relying on any route named here. We quote no fares and no total journey times, because both change daily and depend on your home airport.


## The main ways to route the trip

Flights from the United States to Bali fall into two broad routings. The first crosses the Pacific to a hub in East or Southeast Asia and continues south to Bali. The second heads east across the Atlantic to the Gulf or to Istanbul and continues from there. Which one is shorter depends on where in the United States you start.


## Through East Asia

The October 2026 schedule lists nonstop flights to Bali from Tokyo and Seoul, at roughly seven to eight hours, and from Taipei and Hong Kong, at roughly five to six hours. For travelers on the West Coast, these hubs lie closest to the straight line to Indonesia.


## Through Southeast Asia

Singapore is the closest large hub to Bali on that schedule, at about three and a half hours, with several airlines on the route. Kuala Lumpur, Bangkok and Manila are also listed with nonstop flights to Bali. A routing through one of these puts the long flight first and leaves a short final leg.


## Through the Gulf or Istanbul

The schedule lists nonstop flights to Bali from Dubai and Doha, at about ten hours, and from Istanbul, at about 13 hours. From the eastern United States, flying east can be about as short as flying west. This routing splits the journey into two long flights.



## One ticket or two

How the flights are ticketed matters as much as where they connect. The Department of Transportation ranks itineraries by how safely bags travel: a nonstop is safest, then a through flight, then a connection on one airline, and last a connection between two different airlines. It advises asking whether bags will be transferred on any connection involving two airlines.


## What we recommend

Our advice for a first trip is to book the whole journey to Bali as a single ticket, so that one booking covers both flights. If you build the trip from two separate bookings to save money, ask each airline in advance what happens to you and to your bag if the first flight arrives late. Do not assume the answer.


## If a flight is delayed

The Department of Transportation notes that on international itineraries passengers may be able to recover expenses caused by a delay under the Montreal Convention, by filing a claim with the airline. It also points out that taking an earlier flight leaves more rerouting options if something goes wrong. Keep receipts for anything a delay costs you.


## The time difference

Bali is on Central Indonesian Time, eight hours ahead of UTC, and Indonesia does not observe daylight saving time. That puts Bali 12 hours ahead of New York during daylight saving time and 13 hours ahead in winter, and 15 or 16 hours ahead of Los Angeles. Day and night are close to reversed.


## You lose a calendar day going out

Flying west across the Pacific crosses the International Date Line, so the calendar jumps forward. Depending on the routing, a departure on a Friday evening can land in Bali on a Sunday. Count your hotel nights from the local arrival date printed on the ticket, not from the day you leave home.


## Before you book

Booking flights to Bali touches several rules that have nothing to do with airlines. Your passport must be valid for 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages, and immigration expects a visitor to hold an outbound ticket from Indonesia. A one-way ticket into Bali needs a second booking out.


## Check the calendar

Bali has one day a year when the airport does not handle regular flights. In 2027 Nyepi falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, and press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe a 24-hour halt. Do not book an arrival or a departure for that date, and confirm with your airline if your dates are close to it.


## The layover country has rules too

If your connection is long enough that you want to leave the airport, the entry rules of that country apply to you. Check the State Department's page for the layover country before you plan a day in the city. A transit that stays inside the terminal is a different matter from an entry.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Choosing a flight turned into choosing a hub, a ticket type and an arrival date, and each of those reaches into the trip itself. The hour you land decides whether the first night is spent near the airport or across the island. The day you land decides which hotel night you pay for and never sleep in.

How the routing, the ticket and the first two days fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Are there direct flights from the US to Bali?No. There is no nonstop flight between the United States and Bali, so every routing includes at least one connection.

Where do flights to Bali connect?Schedule data from October 2026 lists nonstop flights to Bali from hubs including Singapore, Hong Kong, Taipei, Seoul, Tokyo, Dubai, Doha and Istanbul.

What is the time difference between Bali and the US?Bali is 12 or 13 hours ahead of New York and 15 or 16 hours ahead of Los Angeles, depending on daylight saving time. Bali does not change its clocks.

Should I book one ticket or separate tickets to Bali?Our advice is a single ticket for the whole journey. The Department of Transportation advises asking whether bags will be transferred on any connection involving two airlines.



===== choosing-a-layover-city-for-bali [flights-and-arrival / long-haul-arrival] 1151w =====
Choosing a Layover City for Bali from the US
The best layover city for Bali is the one that leaves a short final flight and enough time to make it. Since no airline flies nonstop from the United States, the hub is a real choice. Singapore is the closest large hub, at about three and a half hours from Bali; Tokyo, Seoul, Taipei and Hong Kong sit on the direct Pacific path; Dubai, Doha and Istanbul serve the eastern United States.


## What makes a good layover city

A good layover city for a Bali trip does three things: it puts you on a short final flight, it gives you a sensible amount of time between flights, and it does not add much distance. The US Department of Transportation's advice on connections is to check the time between flights and to ask what happens if the first flight is delayed.


## The Department of Transportation's criteria

The Department of Transportation suggests that when fares and service are equivalent, a traveler should choose the least-congested connecting airport, so that it is easier to reach the second flight, and should consider the potential for bad weather at the connecting city. Those two tests apply to an Asian hub as much as to a domestic one.


## Time between flights

We give no minimum connection time in minutes, because it differs by airport and airline and we have no official figure for each hub. Our advice is to prefer a connection that would still work if your first flight landed an hour late, and to treat anything tighter as a risk you are choosing to take.


## The hubs, by distance from Bali

Schedule data for Bali's airport from October 2026 lists nonstop flights from a dozen large hubs. The flight times below are the approximate ones on that schedule, and they describe the second leg of the journey, the one after your long flight. Routes change by season, so check your own dates.


## Singapore

Singapore is about three and a half hours from Bali, and the schedule shows several airlines on the route. A short final leg served by several airlines leaves more ways to continue if a connection is missed. The cost is a very long first flight from the United States to Singapore.


## Hong Kong and Taipei

Hong Kong and Taipei are each roughly five to six hours from Bali on that schedule. Both lie near the straight line between the US West Coast and Indonesia.


## Tokyo and Seoul

Tokyo and Seoul are roughly seven to eight hours from Bali. They make the Pacific crossing shorter and the second leg longer, which splits the journey more evenly. Check how many flights a day run on the route for your dates, since that decides your options if a connection is missed.


## Dubai, Doha and Istanbul

Dubai and Doha are about ten hours from Bali, and Istanbul about 13. These hubs suit travelers from the East Coast and the Midwest, for whom flying east is not much longer than flying west. The journey becomes two long flights, with the layover near the midpoint.



## Staying inside the airport or going out

A layover is either a transit or a visit, and the difference is whether you pass through immigration. A traveler who stays in the terminal is in transit. A traveler who leaves the airport is entering that country, and its entry rules apply. Check the State Department's page for the layover country before planning to go out.


## Passport rules in two countries

Your passport has to satisfy Indonesia in any case: it must be valid for 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages. If you enter the layover country as well, its own validity and blank-page rules apply on top, and an entry stamp there uses part of a page.


## Bags on a long layover

The Department of Transportation's advice on any connection involving two airlines is to ask whether your bags will be transferred. Ask the same question about a long layover on one airline, since a checked bag may or may not be held through to Bali. Know the answer before you decide what to carry on.


## Breaking the journey on purpose

A layover can be extended into a stopover of a night or two, which turns the hardest part of the trip into a short visit. The CDC's advice on jet lag is to arrive at least two days before any important event so the body can adjust. A stopover puts part of that adjustment in a city you might enjoy.


## When a stopover makes sense

Our view is that a stopover is worth considering on a trip of two weeks or more, where a night in the hub costs a small share of the holiday. On a one-week trip the same night is a seventh of your time, and most travelers will prefer to push through to Bali.


## Duty-free purchases at the hub

Anything you buy at the layover airport enters Indonesia under the same allowances as what you packed at home. An adult may bring 1 liter of alcohol, and any excess is destroyed on the spot by the customs officer. Buy with that limit in mind.


## How to choose for your home airport

Choosing a layover city starts from where you live. From the West Coast, the hubs of East Asia are on the way. From the East Coast and the Midwest, both directions are possible, and the Gulf or Istanbul may be as quick as the Pacific. Compare the total journey time your search shows for each, on your dates.


## Our short list of questions

These are our own suggestions for comparing two routings.


- How long is the final flight into Bali, and how many flights a day are there on it?

- Would the connection still work if the first flight were an hour late?

- Are both flights on one ticket?

- What local time do you land in Bali?



## The last question matters most

The arrival time in Bali is easy to overlook while comparing hubs, and it decides what your first evening looks like. How the routing and the arrival hour feed into the first days of the trip is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is the best layover city for Bali from the US?It depends on your home airport. Singapore has the shortest final flight; Tokyo, Seoul, Taipei and Hong Kong are on the Pacific route; Dubai, Doha and Istanbul suit the eastern United States.

How long is the flight from Singapore to Bali?About three and a half hours, on schedule data from October 2026.

Can I leave the airport during a layover?Only if you meet the entry rules of that country. Check the State Department's page for the layover country before you plan to go out.

How long should a layover be on the way to Bali?We have no official minimum for each hub. Our advice is to choose a connection that would still work if your first flight landed an hour late.



===== through-ticket-vs-separate-tickets-to-bali [flights-and-arrival / long-haul-arrival] 1150w =====
Through Ticket vs. Separate Tickets to Bali
A through ticket puts both flights to Bali on one booking; separate tickets are two unrelated bookings that happen to meet at a hub. For a first trip, our advice is the through ticket. Separate tickets can cost less, and they move the risk of a missed connection and a stranded bag from the airline's booking to you.


## What the two options are

A through ticket is a single booking that covers the whole journey from your US airport to Bali, with a connection on the way. Separate tickets are two bookings: one from the United States to a hub such as Singapore or Tokyo, and a second, bought independently, from that hub to Bali. No US airline flies to Bali nonstop, so one of the two applies to every traveler.


## Why people split the ticket

The reason to buy separate tickets is price. A long-haul sale fare to an Asian hub combined with a regional flight into Bali can come to less than a single through fare. We quote no figures, because fares change daily. The saving is real on some dates and absent on others.


## What a connection is, officially

The US Department of Transportation describes a connection as a change of planes, nearly always with a separate flight number for each flight. Its consumer guide ranks a connection between two different airlines as the least secure arrangement for checked bags, behind a nonstop, a through flight, and a connection on one airline.


## What changes when a flight is late

A late first flight is where the two kinds of ticket differ. The Department of Transportation's advice for any connection is to ask yourself what will happen if the first flight is delayed, and to pick another flight or build a longer connection if you do not like the answer. With two separate bookings, that question has two airlines to answer it.


## Ask before you buy

We have no official source on how any particular airline treats a passenger who misses a separately booked flight because another airline's flight was late, so we will not describe it as a rule. Our advice is to ask the second airline directly, before buying, what its policy is for a passenger who does not reach the gate in time.


## What the law offers

The Department of Transportation notes that on international itineraries passengers may be able to recover expenses resulting from a delayed or canceled flight under Article 19 of the Montreal Convention, by filing a claim with the airline. That is a claim made afterward, with receipts. It is not a seat on the next flight.



## Bags

Checked baggage is the practical test of a ticket. The Department of Transportation advises that if you have a connection involving two airlines, you should ask whether your bags will be transferred. On two separate bookings, plan on the possibility that the answer is no, which means collecting the bag at the hub and checking it in again.


## What collecting a bag involves

Collecting a checked bag at a hub usually means passing through that country's immigration and customs, then checking in for the second flight as a new passenger. You are then entering the layover country, and its entry rules apply to your passport. Check the State Department's page for that country before you book this way.


## Carry-on only

Traveling with carry-on bags only removes the baggage question from a split itinerary. It does not remove the missed-flight question. Check the cabin allowance of the second airline as well as the first, since the two may differ.


## How much time to leave

The time between two separately booked flights is yours to choose, and no airline has checked it for you. The Department of Transportation's advice is to check the amount of time between flights on any connection. On separate tickets, our advice is to leave far more than you would accept on a through ticket.


## An overnight at the hub

The safest way to use separate tickets is to stop at the hub overnight. A night between the two flights absorbs most delays, lets you collect and re-check your bag without hurry, and breaks the journey for sleep. It costs a hotel night and requires that you can enter the layover country.


## The return journey

The same exposure exists on the way home, in reverse: the short flight out of Bali has to arrive in time for the long flight to the United States. A missed long-haul flight is the more expensive of the two to replace. If you split the ticket, give the return connection at least as much room as the outbound one.


## Paperwork that depends on the ticket

Indonesia's entry rules are indifferent to how the ticket was bought, with one exception worth knowing. A visitor is expected to hold an outbound ticket from Indonesia. If your flight out of Bali is on a separate booking from your flight in, carry the confirmation of that second booking where you can show it.


## The arrival card

The arrival card that every traveler submits in the 72 hours before landing asks for the flight on which you arrive in Indonesia. On a split itinerary, that is the second flight, the one from the hub to Bali. Enter that flight's details, not the long-haul flight's.


## Our recommendation

For a first trip to Bali, our recommendation is a through ticket, bought as one booking from your home airport to Bali. It keeps both flights on one record and makes the baggage question simple. Separate tickets are a reasonable choice for an experienced traveler who has priced the saving against an overnight at the hub.


## If you do split the ticket

These are our own suggestions.


- Stop overnight at the hub, or leave many hours between the flights.

- Ask the second airline about its policy for late passengers before you buy.

- Confirm you can enter the layover country.

- Check what your travel insurance says about missed connections on separate bookings.



## Where this fits

The ticket type decides how much slack the first and last days of the trip need, and those are the days most plans leave thin. How the ticket, the layover and the arrival day fit together is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is a through ticket?A single booking that covers the whole journey, including the connection. Both flights sit on one reservation.

Is it cheaper to book separate tickets to Bali?Sometimes. A sale fare to an Asian hub plus a regional flight can cost less than a through fare, but the price difference changes daily.

Will my bags be checked through on separate tickets?Do not assume so. The Department of Transportation advises asking whether bags will be transferred on any connection involving two airlines.

What happens if I miss a separately booked flight?We have no official source on any airline's policy, so ask the airline before you buy. A claim for delay expenses under the Montreal Convention is made afterward.



===== arriving-at-bali-airport [flights-and-arrival / long-haul-arrival] 1172w =====
Arriving at Bali Airport: Immigration to Curb, Step by Step
Arriving at Bali's airport means clearing four things in order: the visa, immigration, baggage and customs, with Bali's tourist levy handled before the trip or at a counter. Travelers who bought an e-VOA and filled in the arrival card before flying have little left to do at the airport. The last step is transport, where a ride app or an official taxi beats an offer in the hall.


## What to have ready before you land

An American landing in Bali needs a passport, a visa or the money for one, an arrival card QR code and proof of an onward flight. The passport must be valid for 6 months beyond your arrival date and have 2 blank pages, and immigration expects an outbound ticket from Indonesia.


## The arrival card

Every traveler submits Indonesia's arrival card online or in the official app, and it can only be completed within 72 hours of arrival. It produces a QR code, which the State Department says to save and show to immigration and customs. Keep a screenshot, since airport Wi-Fi is a poor thing to depend on at that moment.


## The tourist levy

Bali's tourist levy is IDR 150,000 per person and is separate from the visa. It can be paid online before travel, with the voucher sent by email, or at payment counters at the airport. Paying it in advance leaves one less line to stand in after a long journey.


## The visa counter and immigration

The visa comes first. A US citizen needs a tourist visa and can buy it online in advance or at the airport on arrival. The State Department says a visa can be purchased on arrival at the airport, and warns that travelers who do so may need to wait in long lines and that the process may take longer.


## If you already have an e-VOA

A traveler with an e-VOA skips the payment counter and goes to immigration with the passport and the visa record. Keep the e-VOA confirmation on your phone and on paper. The official fee is IDR 500,000 whichever way the visa is bought.


## If you buy the visa at the airport

A traveler without a visa pays at the visa on arrival counter before joining the immigration line. Have a payment card ready and, as a backup, cash. We have no official list of the currencies and cards the counter accepts on a given day, so carrying two ways to pay is our advice.


## At the immigration desk

Immigration checks the passport, the visa and the arrival card. The visa allows a stay of 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days. Look at the stamp or the record you are given before you walk away, and note the date your stay ends.



## Baggage and customs

Customs is the last official step, and the arrival card already carries your declaration. Personal goods are free of duty up to USD 500 per person per arrival, and an adult may bring 1 liter of alcohol. Alcohol or tobacco above the allowance is destroyed on the spot by the customs officer.


## Medicine

Medicines are on the list of items that send a passenger to the customs inspection channel. The State Department says all prescription medication must be declared on the arrival card, carried in its original packaging with the prescription and a doctor's letter. Keep those papers in your hand luggage, not in the checked bag.


## Cash

Cash worth IDR 100,000,000 or more must be declared. Few vacationers carry that much. The rule is a duty to declare and not a limit on what you may bring.


## Money and a phone connection

The two things most travelers want before leaving the terminal are rupiah and mobile data. The State Department says Indonesia has ATMs that accept US-issued bank cards, which may charge a conversion or transaction fee, and it advises using ATMs in secure locations such as major bank branches and checking machines for tampering.


## How much cash to take out

Our advice is to withdraw enough rupiah at the airport for the first day's transport, a meal and small purchases, and to do the rest later at a bank branch. Bali's rules for visitors say to change money only at authorized changers, which display a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo with a QR code.


## Data

A ride app needs data the moment you want a car. A travel eSIM set up at home works on landing; a local SIM has to be bought and registered at an operator's counter first. Either way, a visitor's phone does not need to be registered with customs.


## Getting out of the airport

Transport from Bali's airport comes in three forms: a ride booked in an app, an official airport taxi, and a car arranged in advance with your hotel or a driver. Grab operates pickups from a GrabCar Lounge at the International Arrival Terminal, with rides booked in the app.


## The offers in the hall

The State Department's advice for Indonesia is to avoid unlicensed taxis that solicit passengers directly, particularly in airports. A person who approaches you with a price is the case it describes. Its further advice is to confirm the driver and vehicle before getting in and to make sure a taxi has a working meter.


## A pre-arranged car

Our advice for a first arrival after a journey of this length is a car arranged before you fly, with the driver's name and a meeting point in writing. It costs more than an app ride and removes every decision from the moment you are least able to make one.


## The first evening

The first evening in Bali follows a journey with at least one connection and a time difference of 12 hours or more from the United States. The CDC's advice on jet lag is to follow the destination's schedule and stay in well-lit areas during the day. Our advice is to plan nothing for arrival day beyond reaching your room.


## How far to go on day one

The distance between the airport and your first bed is a choice you make when you book, long before you land, and it interacts with the hour your flight arrives. How the arrival time and the first night's location fit together is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What do I need when I land in Bali?A passport valid for six months with two blank pages, a tourist visa or the means to buy one, the arrival card QR code, and an onward ticket.

Can I buy a visa at Bali airport?Yes. The State Department says a visa can be purchased on arrival and warns of long lines. Buying the e-VOA in advance avoids the payment counter.

Is there Grab at Bali airport?Yes. Grab operates pickups from a GrabCar Lounge at the International Arrival Terminal, booked in the app.

Are there ATMs at Bali airport?The State Department says Indonesia has ATMs that accept US-issued bank cards and advises checking any machine for tampering. We have no official list of the machines in the terminal.



===== jet-lag-on-a-us-to-bali-trip [flights-and-arrival / long-haul-arrival] 1137w =====
Jet Lag on a US-to-Bali Trip: A First-48-Hours Plan
Jet lag on a trip from the United States to Bali is about as strong as jet lag gets, because the time difference is 12 to 16 hours and day and night are nearly reversed. The CDC's advice is to shift your sleep before you go, to stay in daylight after you arrive, and to keep naps short. Plan the first two days of the trip lightly.


## Why Bali is hard on the body clock

Jet lag, the CDC explains, is caused by a mismatch between a person's normal daily rhythms and a new time zone. Bali is eight hours ahead of UTC and does not change its clocks. That puts it 12 or 13 hours ahead of New York and 15 or 16 hours ahead of Los Angeles, depending on the time of year.


## What that means in practice

When it is noon in Bali, it is around midnight on the US East Coast and mid-evening on the West Coast. A body still on home time wants to sleep through the Balinese afternoon and wake in the small hours. The CDC notes that jet lag can affect mood, concentration and physical and mental performance.


## The journey adds to it

There is no nonstop flight from the United States to Bali, so the trip is two flights and a connection, with sleep taken in pieces. Fatigue from the journey and jet lag are different things, and on arrival in Bali a traveler has both. The first passes after a night's sleep; the second takes longer.


## Before you leave

The CDC's advice is to start adjusting a few days before departure by moving bedtime an hour or two toward the destination's time: later when traveling west, earlier when traveling east. For a trip across the Pacific, that means staying up later and getting up later for several days before the flight.


## Which direction counts

With a difference of close to 12 hours, the body could adjust in either direction, and the routing decides which one applies. A traveler crossing the Pacific is flying west. A traveler going by way of the Gulf or Istanbul is flying east. Use the CDC's rule for the direction you are actually flying.


## The day before

The CDC suggests that eating smaller meals just before travel may help with stomach trouble. Our advice is to finish packing a day early so the last night at home is a full one. Starting a journey of this length already short of sleep is the most common mistake.



## On the flight

The CDC's advice for the flight is specific. Eat small meals to avoid stomach aches. Avoid alcohol, because it disrupts sleep. Drink plenty of water. Use caffeine strategically and avoid it in the evening. Take any medicine or sleep aid only as directed by your healthcare provider.


## Sleeping on Bali time

Our advice is to set your watch to Bali time when you board and to sleep when it is night there, as far as the flight allows. An eye mask and earplugs cost little and help. On a two-flight journey, decide in advance which of the two flights is your night.


## Sleep medicine and Indonesia

Anyone who plans to carry sleeping tablets should check them against Indonesia's medicine rules before packing. The State Department says some prescription medications, including those for ADHD, are illegal in Indonesia, and that no list of prohibited drugs is published. Carry any prescription in its original packaging with the prescription.


## After you land

The CDC's advice after arrival is to stay in well-lit areas during the day and to follow the destination's sleep schedule. Daylight is the strongest signal the body clock has. In Bali that advice is easy to follow: spend the first mornings outside, on a beach, by a pool or on a walk.


## Naps

The CDC says naps should be no longer than 15 to 20 minutes. A long afternoon sleep on the first day feels wonderful and resets nothing, and it usually produces a wide-awake night. Set an alarm, and get up when it sounds.


## Food and drink

Eat at Bali's mealtimes from the first day, even when you are not hungry, and keep drinking water in the heat. Go easy on alcohol for the first nights, for the reason the CDC gives: it disrupts sleep. Coffee in the morning helps; coffee after mid-afternoon works against you.


## Planning the first days around it

The CDC recommends arriving at least two days before any important event, to give the body time to adjust. On a vacation, the important events are the things you have paid for and cannot move. Keep the first two days free of early starts, long drives and anything booked for a fixed hour.


## What to schedule

Our advice is to give the first two days to things that can slide: a swim, a walk, a massage, an early dinner. Save the sunrise hike, the dive and the long day trip for the middle of the trip. Waking early comes easily to a jet-lagged traveler from the United States, so quiet early mornings are a gift worth using.


## Driving

Jet lag affects concentration, and Bali's roads ask for a great deal of it. Our advice is not to ride a scooter or drive on the first day or two. Indonesia's traffic law also requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven, which is a separate reason to think before renting.


## Coming home

Jet lag applies on the return as well, and it arrives at a worse moment, just before work. The same CDC advice holds in reverse: shift bedtime toward home time for the last few days in Bali, seek daylight after you land, and keep naps short.


## Give yourself a buffer

Our advice is to land at least one full day, and better two, before you return to work. The CDC's two-day guideline for important events applies to a Monday morning meeting as much as to anything in Bali. A short trip leaves the least room for this, which is one reason trip length matters.



## FAQ

How bad is jet lag from the US to Bali?The time difference is 12 to 16 hours depending on your home time zone, so day and night are nearly reversed. Plan light first days.

How do I get over jet lag in Bali?The CDC's advice is to stay in well-lit areas during the day, follow the local sleep schedule, and keep naps to 15 to 20 minutes.

Should I adjust my sleep before flying to Bali?The CDC suggests moving bedtime an hour or two toward the destination's time for a few days before travel: later going west, earlier going east.

How many days should I allow to adjust?The CDC recommends arriving at least two days before any important event.



===== best-time-to-visit-bali [when-to-go / wet-dry-season] 1174w =====
Best Time to Visit Bali
The driest months in Bali are July and August, and the wettest are December through February. Climate normals for south Bali show about 1 inch of rain in July and about 14 inches in January, with temperatures near 80°F all year. For most visitors the best time is May, June or September, when rainfall is low and the peak summer crowds are absent.


## Bali's two seasons

Bali has a dry season and a wet season, and temperature has almost nothing to do with it. Monthly climate normals for the Denpasar station in the south put the average temperature between about 79°F in August and 83°F in November. What changes through the year is rain, and it changes a great deal.


## The dry season

The dry months in south Bali run from about May to October. The normals show rainfall falling from about 4 inches in April to under 3 inches in June, then to about 1 inch in July and 1.4 inches in August, before rising slowly through September and October, which each see about 2.5 inches.


## The wet season

The wet months run from about November to March. Rainfall climbs to about 5 inches in November and about 13 inches in December, peaks near 14 inches in January, and is still above 9 inches in March. Those are averages for a whole month at one station, not a description of any single day.


## The seasons do not start on a date

Indonesia's meteorological agency forecasts the start of each season zone by zone, every year, and the dates move. For 2026 it predicted that the dry season would arrive earlier than normal across most of Bali, with the peak in August. A normal is a long-run average; the year you travel will differ from it.


## Month by month

Bali's year divides into four stretches for a visitor, using the south Bali rainfall normals as the guide. The figures are monthly averages in inches, converted from the millimeters published for the Denpasar station, and they describe the south coast where most visitors stay.


- December to February: the wettest stretch, at about 11 to 14 inches a month.

- March and April: rain easing, from about 9 inches to about 4.

- May to October: the dry stretch, from under 4 inches down to about 1 in July.

- November: the turn back to wet, at about 5 inches.



## What the numbers do not show

The normals come from one weather station at sea level near the airport. They do not describe Ubud, the central mountains or the north coast, and they say nothing about how many days it rains or for how long. We have no official figures for those, so we do not state any.



## Crowds and prices follow a different calendar

Rain is one calendar; demand is another, and the two do not line up neatly. July and August are the driest months and also the northern summer vacation, and late December is wet and busy at once. We have no official visitor or price statistics by month, so we describe the pattern without numbers.


## The calendar effects we can source

Indonesia's own holidays move demand, and those dates are published. In 2027 the national holidays around Nyepi and Eid al-Fitr run together in March, creating a long break for the whole country. Bali's day of silence, Nyepi, falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, and press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe regular flights halting for 24 hours.


## Festivals worth choosing

Galungan, when decorated bamboo poles line the roads, comes every 210 days. In 2027 it falls on January 13 and August 11. A visitor who would like to see it can pick dates around it; a visitor who wants the quietest roads can pick dates away from it.


## Best time by what you want to do

The best time to visit Bali depends on the trip you have in mind, because the activities do not share a season. Beach days, mountain hikes and boat crossings favor the dry months. Surf depends on the coast as much as the month. Low prices and green landscapes favor the wet months.


## Beaches and sunsets

For beach weather, the low-rainfall months of May through October are the safer choice on the numbers. July and August are the driest. Our advice for travelers who can choose freely is May, June or September, when the rainfall normals are low and school vacations in the United States are not driving demand.


## Hiking and volcano sunrises

Mountain weather is not covered by the south coast normals, and conditions at altitude differ from the beach. The dry months are the conventional choice for a sunrise trek, since a clear summit is the whole point. Ask your guide about recent conditions before committing to an early start.


## Surfing

Surf guides describe a split by coast: the west-facing breaks are at their best in the dry season, when the trade winds blow offshore there, and the east-facing breaks take over in the wet season. A surfer can find waves in any month by choosing the right side of the island.


## When not to go

No month is closed to visitors in Bali, though two periods deserve a second look. January has the heaviest rainfall in the normals, at about 14 inches, which matters most for a short trip built around beaches and boats. Press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe a 24-hour halt to regular flights on Nyepi, so check arrival and departure dates against it and confirm with your airline.


## Short trips and wet months

On a two-week trip, a few wet days are absorbed. On a one-week trip, they are a large share of the vacation. Our advice is that the shorter the trip, the more the dry months are worth paying for, since a short trip has no spare days to give to the weather.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. "When" kept turning into "where" and "what". The driest months are the busiest, the best surf moves from one coast to the other, the mountains follow their own weather, and the calendar adds a day when nothing moves. The month you choose changes which base makes sense and which activities belong in the plan.

How the month, the coast and the base fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is the best month to visit Bali?For low rainfall without the peak summer crowds, our advice is May, June or September. July and August are the driest months in the south Bali normals.

When is the rainy season in Bali?From about November to March. South Bali's climate normals show about 13 to 14 inches of rain in December and January.

Is Bali hot all year?Yes. Average temperatures at the Denpasar station stay between about 79°F and 83°F in every month.

What is the worst time to visit Bali?January is the wettest month on average, and on Nyepi, according to press reports of the 2026 arrangements, regular flights halt for 24 hours. No month is closed to visitors.



===== bali-shoulder-season [when-to-go / wet-dry-season] 1091w =====
Bali's Shoulder Season: When It Is and What You Trade
Bali's shoulder season is April to June and September to October: the months on either side of the July and August peak. South Bali's rainfall normals are low in those months, at roughly 2 to 4 inches, against about 1 inch in July. The trade is a slightly higher chance of rain in exchange for fewer visitors, and for most travelers it is a good one.


## When the shoulder season is

The shoulder season in Bali falls in two windows, one before the peak and one after it. The first runs from April to June, as the wet season ends. The second covers September and October, before the rains return in November. Both sit inside or beside the dry season on the rainfall figures.


## The numbers behind it

Climate normals for the Denpasar station in south Bali show about 1 inch of rain in July and about 14 inches in January. The shoulder months sit near the bottom of that range: about 4 inches in April and May, under 3 in June, and about 2.5 in September and October.


## Why the peak is the peak

July and August are the driest months in the normals, and they are also the summer vacation months in the United States, Europe and elsewhere in the northern hemisphere. Weather and school calendars point at the same eight weeks. The shoulder season is what remains of the dry season once those eight weeks are taken out.


## What you gain

The gain from Bali's shoulder season is space.
