Fewer people are traveling, so the things that are limited in supply are easier to get: a room in the place you wanted, a driver on the day you need one, a table at sunset. We have no official occupancy or price statistics by month, so we describe the direction and not the size.


## Temperature stays the same

Nothing is given up in warmth. The normals put the average temperature at the Denpasar station between about 79°F and 83°F in every month of the year. A shoulder-season day is as warm as a peak-season day, and the sea is as swimmable.


## Green landscapes in the first window

April, May and June follow the wettest months of the year, when the south Bali normals show 9 to 14 inches of rain a month. Our observation, which is not a sourced fact, is that the landscape tends to look its greenest in the weeks after the rains, before the long dry stretch.



## What you trade

The trade in the shoulder season is certainty about the weather. A normal of 4 inches in April is four times the July figure, and the seasons do not switch on a fixed date. Indonesia's meteorological agency forecasts the start of the dry season each year by zone, and for 2026 it predicted an earlier start than normal across most of Bali.


## April and October are the edges

April and October are the months closest to the wet season on either side, so they carry the most uncertainty. In a year when the rains end late, April can still feel wet. In a year when they return early, late October can too. May, June and September sit further from the edges.


## Check the calendar as well as the climate

The shoulder months contain their own busy days. Indonesian national holidays create long weekends that fill the island with domestic visitors, and the dates change yearly. In 2027, for example, Eid al-Adha falls on May 17 and Vesak on May 20, each with a collective leave day attached.


## Which shoulder month for which trip

The two shoulder windows suit different trips, because of what comes before and after each. The first window follows the rains and leads into the peak; the second follows the peak and leads into the rains. The rainfall normals favor the second window slightly, at about 2.5 inches a month against 3 to 4.


## May and June

May and June have falling rainfall in the normals, from under 4 inches to under 3. Surf guides describe the west-coast surf season as beginning around May. A traveler who must be home before American schools let out will find these the natural months.


## September and October

September and October follow the driest months of the year. The normals show them at about 2.5 inches each, drier than May or June. Travelers without school-age children have the easiest time using this window, since it falls after the American summer.


## April

April is the least certain of the shoulder months. The normals show about 4 inches of rain, a sharp drop from March's 9. Our advice is to treat April as shoulder season with a wet-season backup plan: keep a couple of days flexible and put the weather-dependent outings in the second half of the trip.


## How to plan a shoulder-season trip

Planning for the shoulder season means leaving a little slack for weather, which costs nothing if the weather is fine. Our suggestions are simple: schedule boat trips and sunrise hikes early in the stay so there is room to move them, book the first nights and leave some later ones open, and pack a light rain jacket.


## What to book ahead anyway

Fewer visitors does not mean none. Our advice is still to book flights early, since the journey from the United States involves a connection and the good itineraries go first, and to reserve anything with fixed capacity that your trip depends on.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Picking a shoulder month looked like a question about rain and crowds, and it turned out to depend on the school calendar at home, the holiday calendar in Indonesia, the coast you want to surf and how much slack your itinerary has. The month is one input among several, and they adjust each other.

How the month, the calendar and the shape of the itinerary fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

When is shoulder season in Bali?April to June and September to October, the months on either side of the July and August peak.

Does it rain in Bali in May?Some. South Bali's climate normals show a little under 4 inches of rain in May, compared with about 14 in January and about 1 in July.

Is September a good time to visit Bali?Yes. The normals show about 2.5 inches of rain in September, and it falls after the northern summer vacation peak.

Is Bali cheaper in shoulder season?We have no official price statistics by month. Fewer visitors generally means more availability, which is the advantage we can state.



===== bali-rainy-season-what-its-like [when-to-go / wet-dry-season] 1106w =====
Bali in Rainy Season: What It Is Actually Like
Bali's rainy season runs from about November to March, and it is warm, green and wet. South Bali's climate normals show about 1 inch of rain in July and about 14 inches in January, so the difference between the seasons is large. A rainy-season trip works well for travelers who plan for it, and less well for a short trip built on beaches and boats.


## How wet the rainy season is

Rainy season in south Bali means monthly rainfall many times higher than in the dry months. The normals for the Denpasar station show about 5 inches in November, about 13 in December, about 14 in January, about 11 in February and about 9 in March. By April the figure is back to about 4 inches.


## For comparison

The same station's normals show about 1 inch in July and 1.4 inches in August. January, then, averages more than ten times the rain of the driest month. A reader in the United States can set those monthly totals beside the figures for their own city to get a feel for the scale.


## What the totals do not tell you

A monthly total says how much rain falls, not when. We have no official figures for the number of rainy days or the hours of rain in a day in Bali, so we do not describe a typical day as fact. Travelers commonly report heavy bursts with dry spells between them, and also the occasional day that stays wet throughout.


## Temperature and humidity

Rainy season in Bali is not a cold season. The normals put the average temperature at the Denpasar station at about 82°F from December through March, slightly warmer than the dry months of July and August, when it is about 79°F. The air holds more moisture, so the same temperature feels heavier.


## What to pack

These are our own suggestions for a wet-season bag: a light rain jacket or poncho, sandals that can get wet, a dry bag for a phone and passport, quick-drying clothes, and mosquito repellent. Leave the heavy raincoat at home. In warm rain, the aim is to dry quickly, not to stay sealed.


## Mosquitoes

The CDC says mosquitoes spread a number of diseases in Indonesia, including dengue, and that many of them cannot be prevented with a vaccine or medicine. Its advice is to cover exposed skin, use an appropriate insect repellent, and sleep in air-conditioned or screened rooms. That advice holds all year and deserves extra attention when there is standing water about.



## What still works in the rain

Most of what brings people to Bali carries on through the rainy season. Temples, markets, restaurants, spas, cooking classes and yoga studios are indoors or under cover. Rice terraces and forests are at their greenest. A driver and a car turn a wet day into a comfortable one, since the rain stays outside the window.


## Surfing

Surf guides describe the wet season as the time when the east-facing coast has the cleaner conditions, because the winds turn to the northwest. A surfer visiting between November and March looks to the east side of the island instead of the famous west-coast breaks.


## Festivals

The wet months hold some of the calendar's main events. Nyepi, the day of silence, falls in March: in 2027 it is on Monday, March 8, 2027. Galungan falls on January 13 in 2027. A rainy-season visitor may see more ceremony than a July visitor does.


## What the rain gets in the way of

Rain in Bali matters most for the outings that depend on a clear sky or a calm sea. A sunrise volcano trek is about the view. A boat crossing to a smaller island depends on sea conditions. A beach day needs sun. We have no official figures on cancellations, so we state the dependence and not a rate.


## Roads and water

Heavy rain slows traffic, and the CDC advises travelers in Indonesia to avoid swimming or wading in water that may be contaminated after flooding. Our advice is to stay out of floodwater on foot or on a scooter, and to allow more time than usual for any drive on a wet afternoon.


## Scooters in the rain

A wet road is harder to ride on, and visibility drops in a downpour. Our advice is to leave the scooter parked when it is raining and to use a car. Indonesian law requires a helmet in any weather, and a license matching the type of vehicle driven.


## How to plan a rainy-season trip

A rainy-season trip to Bali goes best with slack in the schedule. Our advice is to plan outdoor outings for the mornings and keep the afternoons loose, to hold a list of indoor things for wet days, and to avoid stacking weather-dependent bookings on consecutive days with no room to move them.


## Trip length

The longer the trip, the less a wet day costs. On a two-week stay, two or three rained-out days leave plenty. On a five-day stay they are half the vacation. If your dates are fixed in the wet season and the trip is short, choose a base where there is plenty to do under cover.


## The last day

Keep the day before your flight home free of anything that could strand you, such as a boat from another island. How the weather-dependent parts of a trip are placed against the fixed parts is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.


## Who the rainy season suits

Bali's rainy season suits travelers who value quiet and green over guaranteed sun, and anyone whose vacation dates are fixed in the northern winter. It suits a longer trip better than a short one. It is a weaker choice for a first visit of a week built around beach days, island boat trips and a sunrise hike.


## December is its own case

Late December combines high rainfall, about 13 inches in the normals, with the Christmas and New Year vacation. Wet weather and high demand arrive together. A traveler choosing freely within the wet season will find the weeks outside that holiday period calmer.



## FAQ

When is rainy season in Bali?From about November to March. The start and end move from year to year and are forecast annually by Indonesia's meteorological agency.

How much does it rain in Bali in January?About 14 inches on average at the Denpasar station in south Bali, the highest of any month.

Is Bali cold in the rainy season?No. Average temperatures at the Denpasar station are about 82°F from December through March.

Is it worth going to Bali in the rainy season?Yes, for travelers who plan slack into the schedule. It suits longer trips better than short ones built around beaches and boats.



===== surfing-in-bali-by-season-and-coast [beaches-and-surf / wet-dry-season] 1112w =====
Surfing in Bali: Which Coast Works in Which Season
Bali has surf all year, and the coast that works changes with the season. Surf guides describe the west-facing coast, from Canggu down to Uluwatu, as best in the dry season from about April to October, and the east-facing coast, around Sanur, Nusa Dua and Keramas, as best in the wet season from about November to March. Wind direction is the reason.


## Why the coast changes with the season

Surf quality in Bali follows the wind, and Bali's wind reverses between seasons. Surf guides explain that the dry season brings southeast trade winds, which blow offshore on the west-facing coast and hold the waves up cleanly. In the wet season the wind turns to the northwest, which is onshore on the west and offshore on the east.


## Offshore and onshore

An offshore wind blows from the land toward the sea, against the face of the incoming wave, and keeps it smooth. An onshore wind blows from sea to land and crumbles it. The same swell can produce clean surf on one side of the island and messy surf on the other on the same day.


## The seasons in the rainfall record

The two wind seasons match Bali's rainfall seasons. South Bali's climate normals show about 1 inch of rain in July and about 14 inches in January. The dry months are the trade-wind months, and the wet months are the northwest-wind months.

Which coast works in which season: Uluwatu & the Bukit · Seminyak & Canggu · Sanur · Amed. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## Dry season: the west coast

The dry season, from about April to October, is the season for Bali's west-facing breaks. Surf guides name the Bukit peninsula breaks around Uluwatu and Padang Padang, and the beaches at Kuta and Canggu further north. The same guides say the largest swells of the year arrive from June to August.


## What that means for a beginner

The months with the biggest swell are not the gentlest months to learn in. We give no wave heights and do not rate any named break for difficulty, because we have no official source for either. Our advice to a beginner is to book lessons with a surf school and let the instructor choose the beach and the hour.


## Crowds in the water

July and August are also the peak months for visitors generally, so the best-known breaks are shared with more people. A surfer who can travel in May, June or September gets the dry-season wind pattern with less company. Surf guides describe the prime season as starting around May.



## Wet season: the east coast

The wet season, from about November to March, moves the surf to Bali's east-facing coast. Surf guides name Keramas, Sanur and Nusa Dua as the areas that benefit, since the northwest wind that spoils the west coast blows offshore there. The famous west-coast breaks are less reliable in these months.


## Surfing in the rain

Rain itself does not stop surfing; the sea is warm and you are wet anyway. What changes is the water near river mouths after heavy rain. The CDC advises travelers in Indonesia to avoid swimming or wading in water that may be contaminated after flooding. Our advice is to give the water a day after a big downpour.


## Fewer people

The wet season is the quieter season for visitors overall, apart from the Christmas and New Year period. A surfer willing to base on the east side of the island can expect less company than in July. We have no visitor statistics by month, so we state the direction only.


## Choosing a base by season

A surfer's base in Bali should follow the season, because the drive between the two coasts takes time in the island's traffic. In the dry months, a base on the west side or on the Bukit peninsula puts the working breaks close. In the wet months, a base nearer Sanur or Nusa Dua does the same for the east side.


## Non-surfers in the group

A mixed group has a second question: what the people who are not surfing will do. The base that is right for the waves is not always the base that is right for restaurants, shopping or a quiet pool. That is a trade the group should make on purpose before booking.


## Transport with a board

Getting a surfboard around Bali means a scooter with a board rack or a car. Indonesia's traffic law requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and the State Department notes that a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia. A driver with a roof rack avoids the question.


## Safety in the water

Surfing in Bali carries the ordinary risks of reef and current, and medical care is not next to the beach. The State Department says routine medical care is available in Indonesia's major cities and that an injured traveler will likely need to go to a major city for appropriate care. Ambulances can take 45 minutes or longer.


## Insurance

The State Department strongly recommends supplemental insurance covering medical evacuation. Our advice is to check that your travel policy covers surfing by name, since some policies list water sports among their exclusions or charge extra for them. Ask before you travel, in writing.


## Sun

The CDC's advice for outdoor activity in Indonesia is to use sunscreen with an SPF of at least 15, wear protective clothing, and remember that sunlight reflects off sand and water. A long-sleeved rash guard solves most of it for a surfer.


## Planning a surf trip around the calendar

A surf trip to Bali starts with two dates and one decision: when you can travel, how long for, and which coast that month points to. The season sets the coast, and the coast narrows the choice of base. How the month, the coast and the base fit together is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

When is the best time to surf in Bali?There is surf all year. Surf guides describe the dry season, about April to October, as best for the west coast, with the largest swells from June to August.

Can you surf in Bali in the rainy season?Yes. Surf guides describe the east-facing coast, around Keramas, Sanur and Nusa Dua, as the side with cleaner conditions from about November to March.

Why does the surf change sides in Bali?The wind reverses. Southeast trade winds in the dry season blow offshore on the west coast; northwest winds in the wet season blow offshore on the east coast.

Is Bali good for beginner surfers?We do not rate individual breaks. Our advice to a beginner is to book lessons with a surf school and let the instructor choose the beach.



===== is-bali-expensive [money-and-costs / money-leak] 1230w =====
Is Bali Expensive? What Americans Actually Spend
Bali can be cheap or expensive, and the difference is mostly in choices the traveler makes. For an American, the fixed costs are the long-haul flight and a short list of government charges; everything on the ground has a wide range. We quote no daily budget, because prices move and we have no official figures, and we set out the costs that are certain and the ones that surprise people.


## The costs that are fixed

A few costs of a Bali trip are set by the Indonesian government and are the same for every American visitor. The tourist visa costs IDR 500,000. Bali's tourist levy is IDR 150,000 per person. Both are priced in rupiah, so the dollar amount moves with the exchange rate.

100 USD ≈ … IDR (enable JavaScript to see today's rate)

## If you stay longer than 30 days

A stay beyond the first 30 days needs a visa extension, with an official fee of IDR 500,000 per application and a visit to an immigration office. Overstaying without one is charged at IDR 1,000,000 per day, which makes it the most expensive mistake on this page.


## The flight

The flight is the largest single cost for most Americans, and it is the one Bali itself has no part in. There is no nonstop service from the United States, so every fare includes a connection. Fares change daily, and we quote none. When you compare Bali with a closer destination, the flight is where the comparison turns.


## What gets added to prices

Prices in Bali's restaurants and hotels are often not the final price. In Badung regency, which covers Kuta, Seminyak, Canggu, Uluwatu and Nusa Dua, the local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%. A separate service charge may be added on top.


## Service charge

A service charge is a business's own addition, not a tax. Under an Indonesian labor regulation covering hotels and hotel restaurants, it must be distributed to the staff. The regulation sets no percentage, so the rate is whatever the business has chosen. Look for a line at the foot of the menu stating whether prices include tax and service.


## Nightlife

Bars, nightclubs and spas fall under a different tax category. The 2023 tax regulations of Badung and Gianyar set a 40% local tax on that category, in force from January 2024. The rate was publicly contested and may have been amended, so look at how a venue states its prices instead of assuming a figure.



## What paying costs

How you pay adds to what you pay. The State Department says Indonesia has ATMs that accept US-issued bank cards, and that they may charge a currency conversion or transaction fee. It advises checking with your card issuer or bank for international transaction fees before you travel.


## Fees you can remove at home

A foreign transaction fee is set by your own bank, and it is the one cost on this page you can remove before leaving. Our advice is to carry a card that does not charge it. We have no official figures for ATM operator fees or withdrawal limits in Bali, so we quote none.


## Changing money

Bali's rules for visitors say to change money only at authorized money changers, identified by a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo with a QR code. A rate that looks much better than everyone else's is a reason for caution. Count the notes yourself before leaving the counter.


## Where the range is wide

Most of a Bali budget sits in three things with a wide range: where you sleep, how you get around, and what you eat and drink. Each can be done at a fraction of American prices or at something close to them. We give no price bands, because we have no official source for them and they change with season and location.


## Accommodation

A room in a family-run guesthouse and a private pool villa are both normal choices in Bali, and they are far apart in price. Bali's rules require visitors to stay in licensed accommodation. Compare the final price including tax and service, since two listings can differ by that margin alone.


## Transport

Getting around ranges from an app ride for a short hop to a car and driver for the day. The State Department notes that Grab and Gojek show the fare in the app before you confirm. A scooter is the cheapest option on paper; it also carries license and insurance questions that can make it the most expensive one.


## Food and drink

Local food at a simple eating house and an international menu at a beach club are different budgets. Alcohol is the item that most often surprises Americans on the bill, particularly imported wine and spirits. A traveler aged 21 or over may bring 1 liter of alcohol into the country.


## What a day in Bali costs

A day in Bali costs an American traveler roughly $30 to $50 on a budget, $80 to $150 at mid-range, and $240 or more for luxury, per person and not counting the international flight. These brackets are built from BudgetYourTrip's averages of what other visitors report spending, about $29, $80 and $239 a day for the three styles, and from 2026 travel-guide estimates, which run somewhat higher. They are planning ranges, not quotes.


## What the brackets cover

The brackets cover a bed, meals, getting around and an outing or two. Where you land inside a bracket depends mostly on the two items above that vary most, the room and the transport, and on the season. Prices in Bali are set in rupiah, so the dollar figures move with the exchange rate.


## Costs people forget

Three costs are missing from most Bali budgets. The first is travel insurance, which the State Department highly recommends for Indonesia. The second is medical care if something goes wrong: the State Department says hospitals often require payment or a sizable deposit upfront. The third is the connection at home, such as an airport hotel.


## Is it cheaper than home?

Our honest answer is that day-to-day spending in Bali can be well below what the same style of vacation costs in the United States, and that the flight takes back part of the difference. A longer trip spreads the flight over more days. A short trip makes the flight the main cost.


## The small percentages

None of the additions on this page is large alone. Tax, service, a conversion fee and an ATM charge are each a small percentage, and they land on the same dollar. How they stack across a whole trip is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is Bali expensive for Americans?Day-to-day costs have a wide range and can be low. The long-haul flight is the largest fixed cost, and tax and service are added to many restaurant and hotel prices.

What fees do tourists pay to enter Bali?The tourist visa costs IDR 500,000 and Bali's tourist levy is IDR 150,000 per person. The arrival card is free.

Is tax included in Bali restaurant prices?Not always. In Badung regency the local tax on restaurant food and drink is 10%, and a service charge may be added. Check the foot of the menu.

Do ATMs in Bali charge fees?The State Department says ATMs in Indonesia that accept US cards may charge a conversion or transaction fee. Check your own bank's international fees too.



===== getting-around-bali [getting-around / traffic-time] 1076w =====
How to Get Around Bali
Getting around Bali means going by road, in someone's car or on a motorbike. There is no rail network to rely on, and the choices for a visitor are a ride app, a taxi, a car with a driver, or a scooter. Distances are short on paper: it is about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud. The time those miles take is the thing to plan around.


## The four ways to get around

A visitor to Bali has four practical ways to travel: ride-hailing apps, licensed taxis, a hired car with a driver, and a rented scooter. Each suits a different kind of journey. The State Department describes licensed taxis and ride-hailing services as widely available in major Indonesian cities, which in Bali means the built-up south.


## Ride apps

Grab and Gojek are the ride-hailing apps in Bali. The State Department notes that they show driver identification, vehicle information and the fare in the app. For a short trip within the south, such as dinner in the next neighborhood, an app ride is the simplest thing to arrange.


## Taxis

Licensed taxis are the fallback when an app has no cars. The State Department describes Blue Bird taxis as generally considered the most reliable, and advises making sure the meter works and avoiding unlicensed taxis that solicit passengers directly. Its advice is to arrange transport through reputable companies or the apps.


## A car with a driver

A car hired with its driver for the day is a common way to see the island beyond your base. Agree when you book that the driver will wait at each stop and that the route is yours to set. We quote no prices, because they vary by season, distance and vehicle and we have no official source for them.


## A scooter

A rented scooter is the cheapest and most flexible option, and the one with legal conditions attached. Indonesia's traffic law requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven, with a separate class for motorcycles, and the State Department says a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia.


How far the island actually spreads: Seminyak & Canggu · Ubud · Uluwatu & the Bukit · Amed. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## How far apart things are

Bali is a small island by American standards, and its places are close together in miles. Measured on the road network, the airport is about 7 miles from Seminyak, 12 from Canggu, 8 from Nusa Dua, 11 from Sanur and 13 from Uluwatu. Ubud, in the middle of the island, is about 22 miles from the airport.


## Further out

The quieter parts of the island are further but still not far in miles. From the airport it is about 35 miles to the port of Padang Bai in the east, about 51 to Munduk in the central mountains, and about 61 to Amed on the northeast coast. From Ubud, Munduk is about 39 miles and Amed about 49.


## Why miles mislead

We give no drive times, because they change by the hour and the day and we will not freeze a number that will be wrong when you read it. Roads in the south are narrow and shared with large numbers of motorbikes, and the State Department notes that motorcycles pass on either side. Check a live map at the hour you actually intend to travel.


## Driving yourself

Driving a rental car yourself is possible in Bali, within the rules below. Bali's rules for foreign tourists require a valid international or national driving license, obedience to traffic rules, and roadworthy four-wheeled transport that officially belongs to a rental business or association.


## After an accident

The State Department's guidance after a traffic accident in Indonesia is to stay at the scene and notify the police, and it notes that in collisions between a car and a motorcycle the driver of the larger vehicle is often presumed responsible. That presumption is a reason many visitors prefer to leave the driving to someone local.


## Our advice

Our advice for a first trip is not to self-drive a car. A driver brings local knowledge of roads and parking, and takes the legal exposure of driving off your shoulders.


## Matching the method to the journey

The right way to get around Bali depends on the journey, and most trips use more than one. A sensible pattern for a first visit is app rides for short hops in the south, a pre-arranged car for the airport, and a driver for any day that leaves your base area. A scooter is for those licensed to ride one.


## From the airport

Grab operates pickups from a GrabCar Lounge at the International Arrival Terminal, booked in the app. A car arranged in advance with your hotel is the calmer choice after a long flight. The State Department's advice is to avoid unlicensed taxis that approach passengers at airports.


## Between islands

The smaller islands off Bali's coast are reached by boat. We have not been able to confirm fast-boat rules or schedules from an official source, so we give none here. The planning point is that a boat is a weather-dependent link, and it belongs in the middle of a trip, away from your flight home.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The question of how to get around kept returning to where you are staying. An app ride works because the base is in the south. A driver is needed because the day's sights are 30 miles away. A scooter looks attractive because the villa is far from everything. Transport is mostly a consequence of the base.

How the base, the day trips and the transport fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What is the best way to get around Bali?For a first visit: app rides for short trips in the south, and a car with a driver for days that leave your base area. A scooter requires a license valid for motorcycles.

Is there public transport in Bali?There is no rail network to rely on, and visitors travel by road. In practice that means ride apps, taxis, hired drivers and scooters.

How far is Ubud from the airport?About 22 miles by road. Drive times vary with traffic, so check a live map for the hour you will travel.

Can tourists drive in Bali?Bali's rules require a valid international or national driving license. The State Department says a US license alone is not recognized in Indonesia.



===== hiring-a-private-driver-in-bali [drivers-and-ride-apps / traffic-time] 1090w =====
Hiring a Private Driver in Bali: How It Works and What a Day Covers
Hiring a private driver is the easiest way to see Bali beyond your base, and for most first-time visitors it is the right choice for any full-day outing. You book a car and its driver for the day, set the route, and the driver waits at each stop. We quote no rates, because they vary and we have no official source; this page covers how the arrangement works and what to agree in advance.


## What hiring a driver means

Hiring a private driver in Bali means booking a car and the person who drives it for a block of time, often a day, to take you where you want to go. What you are buying, compared with a taxi or an app ride, is a car that stays with you, so confirm when you book that the driver will wait at each stop.


## Why it suits Bali

Bali's sights are spread across the island and its distances are short in miles. Ubud is about 6 miles from the Tegallalang rice terraces and about 27 from the crater rim at Kintamani, and about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud. A day can link several stops, and the driver handles the roads and the parking.


## What it is not

A driver is not automatically a licensed tour guide. Some drivers explain a great deal and some simply drive. If you want commentary on temples and history, ask for that when you book, and expect that a guide and a driver may be two different services.


## How to find a driver

Drivers in Bali are found in three ways: through your accommodation, through a recommendation from someone who has used one, and through booking platforms. The State Department's advice for Indonesia is to arrange transportation through reputable companies. Booking through your hotel puts a business you can return to between you and the arrangement.


## What the rules say about the vehicle

Bali's rules for foreign tourists refer to roadworthy four-wheeled transport that officially belongs to a rental business or association. Ask whether the car is a registered tourist transport vehicle. Asking the question is the only way to know; we cannot tell you that any particular arrangement satisfies the rule.


## Checks before you set off

The State Department's advice for any ride in Indonesia is to confirm the driver and vehicle information before entering. On the morning itself, confirm that the person and the car are the ones you booked. Seat belts for every passenger are worth asking about when you book, not when the car arrives.



## What to agree in advance

A driver booking in Bali goes smoothly when the terms are written down beforehand, in a message you can both refer to. These are our own suggestions, since no official checklist exists. Settle them before the day, not at the end of it.


- The total price, and the number of hours it covers.

- What happens if the day runs longer.

- Whether fuel, parking and tolls are included.

- The list of stops, and that you choose the restaurants and shops.

- The pickup time and place, and the driver's phone number.



## Entrance fees are separate

Sights that charge an entrance fee are a separate cost from the driver unless you have agreed otherwise. We list no amounts, because they change. Carry small rupiah notes for them, and dress for temples, where Bali's rules ask for modest dress.


## Paying

Agree how you will pay when you book, and have rupiah ready if the answer is cash. Bali's rules for visitors say to transact in rupiah and to change money only at authorized changers. Our view is that a tip for a good day is a decision for you; the agreed price is the price.


## Planning the day

A day with a driver in Bali works best with fewer stops than a map suggests. Road distances are short and travel is slow, and we give no drive times because they change by the hour. Our advice is three or four stops in one direction, chosen so the route does not double back through the busy south.


## Start early

Our advice is to leave early in the morning, so that the return drive falls before the evening. Ask your driver what order they would do the stops in; that is the local knowledge you are paying for.


## Ceremonies and closures

A procession or a temple ceremony can close a road, and a local driver is better placed than a visitor to know about it. Build slack into any day that ends with something fixed, such as a dinner reservation or a dance performance with a set start time.


## Transfers between bases

A driver is also the standard way to move between two bases, with luggage. A transfer can be turned into a sightseeing day by adding stops along the way, which saves a separate outing. Canggu to Uluwatu is about 23 miles; Ubud to Amed is about 49; Ubud to Munduk is about 39.


## The airport run

The drive to the airport on departure day is the one journey in Bali with a hard deadline. Book it with a driver you have already used, agree the pickup time the day before, and leave more margin than a live map suggests. A missed long-haul flight is the most expensive outcome a trip can have.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Booking a driver turned out to be the easy part. The real work was upstream: how many stops fit in a day, which direction they lie in, and how far your base is from them. A base in the far south puts the central highlands at the end of a long drive; a base in Ubud puts the southern beaches there instead.

How the base, the day routes and the transfer days fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How much does a private driver cost in Bali?We quote no rate, because prices vary by season, distance and vehicle and we have no official source. Agree the total price and the hours in writing before the day.

Do I need a driver in Bali?Not for short trips in the south, where ride apps work. For a full day that leaves your base area, a driver is the practical choice.

Are entrance fees included with a Bali driver?Do not assume so. Ask when you book whether entrance fees, parking and fuel are included in the price.

Should I tip my driver in Bali?Tipping is optional. The agreed price is the price, and a tip for a good day is your decision.



===== bali-traffic-real-drive-times [drivers-and-ride-apps / traffic-time] 1031w =====
Bali Traffic: Real Drive Times Between the Main Areas
Drive times in Bali cannot be read off the distance, and we have found no official table of them. The airport is only about 12 miles from Canggu and about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud, yet those drives can take several times what the mileage suggests. We publish distances, which are stable, and explain how to get a real time for your own journey.


## Why we print distances and not times

A drive time in Bali depends on the hour, the day, the weather and whether a ceremony has closed a road, so any figure in an article is wrong for most of the people reading it. We have no official source for travel times, and we will not freeze a number. Road distance does not change, and it is what we give.


## How to get a real time

The practical method is a live map on your phone, checked for the same hour and the same day of the week as the drive you are planning. Most map apps let you set a departure time. Do this before you book a dinner, a boat or a tour with a fixed start, and again the night before.


## Ask someone local

Your accommodation and your driver make the same journeys every week. Our advice is to ask them how long a specific drive takes at a specific hour, and to believe the longer of their answer and the map's. A live estimate cannot see a procession that will set out an hour from now.

Short distances, long drives: Seminyak & Canggu · Ubud · Uluwatu & the Bukit · Amed · Munduk & the north. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## The distances, in miles

Road distances between Bali's main areas are short, and that is the source of the surprise. Measured on the road network, the airport is about 4 miles from Kuta, 7 from Seminyak, 12 from Canggu, 8 from Nusa Dua, 11 from Sanur and 13 from Uluwatu. A newcomer reads those figures as ten-minute drives.


## From Ubud

Ubud sits inland, about 22 miles from the airport. From Ubud it is about 6 miles to Tegallalang, 14 to Sanur, 19 to Canggu, 22 to Sidemen, 27 to Kintamani, 34 to Uluwatu, 39 to Munduk and 49 to Amed. Every one of those is a short hop on an American highway and a real journey in Bali.


## The long ones

The longest drives a visitor is likely to make run across the island. The airport to Amed is about 61 miles and to Lovina on the north coast about 62. Amed to Munduk, from the northeast coast to the central mountains, is about 93 miles, which is a full day's travel with stops.



## What slows the roads

Bali's roads are slow for structural reasons, not because of occasional bad luck. The State Department notes that roads in Indonesia are shared with many motorcycles, which pass on either side. The island's main roads in the south run through continuous built-up areas, and the routes into the hills are narrow and winding.


## Time of day

Traffic follows the day's rhythm, as it does anywhere. We have no official traffic data, so we name no rush hours. Our advice is to assume that the late afternoon, when beachgoers, commuters and sunset-seekers are all moving, is a poor time to cross the south, and to check a live map before relying on that or doubting it.


## Ceremonies

A procession or a temple ceremony can close a road for a period, and there is no timetable a visitor can consult. This is part of life on the island and no cause for irritation. It is a reason to keep slack before anything with a fixed start.


## Rain

Heavy rain slows everything. South Bali's climate record shows about 14 inches of rain in January against about 1 inch in July, so a drive in the wet months deserves more margin than the same drive in the dry ones.


## What it means for a day's plan

The practical effect of Bali's traffic is that a day holds fewer stops than a map suggests. Our advice is to plan each day around one area, to group sights that lie in the same direction, and to count the drive back as part of the day. Three stops done calmly beat six done from a car window.


## Day trips from the south

A base in the far south puts the central highlands a long way off: Uluwatu to Ubud is about 34 miles, and Munduk is further still. A day trip of that length is possible and tiring. Travelers who want several days in the hills usually do better to move base than to commute.


## Sunset

Sunset draws people to the same west-facing spots at the same hour. If you want to be somewhere for it, our advice is to arrive well ahead and stay for dinner nearby, so that you are not on the road with everyone else when it ends.


## The drive that matters most

The drive to the airport on departure day is the one with a penalty for being late. The airport is about 22 miles from Ubud and about 12 from Canggu, and both drives pass through the busiest part of the island. Check a live map for your actual departure hour the day before, and leave earlier than it says.


## Why the last night's location matters

Where you sleep the night before your flight decides how long that final drive is. How drive times shape the choice of base, the order of the days and the last night of the trip is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How long does it take to drive from the airport to Ubud?The distance is about 22 miles. The time varies widely with the hour and the day, so check a live map for when you will travel.

How far is Canggu from the airport?About 12 miles by road.

Why is traffic so bad in Bali?Roads are narrow, run through built-up areas, and are shared with large numbers of motorbikes. Ceremonies and rain add delays.

How many places can I visit in a day in Bali?Our advice is three or four stops in one direction. Short distances take much longer than the mileage suggests.



===== scooter-vs-driver-in-bali [scooters / scooter-liability] 1032w =====
Scooter vs. Driver in Bali
For most American visitors, a driver is the better choice in Bali and a scooter is the riskier one. A scooter is cheap and flexible, and riding one legally requires a license that covers motorcycles, which most Americans do not hold. A driver costs more per day and removes the license question and the navigation. The answer changes for an experienced, licensed rider.


## The legal difference

The first difference between a scooter and a driver in Bali is legal. Indonesia's traffic law requires every rider to hold a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and it treats motorcycles as their own class. The State Department adds that a US driver's license alone is not recognized in Indonesia.


## What that means for an American

An American who holds a motorcycle endorsement at home and carries an International Driving Permit that reflects it has the paperwork the law describes. An American with a car license only does not, because a car entitlement does not match a motorcycle. The permit is a translation of the license you have, not an upgrade to it.


## The penalty

Riding without the required license carries up to 4 months' detention or a fine of IDR 1,000,000 for riding without the required license under the traffic law. Those are the statutory maximums. The US Consular Agency in Bali reports a current enforcement campaign that includes traffic and vehicle violations.


## A driver has none of this

A passenger in a hired car needs no license of any kind. The license requirement falls on the person driving. That is the plainest argument for a driver, and for a visitor without a motorcycle license our view is that it settles the matter.


## The safety difference

The second difference is physical. A scooter rider in Bali shares narrow roads with cars, trucks and a great many other motorbikes, which the State Department notes pass on either side. Indonesian law requires a helmet for the rider and the passenger. A car puts a steel body between you and the same traffic.


## After a crash

The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to go to a major city for appropriate medical care, that ambulances can take 45 minutes or longer, and that hospitals often require payment or a sizable deposit upfront. Those are the stakes of any road injury, and a rider has less protection than a car passenger.


## Experience counts

Bali is a hard place to learn to ride. Our advice is blunt: if you have never ridden a motorbike at home, do not start here. The combination of unfamiliar traffic, left-hand driving, heat and jet lag is a poor classroom.



## The insurance difference

The third difference shows up only when something goes wrong. A travel policy may make cover for a motorbike accident depend on the rider being licensed for that vehicle. We cannot tell you what your policy says. The question to put to your insurer in writing is whether you are covered riding a scooter in Indonesia with the license you hold.


## Damage to the scooter

Damage to a rented scooter is a matter between you and the rental shop, on whatever terms you agreed. We have no official source on how rental shops handle it, so we describe no practice. Photograph the scooter from every side before you ride away, and read anything you are asked to sign.


## Passengers in a hired car

As a passenger with a driver, your exposure is that of any passenger in any car. Your own travel insurance still matters for medical cover, which the State Department highly recommends for Indonesia.


## The cost and convenience difference

The case for a scooter is cost and freedom. A scooter rented by the day costs a small fraction of a car with a driver, and it goes where you want when you want, through traffic a car sits in. We quote no prices for either, since they vary and we have no official source.


## Where a scooter makes sense

A scooter suits a licensed, experienced rider making short local trips from a base: to the beach, to a cafe, to dinner. Bali's rules for visitors require a valid license, a helmet, and no riding under the influence of alcohol or drugs, and they prohibit carrying more passengers than the vehicle allows.


## Where a driver makes sense

A driver suits full days out, anything involving luggage, any group with children, and any journey after dark or in rain. The road distances are short, at about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud, and the hours on the road are long, and they pass more easily in a back seat.


## The middle options

The choice is not only between the two. Ride apps cover short hops in the south at a fare shown in advance, and both Grab and Gojek offer rides as a passenger on a motorbike, which gets you through traffic without your holding the handlebars or the liability.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The scooter question was never about scooters. It ran through the license in your wallet, the wording of an insurance policy bought at home, the distance from your base to a hospital, and how far your villa is from the places you want to go. A remote base makes a scooter tempting; a central one makes it unnecessary.

How the license, the insurance and the choice of base fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Should I rent a scooter or hire a driver in Bali?Hire a driver unless you hold a license that covers motorcycles and have real riding experience. Indonesian law requires a license matching the vehicle.

Can I ride a scooter in Bali with a US license?The State Department says a US license alone is not recognized in Indonesia, and the law requires a license matching the vehicle type, with a separate class for motorcycles.

Is a scooter cheaper than a driver in Bali?Yes, by the day. The comparison changes once license, insurance and accident risk are counted.

Is it safe to ride a scooter in Bali?Bali's roads are narrow and crowded with motorbikes. Our advice is not to ride if you have no experience at home.



===== bali-departure-day-how-early-to-leave [flights-and-arrival / traffic-time] 1089w =====
Departure Day in Bali: How Early to Leave for the Airport
On departure day, leave for Bali's airport earlier than the distance suggests. The airport is only about 12 miles from Canggu and about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud, and the drive passes through the busiest roads on the island. Work backward from your airline's check-in deadline, add a live-map estimate for your actual hour, and then add a margin on top.


## How to work out your leaving time

The time to leave for Bali's airport is built from three numbers: when your airline wants you at check-in, how long the drive takes at that hour, and a margin for what a map cannot see. We give none of the three as a fixed figure, because the first is your airline's and the second changes by the hour.


## Start from the airline

Your airline sets the check-in and bag-drop deadlines for an international flight, and they are on your booking or its website. Use the airline's own recommended arrival time as the starting point. A long-haul international departure with checked bags is the case that needs the most time at the airport.


## Add the drive

Check a live map for the drive from your accommodation to the airport, set to the hour and the day of the week you will be traveling. Do it the day before. A drive that looks short at 10 in the morning can look very different in the late afternoon.


## Add a margin

Our advice is to add a generous margin on top of the map's estimate, and to make it larger the further you are from the airport. A live estimate knows about traffic now. It does not know about a procession, a downpour or an accident an hour from now.


## How far the airport is

Bali's airport sits on the narrow neck of land in the south, and most bases are within a short distance in miles. By road it is about 4 miles from Kuta, 7 from Seminyak, 8 from Nusa Dua, 11 from Sanur, 12 from Canggu and 13 from Uluwatu. Ubud is about 22 miles away.


## Further bases

From the east and the north the drive is a journey in itself. The port of Padang Bai is about 35 miles from the airport, Munduk about 51, and Amed about 61. A flight home on the same day as a drive from one of those needs a very early start, or a different plan.



## What can go wrong on the way

The road to Bali's airport is exposed to the same delays as every other road on the island, with the difference that this drive has a deadline. The State Department notes that Indonesia's roads are shared with many motorcycles. Rain slows traffic, and a ceremony can close a road for a period.


## Rainy season

South Bali's climate record shows about 13 to 14 inches of rain a month in December and January. A departure in the wet months deserves a bigger margin than one in July or August. Our advice is to treat the wet-season afternoon as the slowest case and plan for it.


## Nyepi

One date needs checking before any other. Nyepi, the day of silence, falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, and press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe regular flights halting for 24 hours that year. The 2027 arrangements had not been announced when we wrote this, so our advice is to avoid a departure on or next to that date and to confirm with your airline.


## Arranging the ride

The ride to the airport should be the most firmly booked journey of the trip. Our advice is to arrange it the day before with a driver or through your accommodation, to confirm the pickup time in writing, and to have the driver's phone number. An app ride requested at the last minute is a gamble on that one morning.


## Use someone you know

A driver you have already used during the trip has shown up on time before. That record is worth more than a small saving. If you rely on a ride app, check for available cars well before you need to leave, and have a taxi as the fallback.


## If you returned a scooter

Travelers who rented a scooter should return it the day before the flight, not on the morning. That leaves time to settle any question about its condition without a plane to catch.


## At the airport

Leaving Indonesia is simpler than entering it, and two rules still apply. You must leave on the same passport you entered with; the State Department says a replaced passport needs a special pass from immigration before departure. And your stay must be inside the visa's limit, since overstaying is charged at IDR 1,000,000 per day.


## Cash on the way out

Taking rupiah worth IDR 100,000,000 or more out of Indonesia requires a permit from Bank Indonesia, a threshold well above what a vacationer carries. The practical matter is leftover small notes: our advice is to spend them or change them before you leave Indonesia.


## Liquids and souvenirs

Anything you bring home has to enter the United States under American rules. The State Department notes that counterfeit and pirated goods may have to be surrendered or may bring fines on return, and that medicine bought abroad must meet US requirements.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The hour you leave for the airport was decided days earlier, by where you chose to spend the last night. A final night near the airport makes departure day short and dull. A final night in the hills or on another island makes it long and tense. The leaving time is an output of the itinerary, not a separate choice.

How the last night, the last full day and the flight fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How early should I leave for Bali airport?Start from your airline's check-in deadline, add a live-map drive time for your actual hour, and add a generous margin. We give no fixed figure because drive times vary.

How far is Seminyak from Bali airport?About 7 miles by road.

Can I fly out of Bali on Nyepi?Press reports of the 2026 arrangements describe regular flights halting for 24 hours. The 2027 arrangements were not yet announced when we wrote this, so avoid that date if you can and confirm with your airline.

Should I stay near the airport on my last night?It makes departure day easier. The further your last night is from the airport, the larger the margin you need.



===== bali-areas-explained [where-to-go / base-split] 1139w =====
Bali's Areas Explained: Which Part of the Island Is Which
Bali divides into six parts for a visitor: the built-up south, Ubud and the central hills, the quiet east, the north coast, the far west, and three small islands off the southeast shore. The island is about 89 miles from east to west and 50 from north to south. Most first trips happen in the south and around Ubud, which are about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud apart.


## The six parts of Bali

Bali's regions, as travel guides divide them, are South Bali, Central Bali, East Bali, North Bali, West Bali and the southeastern islands. The south holds the airport and most of the hotels. The center holds Ubud and the mountain lakes. The east and north are quieter coasts, and the west is the least visited.


## Why the map misleads

The island is small, and a first-time visitor reads the map as a place where everything is a short drive away. In miles that is true. In hours it is not, because the roads are narrow and busy. Choosing where to stay in Bali is really choosing which of these regions you will see without a long drive.

The island, by where you would sleep: Seminyak & Canggu · Uluwatu & the Bukit · Sanur · Ubud · Sidemen · Amed · Munduk & the north · Nusa Lembongan · Nusa Penida. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## South Bali: the beach resorts

South Bali is the most visited part of the island, and it is several different places sharing one coast. Travel guides describe Kuta as the most heavily developed area, Seminyak as quieter and more upscale, Canggu as a fast-developing stretch of surf beaches and rice fields, and Sanur and Nusa Dua as the calmer resort areas on the other shore.


## The west-facing strip

Kuta, Legian, Seminyak and Canggu run north from the airport along the west-facing coast, in that order. The airport is about 4 miles from Kuta, 7 from Seminyak and 12 from Canggu. This is where the sunset beaches, the restaurants and the nightlife are concentrated, and where the traffic is heaviest.


## The Bukit peninsula

The Bukit is the limestone peninsula at the island's southern tip, below the airport. Travel guides describe it as rugged, with white sand beaches under cliffs and the temple of Uluwatu on a cliff edge. Uluwatu is about 13 miles from the airport. Jimbaran, at the neck of the peninsula, is known for its sheltered beach.


## The east-facing resorts

Sanur and Nusa Dua face east, away from the sunset coast. Travel guides describe Sanur as a mature resort town popular with families and older visitors, and Nusa Dua as an enclave of high-end resorts with little traffic. Sanur is about 11 miles from the airport and Nusa Dua about 8. Boats to the small islands leave from Sanur.



## Central Bali: Ubud and the hills

Central Bali is the island's cultural heart, in the words of the travel guides, and it contains the central mountain range. Ubud is its main town, sitting at about 200 meters above sea level and regarded as the center of Balinese arts. It is inland, with no beach, and cooler than the coast.


## Around Ubud

Ubud is surrounded by villages and rice terraces. The Tegallalang terraces are about 6 miles to the north, and the crater rim at Kintamani, overlooking the active volcano Mount Batur, is about 27 miles away. Bali's rice-terrace landscape and its water temples are a UNESCO World Heritage site, inscribed in 2012.


## The mountain lakes

Higher up, in the central mountains, travel guides describe Bedugul as an area of lakes, a botanical garden and the lakeside temple of Ulun Danu Bratan. Munduk lies further on, about 39 miles from Ubud by road. The air is cooler at altitude, and the pace is slower.


## East and North Bali: the quiet coasts

East Bali and North Bali are the island's quieter coasts, described by travel guides as laid-back fishing villages and black sand beaches under the island's highest mountain, Mount Agung. They are further from the airport: Amed, on the northeast coast, is about 61 miles away, and Lovina, on the north coast, about 62.


## East Bali

The east includes Sidemen, a valley of rice terraces about 22 miles from Ubud; Candidasa, a quiet coastal town; Padang Bai, the small port where ferries to Lombok leave; and Amed, a strip of fishing villages known for coral reefs and diving. Pura Besakih, on the slopes of Mount Agung, is regarded by Balinese Hindus as their mother temple.


## North Bali

The north coast centers on Lovina, a string of villages with black sand beaches that is known for dawn boat trips to see dolphins, and on Singaraja, the largest town on that coast and the island's former colonial capital. Travel guides describe the north as quiet in comparison with the south.


## West Bali

The far west holds West Bali National Park and the ferry crossing to Java. It is the region fewest visitors reach, and it is a long drive from the airport. We mention it for completeness; a first trip of one or two weeks rarely includes it.


## The southeastern islands

Three islands lie off Bali's southeast coast: Nusa Penida, Nusa Lembongan and Nusa Ceningan. Nusa Penida is the largest, at about 247 square kilometers, with rough, hilly roads and famous cliff viewpoints. Nusa Lembongan is small, about 8 square kilometers, and a bridge for walkers and motorbikes links it to tiny Ceningan.


## Getting to them

The islands are reached by boat, mainly from Sanur. Travel guides give the crossing from Sanur as roughly 30 to 45 minutes; check with the operator, since we have no official schedule. A boat is the one link in a Bali trip that depends on the sea.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Describing the regions kept running into distance. The beaches and the rice terraces are in different regions. The surf, the temples, the diving and the quiet are in different regions again. No single base reaches all of them without long days on the road, so knowing the regions leads straight to a harder question: which ones, and from where.

How the regions combine into a workable set of bases is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

What are the main areas of Bali?South Bali with its beach resorts, Ubud and the central hills, East Bali, North Bali, West Bali, and the southeastern islands of Nusa Penida, Lembongan and Ceningan.

How big is Bali?About 89 miles from east to west and 50 miles from north to south.

Which part of Bali has the beaches?The south has the main resort beaches, on both the west-facing and east-facing coasts. The east and north have quieter black sand beaches.

Is Ubud near the beach?No. Ubud is inland in the central hills, about 14 miles by road from Sanur on the coast.



===== ubud-guide [ubud / base-split] 1153w =====
Ubud: What It Is Actually Like and Who It Suits
Ubud is an inland town in the hills of central Bali, regarded as the island's center for arts and culture. It has rice terraces, temples, museums and a monkey forest, and no beach. It suits travelers who want culture, walking and cooler air, and it is the natural base for the center and east of the island. The town center is busy, and the quiet people imagine is in the villages around it.


## What Ubud is

Ubud is a town in central Bali, at about 200 meters above sea level, which makes it cooler than the coast. Travel guides describe it as the cultural center of Bali and its arts and crafts hub. What looks like one town is made up of fourteen villages, each run by its own village committee, known as a banjar.

Map: Ubud Palace, Sacred Monkey Forest, Campuhan Ridge Walk, Tegallalang rice terraces, Tirta Empul, Goa Gajah.

## Where it is

Ubud lies inland, about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud by road. It is about 14 miles from the coast at Sanur and about 19 miles from Canggu. There is no beach. A day at the sea from Ubud is a drive each way, which is a good reason to treat Ubud and the beach as separate parts of a trip.


## Which regency

Ubud is in Gianyar regency, a different local government from the beach resorts of the south, which are mostly in Badung. The practical consequence is small: local tax on restaurant and hotel bills is set by each regency, and Gianyar's rate matches Badung's.


## How Ubud is laid out

Central Ubud is built around three main streets: Jalan Raya Ubud, Jalan Monkey Forest and Jalan Hanoman. They meet near the market and the palace, which mark the center of town. The Sacred Monkey Forest lies at the southern end of the street named after it. This core can be covered on foot.


## The surrounding villages

The villages around the center each have their own character. Campuhan, Sanggingan and Penestanan lie to the north and west; Nyuh Kuning and Pengosekan to the south; Peliatan to the east. Many of the places described as "Ubud" in a hotel listing are in one of these villages, a walk or a short ride from the center.


## Traffic in the center

Central Ubud is walkable, and it is congested. Travel guides describe the center as crowded with visitors and the traffic as very bad. The streets are narrow and were not built for the number of cars and motorbikes that now use them. Staying within walking distance of what you want to do saves a great deal of time.



## What there is to see

Ubud's sights divide into those in the town and those a drive away. In town are the market, the palace, the museums and the Sacred Monkey Forest. The Campuhan ridge walk starts on the western edge of the center. Outside town are the cave temple of Goa Gajah to the east and the spring temple of Tirta Empul to the north.


## Rice terraces

The Tegallalang rice terraces are about 6 miles northeast of central Ubud. Bali's rice-terrace landscape and its water temples are a UNESCO World Heritage site, built on a cooperative irrigation system called subak that dates back to the 9th century. The listing covers five terrace areas and their temples across the island.


## Temples and dress

Temple visits around Ubud follow Bali's rules for visitors, which require modest dress in sacred places and bar tourists from the inner courtyards unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress. Our advice is to carry a sarong and a sash, and to cover shoulders and knees.


## The monkeys

The Sacred Monkey Forest is a temple site with a large resident population of macaques. The CDC says dogs infected with rabies are commonly found in Indonesia, and its general advice to travelers there is to keep away from animals. Our advice at the forest is to carry no food, keep bags closed, and see a doctor promptly after any bite or scratch.


## Day trips from Ubud

Ubud is the best-placed base on the island for the center and the east. By road it is about 27 miles to the crater rim at Kintamani, about 22 to the Sidemen valley, about 39 to Munduk in the mountains and about 49 to Amed on the northeast coast. We give no drive times, since they vary by hour.


## What is far from Ubud

The southern beaches are the weak point. Uluwatu is about 34 miles from Ubud, through the busiest roads on the island. A traveler who wants several beach days and several days of temples and terraces will find it easier to split the stay than to commute between the two.


## Who Ubud suits

Ubud suits travelers interested in culture, crafts, food, yoga and walking, and anyone who wants a few days away from the beach. Our view is that it suits couples and solo travelers especially well. It is a weaker fit for a trip that is mainly about swimming in the sea, surfing or nightlife.


## How long to stay

Our advice is at least three nights, since a day is lost to arriving and the sights lie in several directions. Two nights give one full day, which is enough for the town and not for the surroundings. Travelers who like the pace often wish they had booked more.


## Getting around

Inside the center, walk. For the surrounding sights, hire a car and driver for the day or use a ride app for short hops. A scooter requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven under Indonesian law, which for a motorbike means a motorcycle license.


## Practical notes

Ubud is cooler than the coast, at about 200 meters of altitude, and evenings can call for a light layer. The climate figures published for south Bali come from a station at sea level and do not describe Ubud, so we give no rainfall numbers for the town. Pack a rain jacket in the wet months.


## Money

Bali's rules for visitors say to change money only at authorized changers, which display a license number and a Bank Indonesia logo with a QR code. The State Department advises using ATMs in secure locations such as major bank branches.



## FAQ

Is Ubud worth visiting?Yes, for culture, rice terraces, temples and walking. It has no beach, so it suits a trip that is not only about the sea.

How far is Ubud from the beach?About 14 miles by road from Sanur and about 19 from Canggu.

How many days do you need in Ubud?Our advice is at least three nights, which gives two full days for the town and its surroundings.

Is Ubud walkable?The center is, around three main streets. The surrounding villages and the sights outside town need a car, a driver or a ride app.



===== canggu-seminyak-legian-kuta-explained [seminyak-canggu-kuta / base-split] 1134w =====
Canggu, Seminyak, Legian and Kuta: One Coast, Four Different Stays
Kuta, Legian, Seminyak and Canggu are four neighboring areas on one stretch of Bali's west-facing coast, running north from the airport in that order. Kuta is the most developed and the closest to the airport, Legian sits between Kuta and Seminyak, Seminyak is the more upscale area for dining and villas, and Canggu is the newer, fast-developing surf area with rice paddies behind it.


## One coast, four areas

The four areas share a single long beach facing west, and they blend into one another on the ground. Measured by road from the airport, Kuta is about 4 miles away, Seminyak about 7 and Canggu about 12. Legian lies between Kuta and Seminyak. The whole strip is inside Badung regency.

Map: Kuta Beach, Legian Beach, Seminyak Beach, Canggu (Batu Bolong Beach).

## What they have in common

All four face the sunset, all four have surf, and all four are busy. Surf guides describe this west-facing coast as best in the dry season, from about April to October, when the trade winds blow offshore. In Badung, the local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%.


## What separates them

The difference between the four is character and price level, not geography. Travel guides place them on a line from the most developed and budget-oriented at the Kuta end to the more upscale at Seminyak, with Canggu as the fast-growing newcomer to the north. Which one suits you depends on what you want within walking distance.


## Kuta

Kuta is the original beach resort of Bali and the closest to the airport. Travel guides describe it as by far the most heavily developed area on the island, with a great deal of shopping and nightlife. It is the practical choice for a first or last night, at about 4 miles from the terminal.


## Who Kuta suits

Kuta suits travelers who want low prices, a lively scene and a short transfer. Our view is that it is the least distinctive of the four for a longer stay, since much of it could be a resort strip anywhere. Travelers looking for quiet or for Balinese atmosphere will be happier elsewhere.


## Legian

Legian is the stretch of beach between Kuta and Seminyak, and it works as a middle ground between them. Travel guides describe it as a popular beach town and a good place to learn to surf, with lessons offered on the beach. It is close enough to both neighbors to reach either on a short ride.


## Who Legian suits

Legian suits beginners who want surf lessons on a sandy beach and travelers who want to be near Kuta's energy without being in the middle of it. Our advice to a first-time surfer is to book a lesson with a school and let the instructor choose the spot and the hour.



## Seminyak

Seminyak is the upscale end of the strip. Travel guides describe it as quieter than Kuta, with more upmarket beachside resorts and villas, sophisticated dining and fashionable bars. It is about 7 miles from the airport. Much of what visitors picture as stylish Bali, in the form of restaurants, shops and spas, is here.


## Who Seminyak suits

Seminyak suits couples and groups who want good restaurants, shopping and a beach within a short walk, and who are comfortable with resort prices. It has less surf culture than Canggu and less nightlife chaos than Kuta. Our view is that it is the easiest of the four for a first visit.


## Bills in Seminyak

Restaurant and hotel prices here are often quoted before additions. The local tax is 10%, and a service charge may be added separately by the business. Bars and nightclubs fall under a higher entertainment tax category that was set at 40% in the regency's 2023 regulations; that rate was contested and may have changed, so read how a venue states its prices.


## Canggu

Canggu lies north of Seminyak, about 12 miles from the airport. Travel guides describe it as an area of black sand beaches, surfing and rice paddies that is developing rapidly. Its draw is surf and a newer, less built-up setting than Seminyak.


## Who Canggu suits

Our view is that Canggu suits surfers and longer-stay visitors. The beaches are black sand, and the sea is a surf beach.


## Canggu's position

Canggu is the furthest of the four from the airport and from the Bukit peninsula: Uluwatu is about 23 miles away by road. It is the closest to the sea temple of Tanah Lot, about 7 miles up the coast. Ubud is about 19 miles inland.


## Getting between them

The four areas are close together in miles and slow to move between. Kuta to Seminyak is about 4 miles and Seminyak to Canggu about 7. We give no drive times, because they change by the hour. Check a live map before planning dinner in one area and a hotel in another.


## How to move

Ride apps are the simplest way to travel along the strip. The State Department notes that Grab and Gojek show the fare in the app before you confirm. A scooter is common here and legal only for a rider who holds a license matching the type of vehicle driven.


## Walking

Within each area, the beach itself is the pleasant walking route. Our advice is to choose accommodation by what is around it on foot, since a place that looks central on a map can be a ride away from everything you came for.


## Staying safe on the strip

The busy south is where the State Department's general cautions for Indonesia apply most directly. It describes pickpocketing and snatch-and-grab robberies as common and advises being mindful of phones near roadways. It also reports drink spiking in Bali and advises watching drinks being prepared and never leaving one unattended.


## The sea

This is an open surf coast. We have no official source on currents or lifeguard coverage at each beach, so we state none. Our advice is to swim only where lifeguard flags mark a safe area, and to treat any unflagged stretch as a surf beach, not a swimming beach.



## FAQ

What is the difference between Seminyak and Canggu?Seminyak is the more upscale area for dining, shopping and villas. Canggu, further north, is a fast-developing area of black sand surf beaches and rice paddies.

Is Kuta or Seminyak better?Kuta is closer to the airport, cheaper and more developed. Seminyak is quieter and more upscale. Our view is that Seminyak is the easier base for a first visit.

How far is Canggu from Seminyak?About 7 miles by road. Check a live map for the time, which varies with traffic.

Where is Legian?Between Kuta and Seminyak on the same beach. Travel guides describe it as a good place to learn to surf.



===== uluwatu-and-the-bukit-guide [uluwatu / base-split] 1077w =====
Uluwatu and the Bukit Peninsula: Cliffs, Surf and the Drive In
The Bukit is the limestone peninsula at Bali's southern tip, and Uluwatu is its best-known corner, named for the temple that stands on a cliff above the sea. The area is known for surf breaks, white sand beaches below cliffs, and sunsets. It is about 13 miles from the airport, and it suits surfers and travelers who want scenery and space more than a walkable town.


## What the Bukit is

The Bukit peninsula hangs below the rest of Bali, joined to it by a narrow neck of land where the airport sits. Travel guides describe it as the rugged southernmost part of the island. Uluwatu is on its southwestern corner, Jimbaran at its neck on the west side, and Nusa Dua on its eastern shore.

Map: Uluwatu Temple, Padang Padang Beach, Jimbaran Beach, Nusa Dua Beach.

## How it differs from the rest of the south

The Bukit is limestone, and its coast is cliffs with beaches at the bottom, reached by steps. That geography gives the views the area is known for, and it means that a beach day here usually involves a climb.


## The regency

The peninsula is part of Badung regency, like Seminyak and Canggu. The local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%, with a service charge added separately where a business chooses to add one.


## Uluwatu Temple

Uluwatu Temple is the reason the area's name is known. Travel guides describe it as perched on a cliff about 75 meters above the waves. It is a working temple, and Bali's rules for visitors apply: modest dress is required in sacred places, and tourists may not enter the inner courtyards unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress.


## Visiting

The temple grounds along the cliff edge are the part visitors walk through. Our advice is to wear clothing that covers shoulders and knees and to accept the sarong and sash offered at the entrance. Keep sunglasses, phones and food secure if there are monkeys about.


## Sunset and the dance

The temple is on the west-facing coast, so sunset is the popular hour. A kecak dance is staged for visitors at the temple in the evening; we have not sourced its times or ticket prices, so check locally on the day.



## Beaches and surf

The Bukit's beaches are white sand coves at the foot of cliffs, which sets them apart from the long dark-sand beaches further north. Travel guides name Padang Padang among them. Surf guides list the breaks around Uluwatu and Padang Padang among the west-facing spots that are at their best in the dry season.


## When the surf works

Surf guides describe the dry season, from about April to October, as the time when southeast trade winds blow offshore on this coast, with the largest swells arriving from June to August. We rate no break for difficulty. Many of the Bukit's waves break over reef, so our advice to a beginner is to take lessons elsewhere first.


## Swimming

Reef, tide and swell decide whether a Bukit beach is swimmable on a given day. We have no official source on conditions at each beach, so we describe none as safe. Ask at your accommodation about the tide before you walk down, since some coves have little sand at high water.


## The steps

Most Bukit beaches are reached by long flights of steps down the cliff. That is easy going down and hot work coming back. Travelers with small children, heavy bags or limited mobility should ask how many steps a beach involves before choosing it.


## Jimbaran and Nusa Dua

The Bukit has two gentler shores. Jimbaran, on the west side near the airport, is described by travel guides as having a sheltered beach and seafood restaurants on the sand. Nusa Dua, on the east side, is an enclave of high-end resorts with a long beach. Uluwatu is about 8 miles from Jimbaran and about 12 from Nusa Dua.


## Getting there and around

Uluwatu is about 13 miles from the airport by road, about 17 from Seminyak and about 23 from Canggu. Ubud is about 34 miles away. The peninsula has few through roads, and the drive in and out passes the airport area. We give no drive times; check a live map for your hour.


## Getting around the peninsula
