The Bukit is spread out, and little of it is walkable. Beaches, restaurants and viewpoints are a drive apart. A car and driver for the day, or a ride app where one is available, is the practical way to move. A scooter requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven.


## Ride apps here

We have no official source on ride-app coverage in the Bukit, and it is less built-up than Seminyak or Kuta. Our advice is to arrange your ride back before you set out for a remote beach or the temple at sunset, when many people are leaving at once.


## Who the Bukit suits

The Bukit suits surfers, couples and anyone who wants cliff views, quiet and a villa with space around it. It is a weaker fit for travelers who want to walk to a choice of restaurants, for families needing calm shallow water, and for anyone planning many day trips to central Bali.


## How long to stay

Our advice is two to four nights for most trips: enough for the temple, a few beaches and some unhurried time. As a day trip from Seminyak or Canggu, the Bukit is possible and involves a long return drive in the evening.


## Medical care

The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to go to a major city for appropriate medical care, and that ambulances can take 45 minutes or longer. On a reef-break coast reached by cliff steps, that is worth knowing before you paddle out.



## FAQ

Where is Uluwatu?On the southwestern corner of the Bukit peninsula at Bali's southern tip, about 13 miles by road from the airport.

Is Uluwatu good for beginner surfers?We rate no individual break. Many Bukit waves break over reef, so our advice to a beginner is to take lessons at a sandy beach first.

Can you swim at Uluwatu's beaches?It depends on tide, reef and swell on the day. Ask locally before walking down, since conditions vary by cove.

How far is Uluwatu from Seminyak?About 17 miles by road. Check a live map for the time, which varies with traffic.



===== sanur-and-nusa-dua-guide [sanur-nusa-dua / base-split] 1100w =====
Sanur and Nusa Dua: The Calm Coast
Sanur and Nusa Dua are the calm side of south Bali: two resort areas on the east-facing coast, away from the surf and the nightlife of Kuta and Seminyak. Sanur is a long-established beach town; Nusa Dua is an enclave of large resorts. Both suit families and travelers who want quiet, and Sanur is the main port for boats to the Nusa islands.


## Where they are

Sanur and Nusa Dua both face east, on the opposite shore from Bali's sunset beaches. Sanur is about 11 miles from the airport by road, on the edge of the capital, Denpasar. Nusa Dua is about 8 miles from the airport, on the eastern side of the Bukit peninsula. The two are about 13 miles apart.

Map: Sanur beachfront, Sanur harbor, Nusa Dua Beach.

## The east-facing coast

Facing east changes the day. The sun rises over the sea here and sets behind the land, so this coast is for sunrise walks, not sunset drinks on the beach. Surf guides describe the east-facing coast as the side with cleaner conditions in the wet season, from about November to March.


## Two regencies

Sanur belongs to the city of Denpasar and Nusa Dua to Badung regency. In Badung the local tax on restaurant food and drink and on hotel stays is 10%. We have not sourced Denpasar's rate, so check the foot of the menu in Sanur for what is added.


## Sanur

Sanur is an established resort town on the edge of Denpasar. Travel guides describe it as a mature tourist town that appeals mostly to families and older visitors, with well-priced accommodation and easy beach access. Our view is that it is the most relaxed of the southern bases.


## What Sanur is like

Our description of Sanur, drawn from how guides present it, is of a compact town that is easy on foot. The pace is slower than on the west coast, and the evenings are quiet. Travelers looking for beach clubs and late nights will find little of either. Travelers who want to read a book under a tree by the sea will find exactly that.


## The harbor

Sanur's harbor is the main departure point for boats to Nusa Lembongan and Nusa Penida. Travel guides give the crossing as roughly 30 to 45 minutes; check with the operator, since we have no official schedule. Staying in Sanur the night before a boat removes a cross-town drive from the morning.



## Nusa Dua

Nusa Dua is a purpose-built resort area. Travel guides describe it as an enclave of high-end resorts with a long golden sand beach and very little traffic. The hotels are large and self-contained, and it sits about 8 miles from the airport on the eastern side of the Bukit peninsula.


## What Nusa Dua is like

Nusa Dua feels different from the rest of Bali, by design. It is orderly, quiet and separate from village life. Our view is that it suits travelers who want a resort vacation with reliable standards, and that it will disappoint travelers who want to step out of the hotel into a Balinese street.


## Eating out

Because the area is built around resorts, most meals are eaten inside one. Resort restaurant prices carry the 10% local tax and often a service charge. Travelers on a budget should compare the final price including both, or plan some meals outside the enclave.


## Sanur or Nusa Dua

The choice between Sanur and Nusa Dua comes down to a town or a resort. Sanur is a real place with a walkable center and a range of small hotels. Nusa Dua is a collection of large resorts with their own grounds. Sanur is the better position for Ubud; Nusa Dua is the better position for Uluwatu.


## Distances from each

From Sanur it is about 14 miles to Ubud, about 12 to Seminyak and about 21 to Uluwatu. From Nusa Dua it is about 12 miles to Uluwatu and about 26 to Ubud. We give no drive times. Sanur's position on the east side of the island makes it a practical base for day trips into the hills and along the east coast.


## For families

Both areas are popular with families, for the calm and the space. We have no official source on sea conditions or lifeguards at either beach, so we call neither one safe for swimming. Ask your hotel about the tide and about where it is safe to swim.


## Getting there and around

Both areas are a short distance from the airport, which makes either a comfortable first or last stop. A pre-arranged car is the easy way to arrive. The State Department's advice is to avoid unlicensed taxis that approach passengers at airports and to use reputable taxi companies or ride-hailing apps.


## Around Sanur

Within Sanur, our advice is to walk. For trips beyond the town, ride apps and taxis are available; the State Department describes Blue Bird taxis as generally the most reliable in Indonesia.


## Around Nusa Dua

Inside the resort area, the grounds and the beach path are walkable. Anything outside it needs a car. Our advice is to book a driver for the day when you want to see Uluwatu or the temples, and to agree the price and hours in writing first.


## Who this coast suits

The east-facing coast suits families with young children, older travelers, and anyone who wants a quiet beach base without surf crowds or nightlife. It also suits a traveler heading to the Nusa islands. It is a weaker fit for surfers in the dry season and for those who want the restaurant and bar scene of Seminyak.


## How long to stay

Our advice is that Sanur works well for three or four nights as a base for day trips, and for one night before a boat. Nusa Dua works as a resort stay of any length, on the understanding that you will see little of Bali from inside it.



## FAQ

Is Sanur or Nusa Dua better?Sanur is a walkable beach town with small hotels; Nusa Dua is an enclave of large resorts. Sanur is better placed for Ubud and the island boats, Nusa Dua for Uluwatu.

Is Sanur good for families?Travel guides describe Sanur as appealing mostly to families and older visitors. It is flat, compact and quiet.

Where do boats to Nusa Penida leave from?Mainly from Sanur's harbor. Travel guides give the crossing as roughly 30 to 45 minutes; check with the operator.

Can you see the sunset in Sanur?Sanur faces east, so the sun rises over the sea and sets behind the land.



===== munduk-and-north-bali-guide [north-and-east-bali / base-split] 1064w =====
Munduk and North Bali
Munduk is a mountain village in Bali's central highlands, and the north coast below it, around Lovina, is the island's quiet shore. This is the cool, green side of Bali: waterfalls, coffee and clove plantations, crater lakes and black sand beaches. It is far from the airport, at about 51 miles to Munduk and 62 to Lovina, and it suits travelers who want nature and calm more than beach clubs.


## Where Munduk and the north are

Munduk sits in the western highlands of central Bali, in the wider Bedugul area, at more than 700 meters above sea level. The north coast lies beyond the mountains, with Lovina about 20 kilometers, or 12 miles, from Munduk. By road Munduk is about 51 miles from the airport and about 39 from Ubud.

Map: Munduk Waterfall, Lake Tamblingan, Lake Buyan, Ulun Danu Bratan, Lovina Beach, Sekumpul Waterfall.

## A different climate

Altitude changes the weather. Travel guides describe the highlands as distinctly cool, and the climate figures published for south Bali, which come from a sea-level station, do not apply here. Pack a sweater and a rain jacket for Munduk in any month. The north coast is at sea level and warm.


## A different pace

Travel guides describe North Bali as quiet black sand beaches and the old capital city, in contrast with the heavily visited south. Our view is that the quiet is the reason to come.


## Munduk

Munduk is known for waterfalls, plantations and walking. Travel guides describe the area as popular for trekking and extremely scenic, with coffee and clove trees lining the highland roads. The village sits at more than 700 meters, about 39 miles by road from Ubud.


## Waterfalls

Waterfalls are what Munduk is best known for, and travel guides call Munduk Waterfall perhaps the most impressive accessible falls on the island, powerful in the wet season. We give no trail lengths, difficulty ratings or entrance fees, since we have not sourced them. Ask at your accommodation about the path and its condition after rain.


## The twin lakes

Near Munduk are the twin crater lakes of Buyan and Tamblingan, set in forest. Travel guides mention walks around the lakes. Mornings are the conventional time for views in the mountains, before cloud builds; that is our advice and not a forecast.


## Ulun Danu Bratan

The temple of Ulun Danu Bratan stands on the shore of Lake Bratan, in the same highlands, and travel guides list it among the area's main sights. It is a working temple, so Bali's rules on modest dress in sacred places apply. It is commonly visited on the drive to or from Munduk.



## The north coast

The north coast is Bali's calm sea. Travel guides describe Lovina as a string of quiet coastal villages with black volcanic sand beaches and coral reefs offshore, popular with visitors who want a beach stay without the pace of the southern resorts. Singaraja, nearby, is the largest town on the coast.


## Lovina's dolphins

The outing Lovina is known for is a boat trip at dawn to see dolphins offshore, which travel guides describe as popular and easy to arrange. We have no official source on how the boats are regulated or how often dolphins are seen, so we promise nothing. Ask how many boats go out and how close they approach.


## Singaraja

Singaraja was the administrative capital of Bali in the Dutch colonial period. Travel guides describe wide streets, some old houses and a museum holding traditional palm-leaf manuscripts. It is a working town and not a resort, of interest to travelers who like history.


## Sekumpul

Inland from the coast, Sekumpul is a group of waterfalls in a jungle valley, described by travel guides as among the most spectacular on the island. Reaching waterfalls in Bali generally means steps and a walk; ask locally how long the descent is and whether a guide is required.


## Getting there

The north is a long way from where most visitors land. Munduk is about 51 miles from the airport and Lovina about 62, across the central mountains on winding roads. We give no drive times, since they vary. Our advice is to treat the journey as a day in itself, with stops on the way.


## From Ubud

Ubud is the natural stepping stone. It is about 39 miles from Ubud to Munduk, and the road passes the highlands around Bedugul and Lake Bratan. A driver hired for the transfer can stop at the temple and a viewpoint, which turns a travel day into a sightseeing day.


## Getting around

Public transport is thin in the highlands. A car with a driver is the practical way to reach waterfalls and lakes. A scooter on steep, wet mountain roads asks more of a rider than the flat south does, and it requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven.


## Who the north suits

Munduk and the north coast suit walkers, nature lovers, couples and anyone who has seen the south and wants the other Bali. They suit a second or third base on a trip of ten days or more. They are a weak fit for a first trip of one week, where the drive takes a large share of the time.


## How long to stay

Our advice is two or three nights in Munduk, which allows a waterfall walk, the lakes and a slow morning. Add the north coast if you want sea as well as mountains. A single night is mostly driving.


## Practical notes

The State Department says adequate medical care may be harder to find in rural areas of Indonesia and that an injured traveler will likely need to go to a major city. Carry what you rely on, wear proper shoes on waterfall paths, and bring cash, since card acceptance is not something to count on in villages.



## FAQ

Is Munduk worth visiting?Yes, for waterfalls, plantations, crater lakes and cool mountain air. It suits a trip of ten days or more, as a second or third base.

How far is Munduk from Ubud?About 39 miles by road, through the central highlands. Check a live map for the time.

Is it cold in Munduk?Munduk is above 700 meters and travel guides describe the highlands as distinctly cool. Bring a sweater and a rain jacket.

What is Lovina known for?Quiet black sand beaches and dawn boat trips to see dolphins.



===== sidemen-and-amed-east-bali-guide [north-and-east-bali / base-split] 1099w =====
Sidemen and Amed: East Bali
Sidemen and Amed are the two quiet bases of East Bali. Sidemen is an inland valley of rice terraces below Mount Agung, about 22 miles from Ubud. Amed is a strip of fishing villages on the northeast coast, about 49 miles from Ubud, known for snorkeling and diving from the beach. Together they show the calmer side of the island, and they suit a trip of ten days or more.


## East Bali in outline

East Bali is the region under Mount Agung, the island's highest mountain. Travel guides describe it as laid-back coastal villages and volcanic scenery. Besides Sidemen and Amed it includes the port of Padang Bai, the quiet coastal town of Candidasa, the water palace of Tirta Gangga and Pura Besakih, the temple regarded as the mother temple of Balinese Hinduism.

Map: Sidemen, Pura Besakih, Tirta Gangga, Lempuyang, Jemeluk, Amed, USAT Liberty wreck, Tulamben.

## How far it is

The east is close to Ubud and far from the southern beaches. By road Sidemen is about 22 miles from Ubud and about 35 from the airport; Amed is about 49 miles from Ubud and about 61 from the airport. We give no drive times. The roads are slower and emptier than in the south.


## Sidemen

Sidemen is a valley in the foothills of Mount Agung. Travel guides describe the drive through it as one of the most scenic on the island, with rice terraces and forested hillsides, and note that the walking is undemanding. There is no town center to speak of; the valley itself is the attraction.


## What to do in Sidemen

Sidemen is for walking through rice fields, sitting on a terrace with a view of the volcano, and doing very little. Our view is that it offers what many visitors hope to find around Ubud and no longer do: terraces without crowds. Ask your accommodation for a walking route or a local guide.


## Besakih from Sidemen

Pura Besakih lies on the slopes of Mount Agung, north of the valley. Travel guides describe a complex of more than twenty temples spread over about three kilometers. It is the most important temple on the island, so Bali's rules on dress and on entering inner courtyards apply in full.



## Amed

Amed is a coastal strip running about 14 kilometers, or 9 miles, along the northeast shore, through seven villages including Jemeluk, Bunutan, Lipah and Selang. Travel guides describe black and grey sand and pebble beaches lined with outrigger fishing boats, traditional salt making, and views of Mount Agung.


## Snorkeling and diving

Amed is known for coral reefs close to shore, which makes snorkeling possible from the beach. Travel guides also mention a Japanese shipwreck in shallow water along the strip. We rate no site for difficulty or depth. The State Department notes that diving in Indonesia can be dangerous because safety regulation is minimal, so choose an operator with care.


## The Liberty wreck at Tulamben

Up the coast at Tulamben lies the USAT Liberty, a US Army transport ship torpedoed by a Japanese submarine in 1942. It is one of the best-known wreck dives in Indonesia, and divers staying in Amed commonly visit it on a day trip. Check your travel insurance for how it treats scuba diving before you book.


## Around Amed

The Tirta Gangga water palace is a day trip from Amed, and the Lempuyang temple complex is about 9 miles away by road. Amed is spread out along the coast, and travel guides note that local public transport is limited, so a driver or a short ride is needed to move between villages.


## Sidemen or Amed

Sidemen and Amed answer different wishes. Sidemen is green, inland and close to Ubud; it is about walking and views. Amed is dry, coastal and further away; it is about the sea. A traveler with time for only one should choose by whether they want rice terraces or a reef.


## Doing both

The two are about 26 miles apart by road, so they combine naturally in one loop: Ubud to Sidemen, Sidemen to Amed, and back along the coast. Our advice is at least two nights in each. A single night in either is mostly packing and unpacking.


## The sea in the east

The east coast faces a different stretch of water from the southern resorts. We have no official source on sea conditions at Amed's beaches, so we call none of them safe. Ask where local people swim and snorkel, and stay close to shore.


## Getting there and around

East Bali is reached by road from Ubud or the south. A car with a driver is the practical choice, and the transfer can include stops such as Tirta Gangga. A scooter requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven under Indonesian law.


## Boats from the east

Padang Bai is the port for the ferry to Lombok, and travel guides note fast boats to the Gili Islands from Padang Bai and from Amed. We have no official schedule or safety rules for them, so check directly with an operator and read our page on the Gili crossing before you plan around a boat.


## Money and supplies

Our advice is to bring cash and anything you depend on. The State Department says adequate medical care may be harder to find in rural areas of Indonesia. Bali's rules for visitors say to change money only at authorized changers, which are easier to find in the towns you pass on the way.


## Who East Bali suits

East Bali suits divers, snorkelers, walkers, couples and anyone who wants a quieter island than the one in the south. It suits a longer trip. On a first visit of one week it competes with Ubud and the beaches for limited days, and most travelers will choose those first.


## Mount Agung

Mount Agung dominates the east and is of great spiritual importance to the Balinese. It is an active volcano. Climbing it is a serious undertaking, and conditions and permissions change; we give no route advice. Ask locally and check the current status before planning any climb.



## FAQ

Is Sidemen worth visiting?Yes, for rice terraces, views of Mount Agung and easy walking. It is about 22 miles from Ubud by road.

What is Amed known for?Snorkeling and diving from the beach, black sand, outrigger fishing boats and views of Mount Agung.

How far is Amed from Ubud?About 49 miles by road. Check a live map for the time.

Where is the USAT Liberty wreck?At Tulamben, up the coast from Amed. The ship was a US Army transport torpedoed in 1942.



===== nusa-lembongan-and-ceningan-guide [nusa-islands / boat-day-buffer] 1146w =====
Nusa Lembongan and Ceningan
Nusa Lembongan is a small island off Bali's southeast coast, reached by boat from Sanur in roughly 30 to 45 minutes, and Nusa Ceningan is the tiny island joined to it by a yellow suspension bridge. Together they offer surf, snorkeling, cliffs and a slower pace than the mainland, with no cars to speak of. They suit a stay of two or three nights in the middle of a trip.


## What the two islands are

Nusa Lembongan is one of three islands off Bali's southeast coast, alongside tiny Nusa Ceningan and the much larger Nusa Penida. Lembongan measures about 8 square kilometers, roughly 3 square miles. Travel guides describe it as known for surfing, diving and snorkeling, and as a good place to relax.

Map: Jungutbatu Beach, Mushroom Bay, Dream Beach, Devil's Tear, Yellow Bridge to Ceningan.

## How it differs from Bali

The island is small enough to cross in minutes, and travel guides note that personal cars are not allowed without special village permission. People get around on foot, by bicycle, by motorbike or in small pickup trucks. The effect is a place with far less traffic than any resort area on the mainland.


## Ceningan

Nusa Ceningan sits between Lembongan and Penida. A yellow suspension bridge links it to Lembongan and carries walkers, bicycles and motorbikes. It is the smallest and quietest of the three islands, usually visited as a short outing from Lembongan.


## The areas of Lembongan

Lembongan has a handful of distinct areas, and where you stay shapes the visit. Jungutbatu, in the northwest, is the main village, with a beach where many boats arrive. Mushroom Bay, in the southwest, is a sheltered cove with white sand. The south coast has limestone cliffs, with Dream Beach and Sunset Beach.


## Jungutbatu

Jungutbatu is the island's hub, with the largest choice of places to stay and eat. The surf breaks lie offshore here. It is the practical base for a first visit, since boats land nearby and everything is within a short ride.


## Mushroom Bay

Mushroom Bay is a sheltered cove on the southwest side, described by travel guides as having white sand. It is quieter than Jungutbatu. Some boats from Bali arrive here by floating pontoon, so check where your boat lands before choosing where to sleep.


## The mangroves

The northern tip of the island is covered by a mangrove forest, described by travel guides as a mature ecosystem. It is a calm contrast to the cliffs of the south coast and a common short outing.



## What to do

The islands are about the water. Travel guides list surfing at the offshore breaks, snorkeling and scuba diving among coral reefs, and trips to see manta rays. On land there are the cliff viewpoints of the south coast and the ride across the bridge to Ceningan. Two full days cover it without hurry.


## Snorkeling and manta rays

Snorkeling trips leave from Lembongan by small boat, and manta rays are seen in the waters around neighboring Nusa Penida. We have no official source on how often mantas are seen, so we promise nothing. The State Department notes that diving in Indonesia can be dangerous because of minimal safety regulations; choose an operator carefully and ask about life jackets.


## Surfing

The surf breaks off Lembongan are reef breaks reached by paddling or by boat. We rate none for difficulty. Our advice to anyone who is not an experienced reef surfer is to watch from the shore or take local advice before paddling out.


## Places to be careful

Two spots on Lembongan carry specific warnings in travel guides. At Dream Beach, guides warn visitors to be very careful about entering the water because of fierce rip currents. At Devil's Tear, a rocky outcrop where waves explode against the cliff, guides report that tourists have fallen to their deaths.


## Devil's Tear

Barriers have been put up at Devil's Tear, and they are there for a reason. Stay behind them, stay off wet rock, and do not turn your back on the sea for a photograph. A wave larger than the ones before it gives no warning.


## Motorbikes

Renting a motorbike is the usual way to see the island, and the same law applies as on the mainland. Indonesia's traffic law requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven, and a helmet. Our advice for anyone without a motorcycle license is to walk, cycle or hire a ride.


## Getting there

Boats to Nusa Lembongan leave mainly from Sanur, with other services from Serangan and Padang Bai. Travel guides give the crossing from the south as roughly 35 to 45 minutes. We have no official schedule and name no operator; check times directly, and book a boat that leaves in daylight.


## The wet landing

Travel guides note that most boats to Lembongan involve a wet landing: passengers wade through water that can be knee-deep or higher to board and to get off. Wear shorts and sandals, keep your phone and passport in a dry bag, and travel with luggage you can lift over your head.


## The sea decides

The crossing is open water in a small fast boat, and it depends on sea conditions. We have no official source on cancellations and state no rate. Our advice is to keep at least one full day between your boat back to Bali and your flight home.


## Practical notes

Lembongan is a small island with small-island services. Travel guides report several ATMs. We have no official source on medical facilities there; the State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to go to a major city, which from here begins with a boat. Bring the medicines you rely on.


## How long to stay

Our advice is two or three nights. One night is spent mostly on boats and transfers. Three nights allows a snorkeling trip, a day on the south coast and Ceningan, and a slow morning. Lembongan also works as a base for a day on Nusa Penida, which is about ten minutes away by local boat.


## Who it suits

The islands suit couples, surfers, snorkelers and anyone who wants a beach stay with little traffic. They are less suited to travelers with limited mobility, because of the wet landing and the rough paths, and to anyone uneasy in small boats.



## FAQ

How do you get to Nusa Lembongan?By boat, mainly from Sanur. Travel guides give the crossing as roughly 35 to 45 minutes; check times with the operator.

Are there cars on Nusa Lembongan?Travel guides note that personal cars are not allowed without special village permission. People use motorbikes, bicycles and small pickup trucks.

How many days do you need on Nusa Lembongan?Our advice is two or three nights.

Is Devil's Tear dangerous?Travel guides report that tourists have died after falling there. Stay behind the barriers and off wet rock.



===== nusa-penida-day-trip-or-overnight [nusa-islands / boat-day-buffer] 1106w =====
Nusa Penida: Day Trip or Overnight?
Nusa Penida is better with a night than as a day trip, if your schedule allows it. The island is large, its roads are rough and its famous viewpoints are far apart, so a day trip from Bali covers one side of it in a hurry. An overnight stay gives you the cliffs early and late, without the day-trip crowd. A day trip still makes sense when time is short.


## What Nusa Penida is

Nusa Penida is the largest of the three islands off Bali's southeast coast, at about 247 square kilometers, roughly 95 square miles. That is some thirty times the size of neighboring Nusa Lembongan. It is known for dramatic coastal cliffs and beaches, including Kelingking, Angel's Billabong, Broken Beach, Crystal Bay, Atuh Beach and Diamond Beach.

Map: Kelingking Beach, Angel's Billabong, Broken Beach, Crystal Bay, Atuh Beach, Diamond Beach.

## Why its size matters

Travel guides describe the roads on Nusa Penida as rough, and hilly away from the north coast. The best-known sights are split between the west side and the east side of the island. Reaching both in one day means spending most of it in a car on poor roads.


## Getting there

Boats leave mainly from Sanur, with a slower ferry from Padang Bai. Travel guides give the speedboat crossing from Sanur as roughly 30 minutes, arriving at harbors on the north coast such as Toyapakeh, Buyuk and Sampalan. We have no official schedule; check times with the operator.


## The case for a day trip

A day trip to Nusa Penida is the choice most visitors make, and it works for seeing one side of the island. Travel guides note that most visitors arrive on day trips from Bali. You leave Sanur in the morning, tour by car, and return in the afternoon, with no need to move your luggage.


## What a day covers

A day realistically covers one side: the west, with Kelingking, Angel's Billabong, Broken Beach and Crystal Bay, or the east, with Atuh and Diamond Beach. We give no drive times, since we have no source for them. Our advice is to pick a side and not to let a tour promise both.


## The drawbacks

Day trips share the same hours on the island, so our expectation is that the viewpoints are busiest in the middle of the day. The day also depends on two boat crossings fitting around it, and a late morning boat leaves less time for sightseeing.



## The case for staying overnight

Staying overnight on Nusa Penida changes what the visit is. Accommodation exists along the north coast between Toyapakeh and Sampalan. With a night or two, you can reach a viewpoint early in the morning or late in the afternoon, and you can see both sides of the island on separate days.


## What two days allow

Two days allow one side each, with time to walk down to a beach instead of only looking at it from above. They also leave room for a snorkeling trip; Manta Point, off the island's coast, is a known site for manta rays. We promise no sightings.


## What it costs you

An overnight means carrying your luggage across on the boat and accepting simpler services than on Bali. Travel guides describe the roads as rough. The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to reach a major city, which from Penida begins with a boat.


## Safety on the island

Nusa Penida's scenery comes with real hazards, and travel guides say so. They describe the path down to Kelingking Beach as a clifftop descent of about 400 meters, and Peguyangan Spring as reached by about 700 stairs on a cliff edge. They also warn of heat, and against swimming at Broken Beach at high tide because of the waves.


## The beaches below the cliffs

The beaches under the viewpoints are open to ocean swell. We have no official source on currents at any of them and call none safe for swimming. Our advice is to admire most of them from the sand, and to ask local people before entering the water anywhere.


## Motorbikes

Travel guides describe Penida's roads as rough and hilly. Indonesia's traffic law requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven. Our advice is to hire a car with a local driver.


## Insurance

The State Department strongly recommends insurance that covers medical evacuation in Indonesia. Check before the trip how your policy treats motorbike riding and boat travel, and keep the insurer's assistance number in your phone.


## How to choose

The choice between a day trip and an overnight on Nusa Penida depends on how many days you have and what you want from the island. Choose a day trip if your whole stay is a week and you want a look at the famous cliffs. Choose an overnight if you have ten days or more, or care about photographs without crowds.


## A middle way

A third option is to stay on Nusa Lembongan and visit Penida from there. Travel guides give the crossing between the two islands as about ten minutes by local boat. That gives you Lembongan's easier accommodation with Penida as a short hop.


## Book the boat with daylight to spare

Whichever you choose, our advice is to take a morning boat out and a return that lands in Bali well before dark. Travel guides advise against boarding any boat you have doubts about, whether because of the vessel, overloading or the weather.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Day trip or overnight turned out to be a question about the rest of the itinerary. A day trip starts and ends in Sanur, so it favors a base on that side of Bali. An overnight uses two travel days. And every version has a boat in it, which has to sit somewhere in the trip that a delay would not hurt.

How the island days, the boat crossings and the flight home fit together is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Is one day enough for Nusa Penida?One day covers one side of the island, west or east. Seeing both without rushing takes two days.

How long is the boat from Sanur to Nusa Penida?Travel guides give the speedboat crossing as roughly 30 minutes. Check times with the operator.

Can you swim at Kelingking Beach?We have no official source on conditions and call no Penida beach safe for swimming. Travel guides describe the path down as a clifftop descent of about 400 meters.

Is it worth staying overnight on Nusa Penida?Yes, if you have ten days or more. It lets you see the viewpoints early and late and cover both sides of the island.



===== adding-gili-or-lombok-to-a-bali-trip [beyond-bali / boat-day-buffer] 1056w =====
Adding the Gili Islands or Lombok to a Bali Trip: How Many Days It Takes
Adding the Gili Islands or Lombok to a Bali trip is worth it on a stay of two weeks or more, and hard to justify on a week. The Gilis are three small car-free islands off Lombok, reached from Bali by fast boat or by a short flight to Lombok and a transfer. Each way uses most of a day in each direction, and the boat depends on the sea.


## What the Gili Islands are

The Gili Islands are three small islands off the northwest coast of Lombok, the island east of Bali. Gili Trawangan is the largest and is known as the party island. Gili Air is the closest to Lombok and has an established local community. Gili Meno is the quietest. None of them has cars or motorbikes.


## Why people add them

The Gilis offer something Bali's main island does not: small sandy islands with no traffic, where the day is spent walking, cycling, swimming and snorkeling. For travelers who find south Bali too busy, that contrast is the appeal.


## Lombok

Lombok is a large island in its own right, with its own airport. We have sourced little about Lombok beyond its role as the route to the Gilis, so this page treats it as a gateway and does not describe its sights. It is a separate province from Bali, so Bali's tourist levy and provincial rules do not extend to it.


## Getting there by fast boat

Fast boats run from Bali to the Gili Islands. Travel guides name Padang Bai, in East Bali, as the main port, with a crossing of roughly an hour and a half; boats from the south of Bali take longer, and Amed, on the northeast coast, has the shortest sea route. We have no official schedule and name no operator.


## Getting to the port

The port is part of the journey. Padang Bai is about 35 miles from the airport by road and about 23 from Ubud. Add that drive to the crossing, and a morning boat means an early start from most bases. Staying in East Bali the night before shortens the day.


## What travel guides warn about

Travel guides are direct about the boats. They describe the fast boats as small high-speed craft, note that crew training and safety standards vary considerably, and advise travelers who have concerns about the vessel, the operator, overloading or the weather not to board. They describe sea conditions as roughest in July and August.



## Getting there by air

The alternative to the boat is a flight from Bali to Lombok's international airport, followed by a drive to the harbor area at Bangsal and a short boat to the islands. Travel guides put the drive at well over an hour and a half. The flight is short; the whole journey is not.


## When flying makes sense

Flying suits travelers who are uneasy on small boats, anyone prone to seasickness, and trips in the months when the sea is roughest. It also suits a trip that includes time on Lombok itself. It still ends with a boat, though a much shorter one across sheltered water.


## Domestic flights

A domestic flight is a separate booking from your international ticket, with its own baggage rules, and it does not connect to your flight home. Leave a wide margin if you fly back from Lombok to Bali on the same day as an international departure. Our advice is not to do that at all.


## How much time it takes

A side trip to the Gilis costs two travel days, one each way, whatever route you use. On a seven-night trip that leaves five days for everything else, on two islands. On a fourteen-night trip it leaves twelve. Our advice is at least three nights on the islands, so the days there outnumber the days spent getting there.


## Which Gili

The three islands differ mainly in how lively they are. Trawangan has the most going on, Meno the least, and Air sits between them with more village life. Small boats link the three, so the choice of where to sleep does not stop you seeing the others.


## What to bring

Our advice is to carry cash, since small islands have few ways to get more, and any medicine you rely on. The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to reach a major city for appropriate care. From a small island, that journey starts with a boat.


## The alternative closer to Bali

Travelers who want small-island days without the long crossing have a nearer option. Nusa Lembongan lies off Bali's own coast, roughly 35 to 45 minutes from Sanur by boat according to travel guides. It has no cars to speak of, surf and snorkeling, and takes far less of the trip.


## Gili or Nusa

The Nusa islands are closer and more rugged, with cliffs; the Gilis are further, flatter and sandier. Our view is that a first trip of ten days or fewer is better served by Lembongan, and that the Gilis earn their place on a longer trip or a return visit.


## Where to place it in the trip

A Gili side trip belongs in the middle of an itinerary, never at the end. The boat depends on sea conditions, and we have no official source on how often crossings are cancelled, so we state no rate. Our advice is to be back on Bali with at least one full day, and better two, before your flight home.


## The return leg

The way back is where plans fail, since it is the leg with a deadline behind it. How an island leg, a boat and an international flight are spaced across a trip is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How do you get from Bali to the Gili Islands?By fast boat, mainly from Padang Bai, or by flying to Lombok and continuing by road and a short boat. Each route takes most of a day.

Are the Gili Islands worth it on a one-week Bali trip?Hard to justify. The side trip uses two travel days, so it fits better on a stay of two weeks or more.

Are fast boats to the Gili Islands safe?Travel guides note that safety standards vary and advise not boarding if you have concerns about the vessel, overloading or the weather.

Are there cars on the Gili Islands?No. There are no cars or motorbikes on any of the three islands.



===== uluwatu-tanah-lot-besakih-lempuyang-visits [temples-and-culture / traffic-time] 1175w =====
Uluwatu, Tanah Lot, Besakih, Lempuyang: What Each Temple Visit Involves
Bali's four best-known temples are in four different corners of the island, and each visit is a different kind of outing. Uluwatu is a clifftop temple in the far south, Tanah Lot a sea temple on the southwest coast, Besakih the mother temple on the slopes of Mount Agung, and Lempuyang a mountain temple in the east famous for one photograph. The same dress and access rules apply at all of them.


## The rules that apply at every temple

Bali's rules for foreign tourists apply at every temple on the island. They require modest dress in sacred places, and they bar tourists from the inner and middle courtyards of a temple unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress. Climbing on sacred structures and posing indecently at sacred sites are prohibited.


## What to wear

Our advice is the same for all four temples: clothing that covers shoulders and knees, with a sarong and sash where the temple asks for them. Carry your own sarong if you would like to avoid renting one. The circular containing these rules also says that a person entering the inner areas to pray must not be menstruating.


## Fees and hours

We list no entrance fees or opening hours, because both change and we have no official source for them. Carry small rupiah notes, and ask your driver or accommodation for the current arrangements the day before.

The four temples, and how far apart they really are: Uluwatu Temple · Tanah Lot · Pura Besakih · Lempuyang. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## Uluwatu

Uluwatu Temple stands on the southwestern tip of the Bukit peninsula, and travel guides describe it as perched on a cliff about 75 meters above the sea. It is about 13 miles from the airport by road. The visit is a walk along the cliff edge through the temple grounds, with the ocean below.


## What the visit involves

Uluwatu is the easiest of the four to fit into a beach holiday, since it lies in the resort south. It faces west, so sunset is the popular hour, and a kecak dance is staged for visitors at the temple in the evening. We have not sourced its times, so check locally.


## Getting back

Our advice is to arrange your ride back before you arrive, since sunset is the popular hour, and to keep sunglasses, phones and food secure if there are monkeys about.



## Tanah Lot

Tanah Lot is a sea temple built on a rock just offshore, on the southwest coast. Travel guides describe it as one of Bali's important directional temples, attributed to the 15th-century priest Nirartha. It is about 7 miles up the coast from Canggu by road, which makes it the closest of the four to the west-coast resorts.


## What the visit involves

Visitors see Tanah Lot from the shore and the surrounding paths. Travel guides describe the area as very busy, especially in the late afternoon before sunset, with an approach lined by souvenir shops. Under Bali's rules the inner temple is for worshippers, so the visit is to the setting, not the interior.


## When to go

Sunset is when the crowds come. Our advice for a calmer visit is to go in the morning, when the light is on the temple instead of behind it. Ask locally about the tide if you hope to walk out toward the rock.


## Besakih

Pura Besakih lies on the slopes of Mount Agung in East Bali, and Balinese Hindus regard it as their mother temple. Travel guides describe a complex of more than twenty separate temples spread over about three kilometers. It is about 27 miles from Ubud by road, and far from the southern resorts.


## What the visit involves

Besakih is a large site on a mountainside, with stairways and terraces. Allow time to walk it, and expect cooler air and cloud. A ceremony may close parts of the complex to visitors, so ask at the entrance what is open.


## Guides and touts

Travel guides mention souvenir sellers in the visitor area. We have no official source on the guiding arrangements at Besakih, so we describe none. Our advice is to ask at the ticket office what your ticket includes before accepting any offer of services on the way in.


## Lempuyang

Pura Lempuyang is a temple high in the mountains of Karangasem, in the far east. It is known around the world for one image: a split gate framing Mount Agung, with what looks like a reflection in still water. Lempuyang is about 45 miles from Ubud by road and about 9 from Amed.


## The photograph

Travel writers report two things a visitor should know before making the journey. The reflection in the famous picture is made by a photographer holding a mirror under the phone; there is no pool of water at the gate. And the photograph is taken by numbered turn, with a wait that can run to hours.


## Is it worth the drive?

Our view is that Lempuyang is worth visiting for travelers already staying in East Bali, around Amed or Sidemen, and hard to justify as a day trip from the south for a single picture. Mount Agung is visible only when the cloud allows, and no one can promise that.


## Choosing among them

The four temples suit different trips because of where they are. A traveler based in the south can see Uluwatu and Tanah Lot easily, though not together in one sunset. A traveler based in Ubud or the east is better placed for Besakih and Lempuyang. Few trips need all four.


## By base

These are our own suggestions.


- Staying in the Bukit or Seminyak: Uluwatu.

- Staying in Canggu: Tanah Lot.

- Staying in Ubud or Sidemen: Besakih.

- Staying in Amed: Lempuyang.



## Other temples worth knowing

Smaller sites near Ubud can be more rewarding than a long drive to a famous one. Travel guides describe Tirta Empul as a 10th-century spring temple used for ritual purification, Goa Gajah as an 11th-century cave with a carved demon's mouth at its entrance, and Gunung Kawi as an 11th-century site reached by climbing down 371 steps.


## Ceremony days

Festival days bring worshippers to the temples. Galungan, which comes every 210 days, falls on January 13 and August 11 in 2027. A visit on such a day is a privilege to watch and calls for extra care about dress, distance and photographs.



## FAQ

Which temple in Bali should I visit?Choose by where you are staying: Uluwatu from the south, Tanah Lot from Canggu, Besakih from Ubud or Sidemen, Lempuyang from Amed.

Can tourists go inside Bali's temples?Bali's rules bar tourists from the inner and middle courtyards unless they are there to pray in Balinese traditional dress.

Is the water at the Gates of Heaven real?No. Travel writers report that the reflection is made with a mirror held under the phone by a photographer.

What should I wear to a temple in Bali?Clothing that covers shoulders and knees, with a sarong and sash where the temple asks for them.



===== mount-batur-sunrise-trek [volcanoes-and-hikes / traffic-time] 1160w =====
Mount Batur Sunrise Trek: What the Night Actually Looks Like
The Mount Batur sunrise trek is a pre-dawn climb of an active volcano in the Kintamani highlands, timed to reach the top for sunrise. Travel guides put the climb at about two hours from the lakeside village of Toya Bungkah, and say a local guide is required. The mountain is about 1,700 meters high, it is cold at the top, and the day starts in the middle of the night.


## What Mount Batur is

Mount Batur is an active volcano of about 1,700 meters, roughly 5,600 feet, standing inside a large caldera in the Kintamani area of northeast Bali. A crater lake, Lake Batur, lies beside it. The caldera rim at Penelokan is the main viewpoint, described by travel guides as best early in the day before cloud forms.


## Where it is

Kintamani is in the highlands, about 27 miles from Ubud by road, about 45 from Seminyak and about 48 from the airport. Those distances explain the schedule: a traveler staying in the south has a long drive before the walking begins. From Ubud the drive is shorter by a wide margin.


## An active volcano

Batur is an active volcano, and access depends on its status. We have no live source for volcanic alerts, so check the current situation with your guide or accommodation before you book, and again the day before. A trek that is closed for safety is closed.

The volcano, and the drive to it: Ubud · Mount Batur. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## What the night looks like

The Batur trek is a short night followed by a short climb. A sunrise at the summit means walking in the dark, and walking in the dark means being collected from your hotel well before dawn. We give no clock times, since they vary by season, starting point and operator; ask yours for the pickup time.


## The pickup

The earlier part of the night is a drive. The further your base is from Kintamani, the earlier the car comes. A stay in Ubud, or in the Kintamani area itself the night before, shortens that drive and lengthens your sleep. Our advice is not to attempt it from the far south.


## The climb

Travel guides describe the route from Toya Bungkah as taking about two hours to the summit, with more than half of it through forest. The upper part is on open volcanic ground. You walk by flashlight or headlamp, in a line with other groups. We rate no difficulty; it is a steady uphill walk at night on a mountain.


## At the top

The summit is cold before sunrise. Travel guides describe Kintamani as much cooler than the coast and advise a sweater or jacket. Cloud decides the view, and no one can promise a clear morning. The descent is in daylight, and many treks end with a visit to the hot springs in Toya Bungkah.



## The guide requirement

A guide is required for the Batur climb, according to travel guides, and guides belong to the local Association of Mount Batur Trekking Guides, which has its office in Toya Bungkah. The same guides warn that touts target visitors in the area, and suggest booking the climb in advance.


## Booking

Most visitors book the trek as a package that includes transport, the guide and breakfast. We quote no prices. Ask what is included, how large the group is, and whether the guide is a member of the local association. A booking made through your accommodation gives you someone to hold to account.


## What to ask the operator

These are our own suggestions.


- What time is pickup, and how long is the drive from my hotel?

- How many people are in the group?

- Is a flashlight or headlamp provided?

- What happens if the mountain is closed or the weather is bad?



## What to bring

Packing for Batur is packing for a cold, dark, dusty walk. Our advice: closed shoes with grip, a warm layer and a windproof one, a headlamp if you own one, water, and a small snack. Leave valuables at the hotel. A light scarf or buff helps with dust on the way down.


## Fitness and health

The trek asks for a moderate level of fitness and sure footing in the dark. Anyone with a heart or breathing condition, a knee problem or a fear of heights should think carefully. The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to reach a major city for appropriate care, and a mountain is a long way from one.


## Insurance

Check how your travel insurance treats trekking and volcano climbs. Some policies list hiking above a certain altitude or on active volcanoes among their exclusions. Ask before the trip, in writing.


## When in the trip to do it

The Batur trek fits best in the middle of a trip. On the first days, jet lag from the United States makes a traveler wake early, which helps, and also leaves them short of sleep, which does not. The CDC advises allowing at least two days to adjust before anything important.


## The day after

Our advice is to keep the rest of trek day free. You will be back at your hotel by late morning having been awake since the small hours. Booking a long drive, a transfer to a new base or an evening event for the same day makes for a hard day.


## Season

The dry months, from about May to October, are the conventional choice for a sunrise climb, since the point is a clear view. Climate figures for south Bali do not describe the mountains, so we give no rainfall numbers for Kintamani. In the wet season, ask about recent conditions before committing.


## If you would prefer not to climb

The view of Batur does not require the climb. The caldera rim at Penelokan looks across the crater lake to the volcano, and travel guides describe the view as spectacular early in the day. It is reached by road, about 27 miles from Ubud, and suits anyone who wants the scenery without the night walk.


## The temple and the lake

Pura Ulun Danu Batur, on the caldera rim, is one of Bali's main water temples and part of the island's UNESCO-listed rice-terrace and water-temple landscape. A day in Kintamani that combines the viewpoint, the temple and lunch is a gentler alternative to the trek.



## FAQ

How long does the Mount Batur sunrise trek take?Travel guides put the climb at about two hours from Toya Bungkah. With the drive and the descent, it takes most of a night and a morning.

Do you need a guide for Mount Batur?Yes, according to travel guides. Guides belong to the local Association of Mount Batur Trekking Guides.

Is it cold on Mount Batur?Yes. Kintamani is much cooler than the coast, and a sweater or jacket is advised for the summit before sunrise.

How high is Mount Batur?About 1,700 meters, roughly 5,600 feet.



===== bali-waterfalls-stairs-fees-and-drive [waterfalls-and-rice-terraces / traffic-time] 1092w =====
Bali's Waterfalls: Stairs, Entry Fees and Which Side of the Island They Are On
Bali's waterfalls are spread across the island, and almost all of them sit at the bottom of a valley, which means steps down and steps back up. The easiest to reach are near Ubud; the most dramatic are in the north. Each charges its own entrance fee, which we do not list because fees change. Choose waterfalls by where you are staying, not by a ranking.


## Where the waterfalls are

Bali's waterfalls fall into three groups by location: those around Ubud in the center, those in the central highlands, and those in the north. The group you can reach depends on your base. Ubud is about 22 miles from the airport and is the best starting point for the first group and a reasonable one for the second.


## Around Ubud

Travel guides name four waterfalls in the Ubud area. Tegenungan is about 30 meters high and described as easy to reach. Tibumana, east of Ubud, is a short walk from its parking area. Tukad Cepung is partly hidden in a cave-like canyon and known for light beams. Kanto Lampo involves wading through water for photographs.


## The central highlands

Nungnung lies in a jungle ravine north of Denpasar and is reached by about 500 stone steps, according to travel guides. Further up, in the hills near the mountain lakes, are the Banyumala twin falls near Wanagiri and Munduk Waterfall, which guides call perhaps the most impressive accessible falls on the island.


## The north

The north has Gitgit, described by travel guides as easy to reach, and Sekumpul, a group of several falls side by side in a jungle valley. These are a long drive from the south: Munduk is about 51 miles from the airport and Lovina about 62. They belong to a stay in the north, not a day trip from Seminyak.


The waterfalls are mostly north: Ubud · Munduk & the north. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## The stairs

Stairs are the common feature of Bali's waterfalls. A waterfall is where a river drops into a gorge, and the parking area is at the top. The walk down is easy and the climb back is hot work in humid air. Nungnung's roughly 500 steps are the best-known example, and it is far from the only one.


## Who should think twice

Travelers with knee problems, heart conditions, small children or limited mobility should ask how many steps a waterfall involves before setting out. Tegenungan is described as easy to reach, and Tibumana as a short walk, which makes them the candidates for anyone who wants less climbing.


## What to wear

Our advice is sandals or shoes with grip that can get wet, since steps and rocks near a waterfall are slippery. Bring swimwear under your clothes if you plan to get in, a towel, and a dry bag for your phone. Dress modestly on the way through villages, as Bali's rules for visitors ask.


## Fees and what they cover

Each waterfall is run locally and charges its own entrance fee, paid at a booth near the parking area. We list no amounts, because fees change and we have no official source. Carry small rupiah notes. Parking and, at some sites, a local guide may be charged separately; ask at the booth what the ticket includes.


## Guides

Some waterfalls, particularly the larger ones in the north, are visited with a local guide. We have no official source on where a guide is mandatory, so we state no rule. Ask at the ticket booth, and agree the price before you start walking.


## Swimming and safety

Swimming is the reason many people visit a waterfall, and conditions vary by site and by day. We have no official source on water safety at any of them and call none safe. Heavy rain upstream can raise a river quickly. Our advice is to stay out of the water after heavy rain and to follow what local staff tell you.


## The wet season

Waterfalls are at their fullest in the wet season, from about November to March, when south Bali's climate record shows 9 to 14 inches of rain a month. That is also when paths are muddiest and water is brownest. The dry season gives clearer pools and easier paths, with less volume.


## Water quality

The CDC advises travelers in Indonesia to avoid swimming or wading in water that may be contaminated after flooding. Keep your mouth closed in the water, cover any cuts, and wash afterward. This matters most after rain.


## Planning a waterfall day

A waterfall day in Bali works when the falls lie in one direction. Travel guides suggest a route through the Ubud group in a single day. We give no drive times. Our advice is two or three waterfalls, since each one involves a walk down, time at the water and a climb back.


## Go early

Our advice is to start early. The best-known waterfalls near Ubud draw many visitors, and the first hours of the day are the quietest and the coolest for the climb back. A driver booked for the day can plan the order to suit.


## Combine with what is nearby

A waterfall pairs naturally with something else in the same direction: rice terraces north of Ubud, the mountain lakes near Munduk, or a temple. That makes better use of a driver's day than three waterfalls in a row.


## Which waterfall for which traveler

The right waterfall depends on your base and your legs. These are our own suggestions: Tegenungan or Tibumana for an easy visit from Ubud; Tukad Cepung for photographers; Nungnung for those who do not mind the steps; Munduk and Banyumala for a stay in the highlands; Sekumpul for a stay in the north.


## Getting there

A car with a driver is the practical way to reach waterfalls, which lie off main roads. A scooter on steep, wet lanes is harder riding than in town, and requires a license matching the type of vehicle driven.



## FAQ

What is the easiest waterfall to visit in Bali?Travel guides describe Tegenungan, near Ubud, as easy to reach, and Tibumana as a short walk from its parking area.

How many steps is Nungnung waterfall?About 500 stone steps, according to travel guides.

Do Bali waterfalls charge an entrance fee?Yes, each charges its own fee at a booth near the parking area. We list no amounts because they change.

Can you swim at Bali's waterfalls?At some. We have no official source on water safety, so ask local staff, and stay out after heavy rain.



===== manta-rays-at-nusa-penida [diving-and-snorkeling / boat-day-buffer] 1094w =====
Manta Rays at Nusa Penida: Season, Currents and Who Should Skip It
Manta rays are seen in the waters off Nusa Penida, at sites known as Manta Point and Manta Bay, on snorkeling and diving trips that leave from Bali, Nusa Lembongan and Penida itself. The trip involves open sea in a small boat, and sightings are never guaranteed. It suits confident swimmers who are comfortable in deep water, and it is a poor choice for nervous swimmers and anyone prone to seasickness.


## Where the mantas are

The manta ray sites are off the coast of Nusa Penida, the largest of the three islands southeast of Bali. Researchers describe Manta Point and Manta Bay as shallow reefs used by manta rays throughout the year and visited frequently by tourism operators. The sites include places where mantas come to be cleaned by smaller fish and places where they feed.


## A protected animal

Indonesia gave manta rays full legal protection in 2014 under a decree of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Fisheries, and the waters around Nusa Penida are one of the country's designated manta sanctuaries. Catching or trading them is illegal. Watching them is the legal economy that replaced it.


## No promises

We have no official source for how often mantas are seen, or in which months, so we give no odds and no season. They are wild animals in open water. An operator who guarantees a sighting is promising something no one controls.

Where the boats go: Nusa Penida · Kelingking, Nusa Penida. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## How the trip works

A manta trip is a boat trip. Boats to the Nusa islands leave mainly from Sanur, and travel guides give that crossing as roughly 30 to 45 minutes. Trips are sold as snorkeling tours or as scuba dives, from Bali as a full day or from Lembongan and Penida as a half day.


## From Bali or from the islands

A trip from mainland Bali adds the crossing in both directions to the day. A trip from Nusa Lembongan or Nusa Penida starts much closer, since Lembongan is about ten minutes from Penida by local boat. Travelers already planning island nights will find the manta trip easier to do from there.


## Snorkel or dive

Mantas at these sites can be seen from the surface, which is why snorkeling trips exist. Diving requires certification. The State Department notes that diving in Indonesia can be dangerous because of minimal safety regulations, so the choice of operator matters more here than at home.



## Sea conditions

The manta sites lie on the exposed ocean side of Nusa Penida, not in a sheltered bay. We have no official source on swell or currents there, so we describe none in detail. The planning fact is that the sea decides: trips are moved or cancelled when conditions are poor, and a rough day is uncomfortable even when the trip runs.


## Seasickness

A small boat sitting in ocean swell while snorkelers are in the water is hard on anyone prone to seasickness. If that is you, our advice is to take medication beforehand as your doctor recommends, to eat lightly, and to consider whether this outing is worth a miserable morning.


## In the water

Snorkeling here means deep, open water with no bottom to stand on, and often some swell. Wear the life jacket or flotation offered, stay with the group and the guide, and get back on the boat when you are tired. Ask before you book whether life jackets are carried for every passenger.


## Who should skip it

The manta trip is not for everyone, and saying so is part of an honest description. Our advice is to skip it if you are not a confident swimmer, if deep water frightens you, if you get badly seasick, or if you are traveling with small children. A calmer snorkeling spot will give you a better day.


## Medical considerations

The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to reach a major city for appropriate care. From a boat off Nusa Penida, that journey is a long one. Anyone with a heart, breathing or ear condition should talk to a doctor before diving or snorkeling in open water.


## Insurance

Check how your travel insurance treats snorkeling and scuba diving. Some policies cover snorkeling and exclude diving, or cover diving only to a stated depth or with certification. The State Department strongly recommends cover for medical evacuation in Indonesia.


## Choosing an operator

The operator is the one variable a visitor controls. We name none. Travel guides advise against boarding any boat you have doubts about, whether because of the vessel, overloading or the weather. A responsible operator limits the number of people in the water and briefs you before you get in.


## Questions to ask

These are our own suggestions.


- How many passengers are on the boat, and is there a life jacket for each?

- How many guides are in the water with the group?

- What happens if conditions are poor on the day?

- What are your rules about approaching the mantas?



## How to behave around them

Mantas are protected, and good practice is simple: do not touch, do not chase, do not swim over or in front of them, and keep your distance. A manta that is left alone often stays. One that is crowded leaves, and the next boat finds nothing.


## Where it fits in a trip

A manta trip belongs in the middle of an itinerary, with a spare day after it. The trip depends on the sea, and it may be moved to the next day. Our advice is to keep at least one full day between any boat outing and your flight home.


## Combining it

Most manta trips include other snorkeling stops around Nusa Penida and Lembongan, in calmer water, so the day does not depend on the mantas alone. Ask what the other stops are, since for many people those turn out to be the more enjoyable part.



## FAQ

Where can you see manta rays in Bali?In the waters off Nusa Penida, at sites known as Manta Point and Manta Bay, on snorkeling and diving trips.

Are manta ray sightings guaranteed?No. We have no official source on how often they are seen, and no operator controls wild animals in open water.

Can you see manta rays snorkeling?Yes. Researchers describe the sites as shallow reefs, and trips are sold for snorkelers as well as divers.

Are manta rays protected in Indonesia?Yes. Indonesia gave manta rays full legal protection in 2014, and Nusa Penida's waters are a designated manta sanctuary.



===== choosing-a-yoga-retreat-in-ubud [wellness-and-yoga / None] 1113w =====
Choosing a Yoga Retreat in Ubud: What the Listings Don't Say
Choosing a yoga retreat in Ubud comes down to five things the listings rarely make clear: where the place actually is, what the price includes, who teaches and how much, what happens if you cancel, and whether the accommodation is licensed. We name and rank no retreats. This page gives you the questions to ask, and the facts about Ubud and Bali's rules that bear on the answers.


## Why Ubud

Ubud is the center of yoga and wellness travel in Bali. It is an inland town in the central hills, at about 200 meters above sea level, cooler than the coast and regarded as the island's cultural center. It is about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud by road, and it has no beach.


## What "Ubud" means in a listing

Ubud is made up of fourteen villages, and many places advertised as "Ubud" are in the surrounding countryside. The center has three main streets and is congested with traffic. A retreat in the rice fields is quiet and may be a ride from town; a retreat in the center is walkable and less peaceful.


## Ask for the exact location

Our advice is to ask for the address and look at it on a map before booking. Check the distance to central Ubud and how you would get there. A shuttle that runs twice a day shapes your week differently from a ten-minute walk.

Central Ubud, and what sits north of it: Ubud · Tegallalang rice terraces. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## What the price includes

A retreat price can cover anything from the classes alone to accommodation, all meals, excursions and airport transfers. Listings vary in how clearly they say which. We quote no prices. Ask for a written list of what is included and what costs extra, and for the total you will pay.


## Tax and service

In Gianyar regency, which includes Ubud, the local tax on hotel stays and on restaurant food and drink matches the 10% charged in the southern resorts, and a business may add a separate service charge. Ask whether the price you have been quoted includes both.


## Paying

Ask which payment methods are accepted and in which currency you will be charged. Bali's rules for visitors say transactions should be in rupiah. A deposit paid by bank transfer is harder to recover than one paid by card, so prefer a card where you can.



## The teaching

The teaching is what you are paying for, and it is where listings are vaguest. Ask who the teachers are, what style they teach, how many hours of practice there are each day, and how many students share a class. A retreat and a teacher training are different things with different intensity.


## Level

Ask what level the program is aimed at. A beginner in a class built for experienced students, or the reverse, has a poor week. A good retreat will ask you about your experience and any injuries before you arrive, and that question is a good sign.


## Free time

Ask for a sample daily schedule. Some retreats fill the day from dawn; others leave afternoons open. If you want to see the temples and rice terraces around Ubud, you need free half-days, or extra nights before or after.


## Health and the body

A retreat is a week of physical practice in a tropical climate, often with a changed diet. Tell the organizers about medical conditions and injuries before you book. The State Department says routine medical care is available in Indonesia's major cities and that hospitals often require payment or a deposit upfront.


## Food and water

Ask how drinking water is supplied and how food is prepared. The State Department says tap water in many areas of Indonesia is not safe to drink. Tell the kitchen about allergies in writing before arrival, and ask how they are handled.


## Cleanses and treatments

Some retreats offer fasting, cleanses or other treatments. We give no medical advice on them. Talk to your own doctor before committing to anything that restricts food or involves supplements, particularly if you take regular medication, and bring that medication within Indonesia's import rules.


## Insurance

The State Department highly recommends travel insurance for Indonesia. Check that your policy covers the activities on the program, and that it covers cancellation if you have to withdraw before the retreat begins.


## Rules that apply

Two of Bali's rules bear directly on a retreat booking. The first is that visitors must stay in accommodation that holds a license. The second concerns the visa: the tourist visa does not allow selling goods or services, or being paid by anyone in Indonesia.


## If you are attending

Attending a retreat as a paying guest is tourism. An American needs the tourist visa, which allows a stay of 30 days, extendable once for another 30 days. A program longer than 30 days needs an extension, which involves a visit to an immigration office.


## If you plan to teach or assist

Teaching, assisting or working in exchange for a place is a different matter from attending. The tourist visa prohibits being paid by anyone in Indonesia, and the US Consular Agency in Bali reports stepped-up enforcement against illegal work and visa violations. Get proper advice before agreeing to any such arrangement.


## Cancellation and changes

Cancellation terms matter more for a retreat than for a hotel, because the sums are larger and are paid earlier. Ask for the policy in writing: what you get back if you cancel, by what date, and what happens if the retreat itself is cancelled. A long journey from the United States has more ways to go wrong than a short one.


## Dates to check

Check the retreat dates against Bali's calendar. Nyepi, the day of silence, falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, which brings island-wide restrictions; some travelers seek that out and others would not want to arrive or leave near it.



## FAQ

How do I choose a yoga retreat in Ubud?Ask for the exact location, a written list of what the price includes, the teachers and daily schedule, the cancellation policy, and whether the accommodation is licensed.

Do I need a special visa for a yoga retreat in Bali?Attending as a paying guest is tourism and uses the tourist visa. That visa does not allow being paid by anyone in Indonesia.

Is Ubud a good place for yoga?Ubud is Bali's inland cultural center, cooler than the coast. It has no beach, and the town center is congested.

Are yoga retreat prices in Bali all-inclusive?It varies. Ask for a written list of what is included and whether tax and service are added.



===== where-to-stay-in-bali [choosing-your-base / base-split] 1259w =====
Where to Stay in Bali: Choosing a Base
Where to stay in Bali depends on what you want within easy reach, because the island's areas are far apart in travel time. For a first visit, the choice is usually between a beach base in the south and Ubud in the hills. Seminyak suits dining and beach, Canggu surf, the Bukit cliffs and quiet, Sanur and Nusa Dua families, and Ubud culture and rice terraces.


## How to think about a base

A base in Bali is the area you sleep in and return to each night, and it decides what your days look like. The island is small, at about 89 miles from end to end, and its roads are slow. A sensible base puts the things you most want to do within a short ride, so that long drives are the exception.


## Start from what you want

Our advice is to list the three things you most want from the trip before looking at hotels. Beach and restaurants, surf, temples and terraces, quiet, diving: each points to a different part of the island. A base chosen for its price or its photographs, without that list, is how visitors end up in the car.


## Check the distance in both directions

Every base is close to some things and far from others. Ubud is about 22 miles from the airport to Ubud by road, and about 34 miles from Uluwatu. Seminyak is 7 miles from the airport and about 20 from Ubud. Look at what your base is far from as well as what it is near.

Every base, and how far apart they are: Seminyak & Canggu · Uluwatu & the Bukit · Sanur · Ubud · Sidemen · Amed · Munduk & the north · Nusa Lembongan · Nusa Penida. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## The southern beach bases

South Bali is where most visitors stay, and it offers five quite different bases. Travel guides describe Kuta as the most developed, Seminyak as quieter and more upscale, Canggu as a fast-developing surf area, the Bukit peninsula as rugged cliffs and surf beaches, and Sanur and Nusa Dua as the calmer resort areas on the east-facing coast.


## Seminyak

Seminyak suits travelers who want good restaurants, shops and a sunset beach within walking distance. It is about 7 miles from the airport. Our view is that it is the easiest base for a first visit, since it asks the least of a newcomer and has the most to do on foot.


## Canggu

Canggu, north of Seminyak, suits surfers and longer stays. It is about 12 miles from the airport and the furthest of the west-coast areas from the Bukit. Travel guides describe black sand beaches, surfing and rice paddies. It is more spread out, so plan on a ride for most outings.


## The Bukit and Uluwatu

The Bukit peninsula suits surfers, couples and anyone who wants cliffs, views and space. Uluwatu is about 13 miles from the airport. Little of it is walkable, and its beaches are at the bottom of steps. It is the furthest southern base from Ubud and the central sights.


## Sanur and Nusa Dua

The east-facing coast suits families and travelers who want quiet. Travel guides describe Sanur as a mature resort town popular with families and older visitors, and Nusa Dua as an enclave of high-end resorts with little traffic. Sanur is also where boats to the Nusa islands leave from.



## Ubud and the center

Ubud is the inland alternative to a beach base, and the island's cultural center. It sits at about 200 meters, cooler than the coast, among rice terraces and temples. It has no beach. It is the best-placed base for the central and eastern sights: Kintamani is about 27 miles away and Sidemen about 22.


## Who Ubud suits

Ubud suits travelers who care about culture, crafts, food, walking and yoga. It is a weaker choice for anyone whose trip is mainly about the sea. The center is busy and congested, and the quiet that people associate with Ubud is found in the villages around it.


## The quieter regions

Beyond the south and Ubud lie the quieter bases, which suit a longer trip. East Bali has Sidemen, a rice-terrace valley, and Amed, a strip of fishing villages known for snorkeling. The central highlands have Munduk, known for waterfalls and cool air. The north coast has Lovina's black sand beaches.


## The price of quiet

These bases are far from the airport: Munduk about 51 miles, Amed about 61. They reward the journey with fewer visitors and more landscape. Our view is that they belong on a trip of ten days or more, as a second or third stop, and not on a first week.


## The islands

Nusa Lembongan and Nusa Penida, off the southeast coast, are bases reached by boat from Sanur. They offer small-island days with little traffic. A stay there adds two boat crossings to the trip, which depend on the sea.


## Matching a base to a traveler

The right base in Bali follows from who is traveling. These are our own suggestions, and they describe a starting point, not a rule. Each pairing follows from what the area is known for and how far it sits from the airport.


- First visit, a bit of everything: Seminyak, or Seminyak and Ubud.

- Surfing: Canggu or the Bukit in the dry season.

- Family with young children: Sanur or Nusa Dua.

- Culture, walking, yoga: Ubud.

- Diving and snorkeling: Amed or the Nusa islands.

- Quiet and nature: Sidemen or Munduk.



## Season changes the answer

The time of year moves some of these. Surf guides describe the west-facing coast as best in the dry season, from about April to October, and the east-facing coast in the wet season. A surfer's base in January is not the same as in July.


## What to check about any place

Whatever the area, a few checks apply to every booking. Bali's rules require visitors to stay in licensed accommodation. In Badung regency, which covers most of the southern resorts, the local tax on hotel stays is 10%, and a service charge may be added, so compare final prices.


## Walkability

Look at a map of what surrounds the property. A villa that is cheap because it is isolated will cost you a ride every time you want a meal. If you do not plan to ride a scooter, which requires a motorcycle license in Indonesia, walkability matters a great deal.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Every base came with a list of what it is far from. The beach bases are far from the terraces, Ubud is far from the surf, and the quiet regions are far from the airport. A single base is a choice about what to leave out, which is why most trips longer than a few days end up asking whether one base is enough.

How bases combine, in what order and for how many nights each, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Where should I stay in Bali for the first time?Our view is Seminyak for an easy beach base, or Seminyak combined with Ubud to see the cultural center as well.

Is it better to stay in Ubud or Seminyak?Seminyak for beach, dining and sunsets; Ubud for culture, rice terraces and temples. They are about 20 miles apart by road.

Where should families stay in Bali?Travel guides describe Sanur as popular with families and Nusa Dua as a low-traffic enclave of resorts.
