Carry-on allowances on domestic flights are set by each airline and are often smaller than on long-haul flights. We have no official figure to quote. Check the cabin allowance for your fare, and keep medicines, documents and valuables in the bag that stays with you.


## Surfboards and dive gear

Surfboards, dive equipment and other sports gear are handled under each airline's own rules and are often charged separately. Ask the airline before booking if you are traveling with a board. A short domestic flight can cost more for the board than for the passenger.


## Medicines and batteries

Keep prescription medicine in hand luggage in its original packaging, with the prescription. Indonesia's medicine rules apply on domestic flights as they do on arrival. Spare lithium batteries and power banks belong in the cabin on any flight, not in checked bags.


## Changes, delays and cancellations

Change and refund rules on domestic flights depend on the fare you bought. The cheapest fares are commonly the least flexible. We have no official source on any airline's change fees or on passenger compensation rules for domestic delays in Indonesia, so we state none. Read the conditions on the booking page.


## If your flight is delayed

The practical protection against a delay is time. A domestic flight that lands a few hours late is an inconvenience on a travel day in the middle of a trip. On the day of your international departure, the same delay can cost you the flight home.


## Insurance

Check what your travel insurance says about missed connections between separately booked flights. Policies differ, and some cover a missed onward flight only when a minimum gap was left between the two. Ask before the trip, in writing.


## Connecting to your flight home

The rule that matters most is to keep domestic flights away from your international departure. Our advice is to return to Bali at least one full day before your flight to the United States. A domestic flight and a long-haul flight on the same day, on separate tickets, is a risk with a very large downside.


## The onward ticket

Indonesia's entry rules expect a visitor to hold an outbound ticket from Indonesia. A domestic flight does not leave Indonesia and does not meet that expectation. Your ticket out of the country is the one to show on arrival.


## Nyepi

Flights in and out of Bali are affected one day a year. In 2027 Nyepi falls on Monday, March 8, 2027, and press reports of the airport operator's 2026 announcement describe regular flights halting for 24 hours. Avoid that date for any flight touching Bali, and confirm with the airline.


## At the airport

Domestic and international flights at Bali's airport use different terminals, so allow time to get between them if your plans involve both. Our advice is to carry your passport for a domestic flight, as your identity document at check-in, and to keep it in your hand luggage.


## Check in online

Our advice is to check in online where the airline offers it and to arrive at the airport with margin. The drive to the airport is the unpredictable part: it is about 22 miles from Ubud and 12 from Canggu, and drive times vary widely by hour.



## FAQ

How much baggage is allowed on Indonesian domestic flights?It depends on the airline's service group: up to 20 kg on full-service airlines, up to 15 kg on medium-service airlines, and no-frills airlines may charge. Check your fare.

Will my bags be checked through to my domestic flight?Not on separate bookings. Plan to collect and re-check them, and ask the airlines.

Can I fly back to Bali and leave for the US the same day?Our advice is no. Return to Bali at least one full day before your international flight.

Do I need my passport for a domestic flight in Indonesia?Carry it. It is your identity document for check-in.



===== fast-boats-to-nusa-penida-and-lembongan [boats-and-ferries / boat-day-buffer] 1090w =====
Fast Boats to Nusa Penida and Lembongan: Ports, Operators and Sea Conditions
Fast boats to Nusa Penida and Nusa Lembongan leave mainly from Sanur, with a crossing that travel guides put at roughly 30 to 45 minutes. The route crosses open water in the Badung Strait, and sea conditions decide whether a crossing is comfortable or wise. We name no operators. This page explains the ports, what to expect on board, and what the harbor authority has said about weather.


## Where the boats leave from

Sanur, on Bali's southeast coast, is the main port for the Nusa islands. Travel guides also mention boats from Serangan and from Padang Bai in East Bali, where a slower vehicle ferry runs to Nusa Penida. Sanur's harbor is about 11 miles from the airport by road, about 12 from Seminyak and about 13 from Ubud.


## Where they arrive

On Nusa Penida, boats arrive at harbors on the north coast, which travel guides name as Toyapakeh, Buyuk and Sampalan. On Nusa Lembongan, they arrive at Jungutbatu beach in the north or at Mushroom Bay in the southwest. Check which landing your boat uses before you book a place to stay.


## The crossing

Travel guides give the crossing from Sanur as roughly 30 minutes to Nusa Penida and 35 to 45 minutes to Lembongan. Those are their figures, not an official timetable. We have no official schedule, so check times directly with an operator, and reconfirm the day before.

The crossing, and where it leaves from: Sanur · Nusa Lembongan · Nusa Penida. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## What boarding is like

Boarding differs by island. Travel guides note that most boats to Lembongan involve a wet landing, with passengers wading through water that can be knee-deep or higher. Wear shorts and sandals, keep your phone and passport in a dry bag, and bring bags you can manage on sand.


## On board

Travel guides describe the fast boats as small high-speed craft, and the ride can be bumpy. Our advice is to sit toward the back, where the motion is less, to take seasickness medication beforehand if you need it, and to find out where the life jackets are before the boat leaves.


## Luggage

Travel light for an island leg. Many travelers leave their large suitcases at a hotel on the mainland and take a small bag across. That makes a wet landing simple and keeps your things dry. Ask your mainland hotel whether it stores luggage for returning guests.



## Sea conditions and the harbor authority

The Sanur to Nusa Penida route crosses the Badung Strait, and it comes under the harbor master's office known as KSOP Benoa. Indonesia's state news agency reports that this office issues the sailing approval each boat needs before departure, and that it has told operators not to send boats out when the weather is poor.


## What the forecasts say

The same agency reported that in May 2023 Indonesia's meteorological agency forecast waves of 2.5 to 4 meters in the Badung Strait, and that waves above 2.5 meters put boats on this route at risk. Wave forecasts for Bali's waters are published by the meteorological agency, and an operator should be able to tell you what the day's forecast is.


## The 2025 accident

The risk is not theoretical. The state news agency reported that on 5 August 2025 a fast boat arriving from Nusa Penida with 75 passengers capsized in the entrance channel to Sanur harbor after being hit by a large wave, and that two passengers died. The harbor master attributed it to natural conditions and said the boat was within its certified capacity.


## Choosing a boat

The operator is the choice a passenger controls. We name and rank none, because we have no official source that compares them. Travel guides advise travelers who have concerns about the vessel, the operator, overloading or the weather not to board, and that advice costs nothing to follow.


## What to look for

These are our own suggestions.


- Book directly or through your accommodation, and get a named boat and time.

- Take a morning crossing, in daylight.

- Check that there is a life jacket for every passenger.

- Count the seats and the people; walk away from an overloaded boat.

- Ask what happens to your ticket if the crossing is cancelled.



## When to say no

If the sea looks rough from the beach, if the boat is crowded beyond its seats, or if departure is being pushed late into the afternoon, our advice is to wait for another boat or another day. A lost ticket is a small cost.


## Season

We have no official source on which months are roughest on this crossing, so we name none as fact. Travel guides describe sea conditions between Bali and Lombok as worst in July and August, when swells are strongest, and the 2025 accident happened in August. The dry season is not the same thing as a calm sea.


## Check on the day

Conditions change from day to day. Our advice is to ask your accommodation or the operator about the forecast on the evening before, and to keep your plans loose enough to move the crossing by a day if the answer is poor.


## Practical points

A few practical matters apply to every crossing. Carry cash for the islands. Bring any medicine you rely on. Check how your travel insurance treats boat travel. The State Department strongly recommends insurance covering medical evacuation in Indonesia, and care on a small island begins with a boat back.


## The day trip

A day trip to Nusa Penida means two crossings in one day, with the return in the afternoon. Book a return that lands in Sanur well before dark, and do not plan anything fixed for that evening.


## Where the crossing sits in your trip

Because the boat depends on the sea, where it falls in the itinerary matters as much as which boat you take. How to place an island leg so that a delayed crossing costs you little is set out in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

Where do boats to Nusa Penida leave from?Mainly from Sanur. Travel guides also mention Serangan and a slower ferry from Padang Bai.

How long is the fast boat to Nusa Lembongan?Travel guides give roughly 35 to 45 minutes from the south of Bali. Check times with the operator.

Are fast boats to Nusa Penida safe?Conditions decide. The harbor authority has told operators not to sail in poor weather, and a boat capsized at Sanur in August 2025. Do not board if you have doubts.

Will I get wet boarding the boat to Lembongan?Probably. Travel guides note that most boats involve a wet landing, wading through knee-deep water or higher.



===== when-bali-fast-boats-cancel [boats-and-ferries / boat-day-buffer] 1213w =====
When Bali's Fast Boats Cancel: Your Options and the Buffer You Need
When a fast boat from the Nusa or Gili islands is cancelled, your options are to wait for the next sailing, to switch to another operator or port, or, from Lombok, to fly. None of them is quick. The protection is a buffer: keep at least one full day, and better two, between your boat back to Bali and your flight home. Boats on these routes depend on the sea.


## Why boats are cancelled

Fast boats around Bali are small craft crossing open water, and weather is the documented reason for stopping them. Indonesia's state news agency reports that the harbor master's office for the Sanur route issues a sailing approval for each departure and has told operators not to send boats out when conditions are poor, pointing them to the meteorological agency's wave forecasts.


## What counts as poor conditions

The state news agency reported in May 2023 that waves of 2.5 to 4 meters were forecast in the Badung Strait, which the Sanur to Nusa Penida route crosses, and that waves above 2.5 meters put boats on this route at risk. A cancellation in those conditions is the system working as intended.


## Why you want them to cancel

The same agency reported that in August 2025 a fast boat arriving from Nusa Penida capsized in the entrance to Sanur harbor after being hit by a large wave, and that two passengers died. Forecast waves that day were 2 to 4 meters. A cancelled boat is an inconvenience; the alternative is worse.

The water the crossing has to cross: Sanur · Nusa Lembongan · Nusa Penida. (Enable JavaScript for the interactive map.)


## How often it happens

We have no official source on how often boats to the Nusa or Gili islands are cancelled, and we will not invent a rate. Travel guides describe sea conditions between Bali and Lombok as worst in July and August, when swells are strongest.


## The dry season is not the calm season

A first-time visitor tends to assume that the dry season means good boating weather. Rain and swell are separate things. South Bali's driest months, July and August, are the months travel guides single out for the roughest crossings, and the 2025 accident at Sanur happened in August.


## Plan for more than a few hours

Our caution is that sea conditions do not always clear overnight, which is why we do not count a single afternoon as a buffer. Our advice is to plan as though a cancellation could cost you a full day, and to be pleased when it does not.



## Your options when a boat is cancelled

A cancelled boat leaves three practical choices, and which is open to you depends on where you are. From the Nusa islands, the only way back is by sea. From the Gili Islands, the sea route to Bali can be replaced by a short boat to Lombok, a drive and a flight.


## Wait

The first option is to wait for the next sailing that the harbor authority allows. Ask the operator when that is likely, and ask your accommodation whether you can keep the room. Waiting costs time and possibly an extra night's accommodation, which is why the buffer matters: with spare days in hand, waiting is the simplest choice.


## Switch boat or port

The second option is another operator or another route. We have no official source on how operators handle each other's passengers, so we describe no practice. Travel guides mention a slower vehicle ferry between Nusa Penida and Padang Bai; whether it is running is a question for the day.


## Fly, from Lombok

From the Gili Islands, the alternative is to cross the short stretch of water to Lombok, drive to Lombok's airport and fly to Bali. Travel guides put the drive at well over an hour and a half. It is a separate domestic booking with its own baggage rules, and seats on the day are not guaranteed.


## What happens to your ticket

We have no official source on refund or rebooking rules for cancelled boats, and operators set their own terms. Ask before you buy what happens if the crossing does not run: whether the ticket moves to the next sailing, whether you can have the money back, and how to reach the operator on the day.


## Insurance

Check what your travel insurance says about delays and missed connections caused by cancelled boats. Policies differ a great deal on this point, and some exclude delays on separately booked transport. The State Department highly recommends travel insurance for Indonesia. Ask the question in writing before you travel, and keep the ticket and any cancellation message.


## The buffer you need

The buffer is the time between your return boat and anything you cannot miss. Our advice is at least one full day between the boat back to Bali and an international flight, and two days in July and August or in the wet season. That is our own margin, not an official rule.


## Why one day and not a few hours

A boat landing at Sanur at midday and a flight that evening looks comfortable on paper. It assumes the morning boat ran. If it did not, the next one may be the following morning, and the drive from Sanur to the airport, about 11 miles, is the least of your problems.


## How to lower the odds

A traveler cannot control the sea and can still improve the odds. These are our own suggestions: take a morning boat, since a cancelled morning sailing leaves the afternoon as a fallback; check the forecast with the operator the evening before; and avoid boarding any boat you have doubts about.


## Do not pressure the operator

The harbor authority's message to operators, as reported, was not to force a departure in poor weather. Passengers with flights to catch are part of that pressure. If the boat is not going, accept it. Travel guides give the same advice from the other side: if you have concerns about the vessel, overloading or the weather, do not board.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. The options after a cancellation were all poor, and the real protection was decided weeks earlier, when the island nights were placed in the itinerary. An island leg at the end of a trip turns every swell into a threat to the flight home. The same leg in the middle turns a cancellation into an extra day at the beach.

How to place the island leg, the buffer and the departure for your own dates is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How often are fast boats cancelled in Bali?We have no official source for a rate. Travel guides describe July and August as the roughest months between Bali and Lombok.

What should I do if my fast boat is cancelled?Wait for the next permitted sailing, ask about another boat or port, or from the Gili Islands go to Lombok and fly. Keep the ticket and receipts.

How much buffer do I need before my flight?Our advice is at least one full day between the boat back to Bali and an international flight, and two in July, August or the wet season.

Will I get a refund if the boat is cancelled?Operators set their own terms and we have no official source on them. Ask before you buy.



===== bali-to-gili-fast-boat-vs-flight [boats-and-ferries / boat-day-buffer] 1162w =====
Bali to the Gili Islands: Fast Boat vs. Flying via Lombok
From Bali to the Gili Islands, the fast boat is the direct route and the flight via Lombok avoids the long open-sea crossing. A fast boat from Padang Bai crosses in roughly an hour and a half, according to travel guides, and depends on the sea. Flying means a short flight to Lombok, a drive of well over an hour and a half, and a short boat. Each takes most of a day door to door.


## The two routes

The Gili Islands lie off the northwest coast of Lombok, the island east of Bali, and a traveler has two ways to reach them. One is by sea the whole way, on a fast boat from Bali. The other is by air to Lombok's international airport, then by road to the harbor area at Bangsal, then by a short boat.


## Why neither is fast

The crossing or the flight is the short part of either route. The rest is getting to a port or an airport, waiting, and getting from the far end to your hotel. We give no total door-to-door times, since they depend on your base and the day. Plan on the journey taking most of a day either way.


## The fast boat

Fast boats run from several points on Bali. Travel guides name Padang Bai, in East Bali, as the main port, with a crossing of roughly an hour and a half. Boats from the south of the island take longer, around two and a half hours by the same guides, and Amed, on the northeast coast, has the shortest sea route.


## Getting to the port

Padang Bai is about 35 miles from the airport by road, about 32 from Seminyak and about 23 from Ubud. From the southern resorts, the drive to the port is a journey in itself and usually starts early. Many operators sell a ticket that includes a hotel pickup; confirm the pickup time and place in writing.


## What the crossing is like

Travel guides describe the boats as small high-speed craft, and the route crosses the open strait between Bali and Lombok. Travel guides describe the sea as roughest in July and August, when swells are strongest. Anyone prone to seasickness should prepare for it.


## What travel guides warn about

Travel guides are direct on safety. They note that crew training and safety standards vary considerably between operators, and they advise travelers who have concerns about the vessel, the operator, overloading or the weather not to board. They also advise against crossing at night. We name no operators.



## Flying via Lombok

The air route replaces the open-sea crossing with a short flight and a drive. You fly from Bali to Lombok's international airport, drive across the island to the harbor area at Bangsal or Teluk Nare, and take a short boat to the islands. Travel guides put the drive at about an hour and forty minutes to two hours.


## What it involves

This route has more stages: a drive to Bali's airport, a check-in, the flight, baggage reclaim, the drive, and the boat. Each stage is ordinary, and together they fill the day. The final boat is a much shorter crossing than the one between Bali and Lombok.


## Baggage on the flight

A domestic flight has its own baggage rules. According to Indonesian press reporting on the Transport Ministry's regulation, a full-service airline includes up to 20 kilograms of checked baggage, a medium-service airline up to 15, and a no-frills airline may charge for it. Check the fare before buying, and weigh your bags.


## How to choose

The choice between boat and flight comes down to how you feel about small boats, the month you are traveling and where on Bali you start. These are our own suggestions, and they are a starting point, not a rule.


- Choose the boat if you are starting from East Bali or Ubud, the sea is calm and you travel light.

- Choose the flight in July and August, in the wet season, or if you get seasick.

- Choose the flight if you are uneasy about small fast boats for any reason.

- Choose the flight if you want to see Lombok as well.



## From where you start

Your base on Bali tips the balance. From Amed or Sidemen, the port is close and the boat is the natural choice. From Uluwatu or Canggu, the drive to Padang Bai is long, and the airport is nearer, which makes the flight more competitive than it first appears.


## One way each

The two routes can be combined. Some travelers take the boat out, when a delay costs only beach time, and fly back, when a delay would matter more. That gives one sea crossing for the experience and a return that does not depend on the strait.


## The return is the leg that matters

Whichever route you choose, the return to Bali is the leg with consequences. We have no official source on how often boats are cancelled, and a domestic flight can be delayed too. Our advice is to be back on Bali at least one full day before an international flight, and two days in July and August.


## Never same-day

A boat or a domestic flight from Lombok on the same day as your flight to the United States is a gamble on separate tickets. The US Department of Transportation's advice for any connection is to ask what happens if the first leg is delayed. Here the risk is missing the flight home.


## Practical points for either route

A few points apply whichever way you travel. There are no cars or motorbikes on the Gili Islands, so pack bags you can carry across sand. Bring cash and any medicine you rely on. The State Department says an injured traveler in Indonesia will likely need to reach a major city for care.


## What this decision actually sits inside

Notice what happened across all of this. Boat or flight looked like a question of comfort and price, and the answer kept depending on other things: which side of Bali you sleep on the night before, which month it is, and how many days stand between the return leg and the flight home. The transport choice is the last piece to set, after the nights are placed.

How to place a Gili leg within a Bali trip, and what to put on either side of it, is worked through in the complete Bali trip guide.



## FAQ

How long is the fast boat from Bali to the Gili Islands?Travel guides give roughly an hour and a half from Padang Bai and longer from the south of Bali. Check times with the operator.

Is it better to fly or take the boat to Gili?The boat is more direct; the flight via Lombok avoids the open-sea crossing. Our advice is to fly in July, August, the wet season, or if you get seasick.

Can you fly directly to the Gili Islands?No. You fly to Lombok's airport, drive to the harbor area at Bangsal, and take a short boat.

How far is Padang Bai from Ubud?About 23 miles by road.
